```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove the rational-boundary log abundance conjecture in every dimension over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero: every nef ℚ-Cartier log canonical divisor on a projective log canonical pair with effective rational boundary is semiample. Over ℂ, the proof also establishes canonical nonvanishing for smooth projective varieties in every dimension.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the rational-boundary log abundance conjecture in every dimension over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero: every nef ℚ-Cartier log canonical divisor on a projective log canonical pair with effective rational boundary is semiample. Over ℂ, the proof also establishes canonical nonvanishing for smooth projective varieties in every dimension.

- **claim-log-abundance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $(X,B)$ be a normal projective log canonical pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Assume that $B$ is an effective rational divisor and $K_X+B$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. If $K_X+B$ is nef, then some positive Cartier multiple of it is generated by global sections.
  - Explanation: A nef rational log canonical adjoint is semiample on the original projective variety.
- **claim-logarithmic-subadditivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-asm-iitaka)): Let $f:X\to Y$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers between smooth connected projective complex varieties. Let $D_X,D_Y$ be reduced effective simple normal crossing divisors, allowing zero boundaries, such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}(f^*D_Y)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}(D_X).$$ For a very general smooth fiber $F$, put $D_F=D_X\|_F$. Then $$\kappa(X,K_X+D_X)\ge \kappa(F,K_F+D_F)+\kappa(Y,K_Y+D_Y).$$ Here $D_F$ is reduced with simple normal crossings and $K_F+D_F\sim(K_X+D_X)\|_F$. The convention $(-\infty)+b=-\infty$ applies also when $b=-\infty$, and the zero divisor on a point has Iitaka dimension zero.
  - Explanation: Logarithmic Iitaka dimension is bounded below by the sum of the fiber and base dimensions under the stated boundary support condition.
- **claim-nef-model-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-mmp-comparison)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be log canonical and let $(X',\Delta')$ be a log minimal model. On a common resolution $$X \xleftarrow{u} W \xrightarrow{v} X'$$ there is an effective $v$-exceptional divisor $F$ such that $$\label{mmp:comparison-equation} u^*(K_X+\Delta)=v^*(K_{X'}+\Delta')+F.$$ If $K_X+\Delta$ is nef, then $F=0$. Consequently a nef rational adjoint is semiample whenever it has a good log minimal model. If $(X,\Delta)$ is klt, its log minimal model is klt and extracts no divisors.
  - Explanation: The effective exceptional comparison disappears when the source adjoint is nef, allowing semiampleness to descend. A klt source also prevents extraction.
- **claim-whole-boundary-semiampleness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-boundary-restriction)): Assume Assumption <a href="#mmp:lower-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mmp:lower-models">3</a>. Let $(V,D)$ be a projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial dlt $n$-fold with $D$ reduced and $M=K_V+D$ nef. Then $M\|_D$ is semiample as a rational line bundle on the reduced scheme $D$.
  - Explanation: Lower-dimensional good models and normalization gluing provide semiampleness on the whole reduced boundary.
- **claim-uniform-trivial-perturbation** (bound, [source](#stmt-mmp-trivial-perturbation)): Let $(Z,\Delta)$ be a projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial dlt rational pair of dimension $n$, and suppose $L=K_Z+\Delta$ is nef. Set $P=\Delta$ if the pair is klt and $P=\lfloor\Delta\rfloor$ otherwise. Fix $m>0$ such that $mL$ is Cartier. For every rational $$0<\epsilon<\frac{1}{4nm+2},$$ every step of an LMMP for $L-\epsilon P$ is $L$-trivial. On all its models, the transform of $L$ is nef and its multiple by the same integer $m$ is Cartier.
  - Explanation: The permitted perturbation makes every driving step trivial for the original nef adjoint and preserves its fixed Cartier index.
- **claim-inductive-good-model-reduction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-inductive-reduction)): Assume Assumption <a href="#mmp:lower-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mmp:lower-models">3</a>. Assume in addition that every smooth projective $n$-fold with pseudo-effective canonical divisor has nonnegative Kodaira dimension. Then every projective lc $n$-fold with real boundary and pseudo-effective adjoint has a good log minimal model. In particular, every nef rational lc adjoint in dimension $n$ is semiample.
  - Explanation: Smooth canonical nonvanishing in the current dimension upgrades the lower-dimensional good-model hypothesis to good models in that dimension.
- **claim-signed-boundary-abundance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-signed)): Let $(X,D)$ be a projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial dlt pair over $\mathbb{C}$, with $D=\sum_iD_i$ reduced. Suppose that $$L=K_X+D\quad\text{is nef},\qquad L-cD\quad\text{is pseudo-effective} \quad\text{for some }c>0,$$ and that $$L\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}G=\sum_i a_iD_i,\qquad a_i\in\mathbb{Q}.$$ If $L\|_D$ is semiample as a rational line bundle on the reduced scheme $D$, then $L$ is abundant: $$\kappa(X,L)=\nu(X,L).$$
  - Explanation: A signed representative supported on the reduced boundary yields abundance when the small boundary subtraction is pseudo-effective and the whole boundary restriction is semiample.
- **claim-positive-boundary-image** (bound, [source](#stmt-sg-numerical-boundary)): If $v=n$, then $L$ is big. If $v=0$, then $D=0$ and $L\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}0$. In the remaining case $0<v<n$, put $r=v-1$. Every component of $D$ has $\phi$-image of dimension at most $r$, and some component of $G_+$ has image of dimension $r$. Moreover, $$\dim\phi\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}G_+\cap(\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}G_-\cup D_0)\bigr)<r;$$ when $r=0$, the intersection in this formula is empty.
  - Explanation: Intersection inequalities locate a positive boundary component of maximal image dimension and force its intersections with the remaining boundary into smaller images.
- **claim-signed-root-adjunction-construction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-signed-construction)): Choose $\ell$ divisible by $m$ and every nonzero integer $\|ma_i\|$, and set $a=\ell/m$. There is a normal tame Deligne–Mumford stack $\mathcal X$, considered on a neighborhood of a reduced Cartier divisor $S$ of pure dimension $N=n-1$, together with a morphism to $X$ and a reduced boundary $T$ having no component in common with $S$, with the following properties.* 1. *The pair $(\mathcal X,S+T)$ is log canonical, and a fixed isomorphism of reflexive sheaves is given by $$\omega_{\mathcal X}(S+T)\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal X}(aS). \tag{\(\ast_{\mathrm{adj}}\)}\label{sg:ambient-adjunction}$$ The support of $T$ is locally set-theoretically principal. The ambient space and $S$ are Cohen–Macaulay on scheme charts. Off $T$, the ambient space is Gorenstein and the displayed isomorphism is ordinary Cartier adjunction data.* 2. *Put $J=(S\cap T)_{\mathrm{red}}$. Both $S$ and $J$ are Du Bois on scheme charts, the ideal $\mathcal I_{J/S}$ is maximal Cohen–Macaulay, and $$\mathcal B:= \mathcal Hom_S(\mathcal I_{J/S},\omega_S) \simeq\mathcal{O}_S(aS).$$ The identification agrees off $J$ with the residue convention specified by <a href="#sg:ambient-adjunction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sg:ambient-adjunction">[sg:ambient-adjunction]</a>.* 3. *There is a finite representable morphism $$S\longrightarrow D\times_P\mathcal P$$ whose image covers $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}G_+$. The line $\mathcal{O}_S(S)$ is the pullback of $C$. The image of $J$ in $P$ has dimension less than $r$, whereas the image of $S$ has dimension $r$.* 4. *There is a smooth projective bundle $p:\mathcal Y\to\mathcal P$ and a representable projective morphism $g:S\to\mathcal Y$. This is a morphism from the reduced divisor $S$; no extension to a neighborhood of $S$ in $\mathcal X$ is asserted here. Writing $h=p\circ g$ and $\mathcal T=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}_{\mathcal P}h_*\mathcal{O}_S$, the morphism $g$ factors through a closed embedding $\mathcal T\hookrightarrow\mathcal Y$, and $$g_*\mathcal{O}_S=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal T},\qquad \mathcal{O}_S(S)=g^*C,\qquad \mathcal B=g^*C^a.$$* 5. *For a separated quasi-projective scheme chart $U\to\mathcal Y$ that is etale and of finite type, put $S_U=S\times_{\mathcal Y}U$. The etale morphism $S_U\to S$ extends compatibly to every nilpotent thickening $qS$ in $\mathcal X$. Only this etale chart is extended; an extension of $g$ to $qS$ is not needed to define the chart. The resulting $qS_U$ are quasi-projective schemes, separated and quasi-finite over $X$.* *All the adjunction and duality identifications are compatible with changes of etale chart.
  - Explanation: Root covers, separation of opposite signs and a quotient retaining the normal line produce compatible adjunction data and ordinary scheme charts for lifting.
- **claim-compatible-formal-transitions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-formal-lifting)): For every such chart $U$, every $j\geq0$, and every $k\geq1$, the transition $$g_*(I^j/I^{j+k+1}) \longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^{j+k})$$ is surjective as a map of sheaves of abelian groups. Its kernel is $\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal T_U}\otimes C^{-j-k}$, where $\mathcal T_U=\mathcal T\times_{\mathcal Y}U$. The statements are compatible with further etale changes of charts. On an affine chart, these transitions are also surjective on global sections.
  - Explanation: All adjacent infinitesimal transitions are surjective as sheaf maps of abelian groups, with coherent kernels and compatible affine global lifts.
- **claim-lowest-hodge-obstruction-piece** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hg-lowest-piece)): The following identifications hold compatibly on the charts: $$F_{<0}A_{0,U}=0,\qquad F_0A_{0,U}=\mathcal B\|_{S_U}, \qquad F_{<0}M_U=0,\qquad F_0M_U=R^1g_*(\mathcal B\|_{S_U}).$$ In a smooth ambient embedding $S_U\hookrightarrow V$ of codimension $c$, the underlying module of $A_{0,U}$ is $\mathcal H^c_{S_U}(\omega_V)$, localized off $J_U$. The lowest-piece inclusion is the adjunction, or Ext-to-support, inclusion off $J_U$, extended by meromorphic localization.
  - Explanation: Duality and projective strictness identify the coherent obstruction sheaf with the actual lowest filtered inclusion into the differential module.
- **claim-negative-twist-hypercohomology** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hg-negative-vanishing)): Let $M$ be a compatible system of mixed Hodge modules in perverse degree zero on the scheme etale charts of $\mathcal Y$. Suppose its support is finite over $\mathcal P$. Then, for every integer $k$, every positive integer $b$, and every $j<0$, $$\mathbb H^j\!\left( \mathcal Y,\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}^F_k\mathop{\mathrm{DR}}_{\mathcal Y}(M)\otimes p^*C^{-b} \right)=0.$$ Here the graded de Rham complex is the descended coherent complex.
  - Explanation: Finite support over the root gerbe allows negative-twist vanishing to descend from ordinary projective-space Hodge-module vanishing.
- **claim-dominating-compact-null-family** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-null-family)): Assume the transition surjectivity of Proposition <a href="#prop:formal-lifting" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:formal-lifting">11</a>. There is a dominating algebraic family of integral projective subvarieties of $X$ of dimension $$d=n-1-r=n-v$$ whose general members avoid $D$ and on which $L$ is numerically trivial.
  - Explanation: Compatible formal lifts algebraize to projective boundary-avoiding members, and a countability argument selects a dominating family.
- **claim-vertical-nef-divisor-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sg-vertical-descent)): Let $f:Y\to B$ be a surjective projective morphism from a normal integral variety to a smooth projective variety, with geometrically connected integral generic fiber. Suppose all fibers have dimension at most $\dim Y-\dim B$. Let $G_Y$ be a vertical $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor that is relatively nef. Then $$G_Y=f^*G_B$$ for a $\mathbb{Q}$-divisor $G_B$ on $B$.
  - Explanation: The fiber-dimension bound makes vertical primes divisorial over the base; relative nefness eliminates the residual divisor after subtracting a pullback.
- **claim-counterexample-irregularity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ex-irregularity)): Every smooth projective birational model of $X$ has irregularity zero. Consequently, on such a model, or on a projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial terminal birational model, numerical equivalence of rational divisors implies their $\mathbb{Q}$-linear equivalence.
  - Explanation: A hypothetical nonvanishing counterexample has no irregularity, so numerical equivalence of rational divisors becomes rational-linear equivalence on the specified models.
- **claim-algebraic-web-quotient** (construction, [source](#stmt-ex-web)): Let a smooth projective variety be dominated generically finitely by the total space of a family with smooth integral projective general fibers. On a dense regular open, form the distribution generated by the tangent spaces of the fiber images, taking spans, saturation, and Lie brackets. Its general leaves are dense opens of algebraic subvarieties. Their closures are the general fibers of a rational map, which has connected general fiber after Stein factorization.
  - Explanation: The saturated bracket-closed distribution generated by the family has algebraic general leaf closures and hence a rational quotient.
- **claim-counterexample-moving-general-type** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-general-type)): Every positive-dimensional proper subvariety through a very general point of $X$ is of general type on resolution. The assertion holds also on every projective birational model.
  - Explanation: A covering family of nongeneral-type proper subvarieties would produce canonical nonvanishing, contradicting the assumed counterexample.
- **claim-supporting-functional-positivity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ex-supporting)): Let $Y$ be a projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial terminal birational model of $X$. If a rational divisor $P$ has $\kappa(Y,P)\geq1$, then $K_Y+cP$ is big for every rational $c>0$. Consequently, if $\alpha\neq0$ is a supporting functional of the pseudo-effective cone with $\alpha(K_Y)=0$, then $\alpha(P)>0$.
  - Explanation: A positive-Iitaka divisor moves the canonical class into the big cone, forcing strict positivity under a supporting functional.
- **claim-fixed-current-valuation-acc** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ex-valuation-acc)): For a fixed $T$ and a fixed $M$, the set $$\{\nu_E(T): A_V(E)\leq M\}$$ satisfies the ascending chain condition.
  - Explanation: For a fixed current and discrepancy ceiling, divisorial Lelong numbers cannot form a strictly increasing infinite sequence.
- **claim-no-canonical-current-pullback** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-no-pullback)): Let $Y$ be a fixed projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial terminal birational model of $X$, with $K_Y$ nonbig. A positive current in the pullback class of $K_Y$ on a smooth resolution cannot, on a dense regular Zariski open, annihilate the tangent spaces of the general fibers of a rational fibration of positive relative dimension.
  - Explanation: A canonical current annihilating positive-dimensional fibration directions would descend to a smaller quotient and ultimately force nonvanishing.
- **claim-exceptional-current-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-ex-current-comparison)): Fix a positive current $T$ on a smooth resolution in the pullback class of $K_Y$. On a common smooth resolution of $Y\dashrightarrow Y_t$, write $$p^*K_Y=p_t^*K_t+J_t,\qquad J_t\geq0,$$ where $J_t$ is exceptional over $Y_t$. Then $\nu_E(T)\geq\operatorname{coeff}_E J_t$ for every prime on that resolution.
  - Explanation: Multiplier approximation and exceptional divisor comparison bound each canonical correction coefficient by the corresponding Lelong number.
- **claim-no-normalized-bounded-covering-curves** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-bounded-curves)): There are no sequences $t_j\downarrow0$ and covering families of curves on $Y_{t_j}$, with smooth projective domains $C_j$, for which both $$g(C_j),\qquad \frac{(K_{t_j}+t_jA_{t_j})\cdot C_j}{\mu_{t_j}}$$ are bounded by a fixed constant. Degrees mean degrees on the domains, or equivalently intersection with their pushforward cycles.
  - Explanation: Bounded genus and normalized adjoint degree force an exact zero current gap, producing the forbidden rational fibration.
- **claim-floor-special-termination** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-special-termination)): Assume Assumption <a href="#mmp:lower-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mmp:lower-models">3</a>. Let $(V,\Delta)$ be a projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial dlt $n$-fold with rational boundary and pseudo-effective adjoint. Choose an effective ample rational divisor $A$ such that $(V,\Delta+A)$ is dlt and $K_V+\Delta+A$ is nef. The LMMP for $K_V+\Delta$ with scaling of $A$ has special termination: if the program is infinite, its flipping loci are eventually disjoint from the round-down of the boundary.* *In particular, if at every stage the adjoint is rationally linearly equivalent to a signed divisor supported on that round-down, the program terminates at a log minimal model.
  - Explanation: Lower-dimensional programs on floor components yield special termination. A signed floor-supported adjoint then leaves no possible late negative flips.
- **claim-signed-canonical-log-bigness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-signed-alternative)): On any model $Y_t$, let a signed rational divisor represent $K_t$ up to $\mathbb{Q}$-linear equivalence. On a log resolution $p:W\to Y_t$, let $D$ be the reduced SNC divisor consisting of its strict support and all exceptional divisors. Then $K_W+D$ is big.
  - Explanation: The reduced support of a signed canonical representative gives a big logarithmic adjoint on a resolution of a counterexample model.
- **claim-fixed-cover-birational-obstruction** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ex-fixed-cover)): Let $Y$ be a projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial terminal birational model of the counterexample $X$ in Equation <a href="#ex:counterexample" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ex:counterexample">[ex:counterexample]</a>, and let $T_i\to Y$, $i=1,2$, be fixed finite surjective morphisms from normal integral projective varieties. There is no integral parameter variety $A_0$ and closed subvariety $$\Gamma\subset A_0\times T_1\times T_2$$ flat over $A_0$, with each fiber the integral graph of a birational map $T_1\dashrightarrow T_2$, for which the projection $\Gamma\to T_1\times T_2$ is dominant.
  - Explanation: Dominant graph evaluation would make a fixed cover birational to an abelian variety, whose ramification and canonical current force nonvanishing downstairs.
- **claim-numerical-generic-subadjunction** (bound, [source](#stmt-common-numerical-subadjunction)): Let $Z$ be a projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety over $\mathbb{C}$, let $\Theta\geq0$ be a rational divisor, and let $V\subset Z$ be an integral positive-dimensional subvariety. Suppose that $(Z,\Theta)$ is lc at the generic point of $V$ and that a divisor of log discrepancy zero has center $V$. Let $f=\dim V$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor on $V$, and let $\rho:V^*\to V$ be a smooth projective resolution, factoring through the normalization. Then $$\label{common:subadjunction-inequality} K_{V^*}\cdot(\rho^*P)^{f-1} \leq (K_Z+\Theta)\|_V\cdot P^{f-1}.$$ The right side is the intersection on $V$ of the restricted rational Cartier class with $P^{f-1}$. No $\mathbb{Q}$-Gorenstein hypothesis on the normalization of $V$ is required.
  - Explanation: Generic log canonicity and an lc place yield a canonical-intersection inequality on a resolution of the center, without requiring its normalization to be rational Gorenstein.
- **claim-high-multiplicity-base-component** (bound, [source](#stmt-common-base-component)): Let $Z$ be a projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety of dimension $d$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor, and let $k>0$ be an integer for which $kP$ is Cartier. Let $0\ne\mathcal H\subset H^0(Z,\mathcal{O}_Z(kP))$ be a linear subspace with proper base locus and base ideal $\mathfrak b$. Suppose that $V$ is an integral base-locus component of dimension $f<d$, isolated at its generic point $\eta_V$, and that $\eta_V\in Z_{\rm sm}$. Write $a=d-f$ and $\mathfrak m=\mathfrak m_{\mathcal{O}_{Z,\eta_V}}$. If $$\mathfrak b\mathcal{O}_{Z,\eta_V}\subset\mathfrak m^h \qquad(h\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}),$$ then $$\label{common:base-component-degree} P^f\cdot V\leq (k/h)^aP^d.$$ There are a rational number $c$ with $0<c\leq ak/h$ and an effective rational divisor $\Theta\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}cP$ such that $(Z,\Theta)$ is lc at $\eta_V$ and has an lc place with center $V$. If $f>0$, on a smooth resolution $V^*$ one consequently has $$\label{common:base-component-canonical} K_{V^*}\cdot P^{f-1} \leq (K_Z+cP)\|_V\cdot P^{f-1}.$$ The degree estimate includes $f=0$; the canonical estimate is asserted only for positive-dimensional $V$.
  - Explanation: Ordinary local multiplicity bounds the degree of an isolated base component. A realized ideal threshold supplies the canonical-intersection bound for positive-dimensional centers.
- **claim-full-moving-jet-kernel** (bound, [source](#stmt-common-moving-kernel)): Let $Z$ be an integral projective variety of dimension $d$, and let $P$ be a nef, big, semiample rational Cartier divisor. Fix an integer $k$ with $kP$ Cartier and positive real numbers $$\tau_0<\tau_1<\cdots<\tau_d, \qquad\tau_{i+1}-\tau_i=\delta>0.$$ For a smooth point $x$, let $\mathcal H_i(x)$ be the entire subspace of sections of $kP$ vanishing at $x$ to order at least $\lceil k\tau_i\rceil$. Suppose that for very general $x$ all these subspaces are nonzero and the base locus of $\mathcal H_0(x)$ has a positive-dimensional component through $x$. If $k\delta\geq4$, then, after an algebraic parameter extension and restriction to nonempty opens, there are an irreducible parameter space $T$, a fixed adjacent pair of levels, and a family of integral subvarieties $V_t$ common to the two base loci such that the incidence $$\Gamma=\{(t,z):z\in V_t\}\subset T\times Z$$ dominates $Z$. Each general $V_t$ has dimension in $[1,d-1]$, is an isolated component of the higher base locus at its generic point, and its entire higher-series base ideal is contained there in $$\label{common:moving-multiplicity-order} \mathcal I_{V_t}^{h},\qquad h=\lceil k\delta/2\rceil, \qquad k/h\leq2/\delta.$$ These are ordinary local ideal powers in the smooth ambient open.* *The incidence base ideal lies in $\mathcal I_\Gamma^h$ on a dense open of $\Gamma$. If, in addition, $Z$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial and klt, then for a general member, with $f=\dim V_t$, one has $$\label{common:tracking-bounds} \begin{split} P^f\cdot V_t&\le(2/\delta)^{d-f}P^d,\\ K_{V_t^*}P^{f-1}&\le(K_Z+cP)\|_{V_t}P^{f-1}, \qquad 0<c\le2(d-f)/\delta . \end{split}$$ In particular the weaker bound $c\le2d/\delta$ also holds. Positivity of $P^f\cdot V_t$ follows if $V_t$ meets the open where the big semiample contraction is an isomorphism. No such positivity is asserted for every exceptional subvariety.
  - Explanation: Differentiating the full moving kernels produces ordinary high-order vanishing on a dominant incidence and permits degree and subadjunction estimates.
- **claim-isolated-ideal-fiber-restriction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-common-fiber-restriction)): In the setting of Lemma <a href="#common:moving-kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:moving-kernel">29</a>, let $g:Z\to B$ be a morphism. There is a dense open of the incidence on which the following holds. Choose $(t,z)$ in that open, let $b=g(z)$, and suppose that $V_t$ is smooth over the smooth open of its actual image at $z$. Let $F$ be the reduced component through $z$ of its scheme fiber. Locally on $Z_b$ at $z$, restriction of the higher kernel’s base ideal has support exactly $F$ and is contained in $\mathcal I_F^h$. If $F$ is proper in an integral ambient fiber component containing it, the restricted series is nonzero there and $F$ is an isolated base component at its generic point. Application of Lemma <a href="#common:base-component" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:base-component">28</a> on that ambient component requires all its hypotheses, including projectivity, $\mathbb{Q}$-factoriality, klt singularities, and smoothness at the generic point of $F$, separately; they are not conclusions of this restriction lemma.
  - Explanation: At a suitable general incidence point, restriction preserves the support and ordinary-power containment of the isolated base ideal. Global singularity and positivity assumptions must still be checked separately.
- **claim-finite-covers-fixed-branch-complement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-common-finite-covers)): Let $Y$ be a normal projective complex variety. Let $\mathcal V\to T$ be a smooth projective family with integral fibers, over an integral parameter variety, and let $g:\mathcal V\to Y$ be a morphism whose general fiber maps $g_t:V_t\to Y$ are dominant and generically finite of degree at most a fixed integer $e$. After a finite parameter extension and shrinking, spread the geometric generic components of the zero divisor of the relative Jacobian over $Y_{\rm sm}$ to divisors $\mathcal R_1,\ldots,\mathcal R_N$ in $\mathcal V$. Retain the notation $\mathcal V,T,g$ after this base change. Let $I$ be the set of indices $j$ for which the image closure $\overline{g_t((\mathcal R_j)_t)}$ is a divisor in $Y$ for general $t$. Assume $$\overline{g(\mathcal R_j)}\ne Y\qquad(j\in I).$$ Then, after shrinking again, one smooth nonempty open $Y^\circ\subset Y$ makes every general finite normal Stein cover of $g_t$ finite étale over $Y^\circ$. There are only finitely many such covers of $Y$, up to isomorphism over $Y$. The same conclusion holds simultaneously for finitely many projections. The open and finite lists may depend on all fixed parameters of the family.
  - Explanation: Bounded degree together with nonsweeping divisorial branch images gives a fixed smooth branch complement and only finitely many finite normal covers.
- **claim-scalar-vanishing-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-scalar-jets)): For sufficiently small $t$, let $P$ be the positive part of $2r(K+2tA)$, and put $\rho=(P^n)^{1/n}$. At a very general point, every nonzero section of every Cartier multiple $kP$ has order at most $4k\rho$. The same statement holds on a common resolution after adding an effective exceptional divisor that does not change the section spaces. Moreover $$2r\mu_t\le\rho\le4r\mu_t,$$ so in particular $\rho\le8\epsilon$ for small $t$.
  - Explanation: Excessive scalar vanishing would create a moving general-type component and forbidden normalized covering curves.
- **claim-bundle-models-volumes-and-weights** (construction, [source](#stmt-jet-bundle-models)): Both the total class $M$ and the slice class have big semiample positive parts $P$ on $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt models $Z$, with $$K_Z+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}c_tP,\qquad \Delta\ge0,$$ where $c_t$ is bounded independently of small $t$. Their volumes satisfy, respectively, $$\begin{aligned} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(M)&=\frac{(2n+1)!}{(n!)^2} \int_0^q\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(L_b)\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(L_{q-b})\,db,\label{jet:total-volume}\\ \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(L+q\xi)&=(n+1)\int_0^q\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(L_b)\,db.\label{jet:slice-volume} \end{aligned}$$ In particular both volumes are bounded above and below by positive constants times $q$. For each rational weight, on common resolutions, $$\label{jet:weight-domination} P\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}N_{b,1}+N_{q-b,2}+E_b,\qquad E_b\ge0,$$ with the analogous one-term formula on slices. At a generic point of a base divisor of a slice, and on a general torus fiber, the full system has no fixed subtraction.
  - Explanation: Big klt adjoint models provide the positive parts, while the toric weight decomposition gives volume integrals and effective comparisons with axis models.
- **claim-large-two-slot-local-positivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-large-seshadri)): For each fixed sufficiently small $\epsilon>0$, there is an integer $q>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small rational $t>0$ and $r$ as in Equation <a href="#jet:normalization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="jet:normalization">[jet:normalization]</a>, the positive part $P$ of Equation <a href="#jet:two-slot" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="jet:two-slot">[jet:two-slot]</a> has $$\varepsilon(P;x)>2n+2$$ at a very general smooth point of its torus open. In particular, for some sufficiently divisible integer $k>0$, the complete system $\|kP\|$ separates jets of order strictly greater than $(2n+2)k$ at such a point. The section spaces and these general-point jets agree on the original bundle and on common birational resolutions.
  - Explanation: Slice estimates eliminate moving base components except correspondences; branch finiteness and the fixed-cover obstruction eliminate those as well.
- **claim-finite-data-frobenius-incompatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-common-finite-data-frobenius)): Let $W$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $n\ge2$. Let $L,H$ be rational Cartier divisors, with $H$ ample, let $S$ be an integral Cartier divisor, and fix an integer $q>0$ and real numbers $r>1$, $\epsilon>0$. Suppose $$\label{common:intersection-bounds} \begin{gathered} H^n\le(2\epsilon)^n,\qquad K_WH^{n-1}\le2H^n/r,\qquad (2L+qS)H^{n-1}\le4H^n,\\ (14\epsilon)^n<\tfrac14,\qquad 80\epsilon<\tfrac34. \end{gathered}$$ Assume that the following fixed data exist.* 1. *A smooth integral complete-intersection flag from $W$ to a curve $C$, whose successive divisor classes are $h_i=l_iH$ for fixed positive integers $l_i$, with $\mu_{\min}(\Omega_W^1\|_C)\ge0$.* 2. *On the projective bundle $$Z=\mathbf P\bigl(\mathcal O_W(S)_1\oplus \mathcal O_W(S)_2\bigr)\longrightarrow W\times W, \qquad \xi=c_1(\mathcal O_Z(1)),\qquad M=L_1+L_2+q\xi,$$ use the convention that the direct image of $\mathcal O_Z(a\xi)$ is the $a$-th symmetric power of the displayed sum. A smooth point $z_*$ in the complement of the two axes and finitely many sections of $k_0M$, for some integer $k_0>0$ with $k_0L$ Cartier, generate jets through order $(2n+2)k_0$ at $z_*$.* 3. *A point $x\in W$, its blowup $\pi_x:\widehat W\to W$ with exceptional divisor $J$, and a curve class $$\gamma=f_*(A_1\cdots A_{n-1}),$$ where $f:V\to\widehat W$ is one fixed birational morphism, $V$ is smooth integral projective, and the $A_i$ are ample integral Cartier divisors, such that $J\cdot\gamma>0$ and $$\label{common:movable-input} \pi_x^*(L+K_W/2+\lambda S)\cdot\gamma \le40\epsilon J\cdot\gamma\qquad(0\le\lambda\le q).$$ It suffices to check the two endpoints in this affine inequality.* *These data cannot coexist.
  - Explanation: Fixed jets force a full-rank evaluation determinant, while the small polarization and movable witness impose incompatible diagonal vanishing orders.
- **claim-spread-frobenius-jet-generation** (construction, [source](#stmt-common-positive-characteristic-jets)): For a sufficiently large residual characteristic $p$, set $$N_p=\lfloor p/k_0\rfloor,\qquad B_p=k_0N_pL+T_0.$$ The sections of $pq\xi+B_{p,1}+B_{p,2}$ generate jets through order $(2n+2)k_0N_p$ at $z_*$. In particular, they surject onto the quotient of its local ring by the $p$-th powers of all regular parameters.
  - Explanation: Products of the fixed jet sections and finitely many filler sections generate the required ordinary jets and hence the Frobenius local quotient.
- **claim-two-slot-generic-full-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-common-generic-rank)): The map in Equation <a href="#common:two-slot-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:two-slot-evaluation">[common:two-slot-evaluation]</a> has generic rank $R$.
  - Explanation: Projection to the constant torus basis coefficient extracts the divisible weights, whose values span the full product of the base Frobenius fibers.
- **claim-uniform-truncated-slope-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-common-truncated-slope)): There is a constant $c\ge0$, independent of the sufficiently large residual characteristic $p$ and of $j$, such that for $V=\Omega_W^1\|_C$ one has $$\mu_{\max}(A_j(V))\le(p-1)K_W\cdot C+nc.$$
  - Explanation: A minimum-slope estimate, tensor quotient comparison and complementary socle pairing bound every truncated symmetric piece uniformly.
- **claim-diagonal-section-deficit** (bound, [source](#stmt-common-diagonal-sections)): For all sufficiently large $p$, $$h^0(\Delta_F,\mathcal L_p) \le R\bigl(7^nH^n+O(1/p)\bigr)<R/4.$$ The error constant is independent of the filtration index $j$.
  - Explanation: The same flag polynomial bounds each graded piece by its rank. Summing ranks yields the diagonal section deficit without an additional filtration-length factor.
- **claim-inductive-smooth-nonvanishing** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-smooth-nonvanishing)): Assume the lower-dimensional real-boundary good-model hypothesis of Assumption <a href="#mmp:lower-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mmp:lower-models">3</a>. If $X$ is a smooth projective complex variety of dimension $n$ and $K_X$ is pseudo-effective, then $\kappa(X,K_X)\geq0$.
  - Explanation: The counterexample geometry supplies all fixed witnesses of the Frobenius incompatibility theorem, so a pseudo-effective smooth canonical divisor has nonnegative Iitaka dimension.
- **claim-characteristic-zero-field-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-field-descent)): The conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> over $\mathbb{C}$ implies its conclusion over every algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.
  - Explanation: The evaluation map of an actual Cartier line bundle descends by faithful flatness after the pair and its nefness are transferred to a complex base change.
- **claim-real-boundary-complex-good-models** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-good-models-complex)): Every projective log canonical pair $(X,B)$ over $\mathbb{C}$, with effective real boundary and pseudo-effective real Cartier adjoint $K_X+B$, has a good log minimal model. Real semiampleness has the meaning fixed in Section <a href="#sec:induction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:induction">2</a>.
  - Explanation: The simultaneous dimension induction establishes good log minimal models for pseudo-effective real-boundary lc adjoints over the complex numbers.
- **claim-smooth-canonical-good-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-smooth-good-model)): Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex variety with pseudo-effective $K_X$. There is a normal projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety $V$ and a $K_X$-negative birational contraction $X\dashrightarrow V$ such that $K_V$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier and semiample. On a common smooth resolution $p:W\to X$, $q:W\to V$, compatible canonical divisors satisfy $$p^*K_X=q^*K_V+E, \qquad E\geq0\quad\text{and }E\text{ is }q\text{-exceptional}.$$ In particular $\kappa(X,K_X)=\kappa(V,K_V)$.
  - Explanation: The smooth zero-boundary case has a klt good model with an effective exceptional comparison that preserves all pluricanonical section spaces.
- **claim-semiample-zero-iitaka-triviality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-semiample-zero)): Let $Z$ be a nonempty integral projective variety over an algebraically closed field, and let $A$ be a semiample $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor. If $\kappa(Z,A)=0$, then $A\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}0$.
  - Explanation: A generated multiple defines a morphism with point image, so a section nonzero at that image trivializes the line bundle.
- **claim-nef-adjoint-zero-iitaka-triviality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-qlinear-zero)): Let $(X,B)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. If $\kappa(X,K_X+B)=0$, then $K_X+B\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}0$. In particular, every nef rational adjoint of Iitaka dimension zero on a projective klt fivefold over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero is $\mathbb{Q}$-linearly trivial.
  - Explanation: Main abundance followed by the semiample zero-Iitaka lemma gives rational-linear triviality, including the stated klt specialization.
- **claim-semi-log-canonical-abundance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-slc-abundance)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a projective semi-log-canonical pair over $\mathbb{C}$, where $\Delta$ is an effective rational divisor and $K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. If $K_X+\Delta$ is nef, then it is semiample.
  - Explanation: Normal abundance applies componentwise to the normalization, and conductor gluing descends semiampleness to the nonnormal pair.
- **claim-supported-dlt-section-extension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-dlt-extension)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a projective dlt pair over $\mathbb{C}$, where $\Delta$ is an effective rational divisor, and put $S=\lfloor\Delta\rfloor$. Assume that $K_X+\Delta$ is nef and that, for an effective rational divisor $D$, $$K_X+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}D\geq0, \qquad S\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}D.$$ Then, for every integer $m\geq2$ for which $m(K_X+\Delta)$ is Cartier, the restriction map $$H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal{O}_X(m(K_X+\Delta))\bigr) \longrightarrow H^0\bigl(S,\mathcal{O}_X(m(K_X+\Delta))\|_S\bigr)$$ is surjective.
  - Explanation: Semiampleness and the retained support condition permit the cited extension theorem for the specified Cartier multiples.
- **claim-rounded-log-ring-finite-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-lc-finite-generation)): 1. *Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, let $(X,\Delta)$ be a normal projective log canonical pair over $k$, and assume that $\Delta\geq0$ is rational and $D=K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. Then $$R(X,D)=\bigoplus_{m\geq0} H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal{O}_X(\lfloor mD\rfloor)\bigr)$$ is a finitely generated $k$-algebra.* 2. *Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a log canonical pair over $\mathbb{C}$, with $\Delta\geq0$ rational and $K_X+\Delta$ $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. For every proper morphism $f:X\to S$ onto an algebraic variety $S$, the relative log canonical algebra $$\mathcal R(X/S,K_X+\Delta) =\bigoplus_{m\geq0}f_*\mathcal{O}_X\bigl(\lfloor m(K_X+\Delta)\rfloor\bigr)$$ is a finitely generated $\mathcal{O}_S$-algebra.
  - Explanation: The absolute rounded ring is preserved by the nef-model comparison and decomposes into finitely many residue-class section modules. The proper complex relative statement follows from good-model reductions.
- **claim-uniruledness-and-tensor-criterion** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-uniruled-mumford)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety.* 1. *$X$ is uniruled if and only if $\kappa(X)=-\infty$.* 2. *$X$ is rationally connected if and only if $$H^0\bigl(X,(\Omega_X^1)^{\otimes m}\bigr)=0 \quad\text{for every integer }m>0.$$
  - Explanation: Canonical nonvanishing identifies non-uniruledness with nonnegative Kodaira dimension and supplies the tensor nonvanishing needed for Mumford's rational-connectedness criterion.
- **claim-pluriform-rational-quotient** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-pluriforms)): 1. *For every smooth connected projective complex variety $X$, $$\dim R(X)= \max_{\substack{p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq0},\,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\\ H^0(X,\operatorname{Sym}^k(\Omega_X^p))\ne0}} p.$$* 2. *A compact connected Kähler manifold $X$ is rationally connected if and only if $$H^0\bigl(X,\operatorname{Sym}^k(\Omega_X^p)\bigr)=0 \quad\text{for every pair of integers }p,k>0.$$
  - Explanation: The largest exterior degree supporting a symmetric pluriform equals the MRC base dimension. Complete positive-degree pluriform vanishing also characterizes rational connectedness in the compact Kähler setting.
- **claim-cotangent-invariants-mrc** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-cotangent-mrc)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety. Then $$\omega(X)=\omega(R(X))= \begin{cases} -\infty,&\text{if }R(X)\text{ is a point},\\ \kappa(R(X)),&\text{if }\dim R(X)>0. \end{cases}$$ In particular, if $X$ is positive-dimensional and non-uniruled, then $\omega(X)=\kappa(X)$. If $X$ is positive-dimensional and $\kappa(X)\geq0$, then $$\kappa^+(X)=\kappa(X).$$
  - Explanation: Cotangent Iitaka invariants descend to the MRC base and equal its Kodaira dimension when that base is positive-dimensional.
- **claim-zero-kodaira-finite-fundamental-group** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-finite-pi-one)): Let $X$ be a positive-dimensional smooth connected projective complex variety. If $\kappa(X)=0$ and $\chi(X,\mathcal{O}_X)\ne0$, then its ordinary topological fundamental group $\pi_1(X)$ is finite.
  - Explanation: Equality of cotangent and canonical invariants allows the cited finiteness criterion to apply when the holomorphic Euler characteristic is nonzero.
- **claim-quasi-projective-cover-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-quasi-projective-covers)): Let $X$ be a connected normal projective complex variety.* 1. *Its universal cover is biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety if and only if there is a finite étale Galois cover $X'\to X$ and a locally trivial holomorphic fiber bundle $X'\to A$, where $A$ is an abelian variety and the fiber is simply connected and projective.* 2. *If $\widetilde X\to X$ is an infinite étale Galois cover and $\widetilde X$ is biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety, there are a finite étale Galois cover $X'\to X$, a locally trivial holomorphic fiber bundle $\alpha:X'\to A$ over an abelian variety, and an étale cover $\widetilde A\to A$ with no positive-dimensional compact analytic subvarieties, such that $$\widetilde X\simeq X'\times_A\widetilde A$$ as covering spaces over $X$.
  - Explanation: Smooth abundance removes the abundance premise from the cited classification of quasi-projective analytic covers through holomorphic bundles over abelian varieties.
- **claim-relative-analytic-compact-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-analytic-abundance)): Let $\pi:X\to Y$ be a projective surjective morphism of normal complex analytic varieties. Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a log canonical pair with $\Delta$ an effective rational divisor and $D=K_X+\Delta$ $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. Assume that $D$ is $\pi$-nef over all of $Y$. For every compact subset $W\subset Y$, there exist an analytic open neighborhood $U\supset W$ and a positive integer $m$ such that $mD\|_{X_U}$ is Cartier, where $X_U=\pi^{-1}(U)$, and the line bundle $$L=\mathcal{O}_{X_U}(mD\|_{X_U})$$ is generated relative to $\pi_U:X_U\to U$. Equivalently, the evaluation map $$\pi_U^*(\pi_U)_*L\longrightarrow L$$ is surjective.
  - Explanation: Algebraic abundance on general stratum fibers gives relative log abundance, and local generated multiples combine over each compact subset of the analytic base.

```json
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mmp-trivial-perturbation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(Z,\\Delta)$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial dlt rational pair of dimension $n$, and suppose $L=K_Z+\\Delta$ is nef. Set $P=\\Delta$ if the pair is klt and $P=\\lfloor\\Delta\\rfloor$ otherwise. Fix $m>0$ such that $mL$ is Cartier. For every rational $$0<\\epsilon<\\frac{1}{4nm+2},$$ every step of an LMMP for $L-\\epsilon P$ is $L$-trivial. On all its models, the transform of $L$ is nef and its multiple by the same integer $m$ is Cartier.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-inductive-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Assume Assumption <a href=\"#mmp:lower-models\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mmp:lower-models\">3</a>. Assume in addition that every smooth projective $n$-fold with pseudo-effective canonical divisor has nonnegative Kodaira dimension. Then every projective lc $n$-fold with real boundary and pseudo-effective adjoint has a good log minimal model. In particular, every nef rational lc adjoint in dimension $n$ is semiample.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-signed",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,D)$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial dlt pair over $\\mathbb{C}$, with $D=\\sum_iD_i$ reduced. Suppose that $$L=K_X+D\\quad\\text{is nef},\\qquad L-cD\\quad\\text{is pseudo-effective}\n \\quad\\text{for some }c>0,$$ and that $$L\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}G=\\sum_i a_iD_i,\\qquad a_i\\in\\mathbb{Q}.$$ If $L|_D$ is semiample as a rational line bundle on the reduced scheme $D$, then $L$ is abundant: $$\\kappa(X,L)=\\nu(X,L).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sg-numerical-boundary",
        "exact_quote": "If $v=n$, then $L$ is big. If $v=0$, then $D=0$ and $L\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}0$. In the remaining case $0<v<n$, put $r=v-1$. Every component of $D$ has $\\phi$-image of dimension at most $r$, and some component of $G_+$ has image of dimension $r$. Moreover, $$\\dim\\phi\\bigl(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}G_+\\cap(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}G_-\\cup D_0)\\bigr)<r;$$ when $r=0$, the intersection in this formula is empty.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-signed-construction",
        "exact_quote": "Choose $\\ell$ divisible by $m$ and every nonzero integer $|ma_i|$, and set $a=\\ell/m$. There is a normal tame Deligne–Mumford stack $\\mathcal X$, considered on a neighborhood of a reduced Cartier divisor $S$ of pure dimension $N=n-1$, together with a morphism to $X$ and a reduced boundary $T$ having no component in common with $S$, with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *The pair $(\\mathcal X,S+T)$ is log canonical, and a fixed isomorphism of reflexive sheaves is given by $$\\omega_{\\mathcal X}(S+T)\\simeq\\mathcal{O}_{\\mathcal X}(aS).\n     \\tag{\\(\\ast_{\\mathrm{adj}}\\)}\\label{sg:ambient-adjunction}$$ The support of $T$ is locally set-theoretically principal. The ambient space and $S$ are Cohen–Macaulay on scheme charts. Off $T$, the ambient space is Gorenstein and the displayed isomorphism is ordinary Cartier adjunction data.*\n\n2.  *Put $J=(S\\cap T)_{\\mathrm{red}}$. Both $S$ and $J$ are Du Bois on scheme charts, the ideal $\\mathcal I_{J/S}$ is maximal Cohen–Macaulay, and $$\\mathcal B:=\n     \\mathcal Hom_S(\\mathcal I_{J/S},\\omega_S)\n     \\simeq\\mathcal{O}_S(aS).$$ The identification agrees off $J$ with the residue convention specified by <a href=\"#sg:ambient-adjunction\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sg:ambient-adjunction\">[sg:ambient-adjunction]</a>.*\n\n3.  *There is a finite representable morphism $$S\\longrightarrow D\\times_P\\mathcal P$$ whose image covers $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}G_+$. The line $\\mathcal{O}_S(S)$ is the pullback of $C$. The image of $J$ in $P$ has dimension less than $r$, whereas the image of $S$ has dimension $r$.*\n\n4.  *There is a smooth projective bundle $p:\\mathcal Y\\to\\mathcal P$ and a representable projective morphism $g:S\\to\\mathcal Y$. This is a morphism from the reduced divisor $S$; no extension to a neighborhood of $S$ in $\\mathcal X$ is asserted here. Writing $h=p\\circ g$ and $\\mathcal T=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}_{\\mathcal P}h_*\\mathcal{O}_S$, the morphism $g$ factors through a closed embedding $\\mathcal T\\hookrightarrow\\mathcal Y$, and $$g_*\\mathcal{O}_S=\\mathcal{O}_{\\mathcal T},\\qquad\n     \\mathcal{O}_S(S)=g^*C,\\qquad \\mathcal B=g^*C^a.$$*\n\n5.  *For a separated quasi-projective scheme chart $U\\to\\mathcal Y$ that is etale and of finite type, put $S_U=S\\times_{\\mathcal Y}U$. The etale morphism $S_U\\to S$ extends compatibly to every nilpotent thickening $qS$ in $\\mathcal X$. Only this etale chart is extended; an extension of $g$ to $qS$ is not needed to define the chart. The resulting $qS_U$ are quasi-projective schemes, separated and quasi-finite over $X$.*\n\n*All the adjunction and duality identifications are compatible with changes of etale chart.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-signed-construction",
          "exact_quote": "Choose $\\ell$ divisible by $m$ and every nonzero integer $|ma_i|$, and set $a=\\ell/m$. The ambient space and $S$ are Cohen–Macaulay on scheme charts. Off $T$, the ambient space is Gorenstein and the displayed isomorphism is ordinary Cartier adjunction data.*\n\n2.  *Put $J=(S\\cap T)_{\\mathrm{red}}$. This is a morphism from the reduced divisor $S$; no extension to a neighborhood of $S$ in $\\mathcal X$ is asserted here. Writing $h=p\\circ g$ and $\\mathcal T=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}_{\\mathcal P}h_*\\mathcal{O}_S$, the morphism $g$ factors through a closed embedding $\\mathcal T\\hookrightarrow\\mathcal Y$, and $$g_*\\mathcal{O}_S=\\mathcal{O}_{\\mathcal T},\\qquad\n     \\mathcal{O}_S(S)=g^*C,\\qquad \\mathcal B=g^*C^a.$$*\n\n5.  *For a separated quasi-projective scheme chart $U\\to\\mathcal Y$ that is etale and of finite type, put $S_U=S\\times_{\\mathcal Y}U$. Only this etale chart is extended; an extension of $g$ to $qS$ is not needed to define the chart. Only this etale chart is extended; an extension of $g$ to $qS$ is not needed to define the chart. The resulting $qS_U$ are quasi-projective schemes, separated and quasi-finite over $X$.*\n\n*All the adjunction and duality identifications are compatible with changes of etale chart.",
      "explanation": "Root covers, separation of opposite signs and a quotient retaining the normal line produce compatible adjunction data and ordinary scheme charts for lifting.",

      "hypotheses": [

      "quantifiers": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-signed-construction",
          "exact_quote": "There is a normal tame Deligne–Mumford stack $\\mathcal X$, considered on a neighborhood of a reduced Cartier divisor $S$ of pure dimension $N=n-1$, together with a morphism to $X$ and a reduced boundary $T$ having no component in common with $S$, with the following properties.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"

        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-formal-lifting",
        "exact_quote": "For every such chart $U$, every $j\\geq0$, and every $k\\geq1$, the transition $$g_*(I^j/I^{j+k+1})\n       \\longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^{j+k})$$ is surjective as a map of sheaves of abelian groups. Its kernel is $\\mathcal{O}_{\\mathcal T_U}\\otimes C^{-j-k}$, where $\\mathcal T_U=\\mathcal T\\times_{\\mathcal Y}U$. The statements are compatible with further etale changes of charts. On an affine chart, these transitions are also surjective on global sections.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-formal-lifting",
          "exact_quote": "For every such chart $U$, every $j\\geq0$, and every $k\\geq1$, the transition $$g_*(I^j/I^{j+k+1})\n       \\longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^{j+k})$$ is surjective as a map of sheaves of abelian groups. Its kernel is $\\mathcal{O}_{\\mathcal T_U}\\otimes C^{-j-k}$, where $\\mathcal T_U=\\mathcal T\\times_{\\mathcal Y}U$. The statements are compatible with further etale changes of charts. On an affine chart, these transitions are also surjective on global sections.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every such chart $U$, every $j\\geq0$, and every $k\\geq1$, the transition $$g_*(I^j/I^{j+k+1})\n       \\longrightarrow g_*(I^j/I^{j+k})$$ is surjective as a map of sheaves of abelian groups. Its kernel is $\\mathcal{O}_{\\mathcal T_U}\\otimes C^{-j-k}$, where $\\mathcal T_U=\\mathcal T\\times_{\\mathcal Y}U$. The statements are compatible with further etale changes of charts.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-special-termination",
        "exact_quote": "Assume Assumption <a href=\"#mmp:lower-models\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mmp:lower-models\">3</a>. Let $(V,\\Delta)$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial dlt $n$-fold with rational boundary and pseudo-effective adjoint. Choose an effective ample rational divisor $A$ such that $(V,\\Delta+A)$ is dlt and $K_V+\\Delta+A$ is nef. The LMMP for $K_V+\\Delta$ with scaling of $A$ has special termination: if the program is infinite, its flipping loci are eventually disjoint from the round-down of the boundary.*\n\n*In particular, if at every stage the adjoint is rationally linearly equivalent to a signed divisor supported on that round-down, the program terminates at a log minimal model.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-common-numerical-subadjunction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety over $\\mathbb{C}$, let $\\Theta\\geq0$ be a rational divisor, and let $V\\subset Z$ be an integral positive-dimensional subvariety. Suppose that $(Z,\\Theta)$ is lc at the generic point of $V$ and that a divisor of log discrepancy zero has center $V$. Let $f=\\dim V$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor on $V$, and let $\\rho:V^*\\to V$ be a smooth projective resolution, factoring through the normalization. Then $$\\label{common:subadjunction-inequality}\n K_{V^*}\\cdot(\\rho^*P)^{f-1}\n \\leq (K_Z+\\Theta)|_V\\cdot P^{f-1}.$$ The right side is the intersection on $V$ of the restricted rational Cartier class with $P^{f-1}$. No $\\mathbb{Q}$-Gorenstein hypothesis on the normalization of $V$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-common-numerical-subadjunction",

Suppose that $(Z,\\Theta)$ is lc at the generic point of $V$ and that a divisor of log discrepancy zero has center $V$. Let $f=\\dim V$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor on $V$, and let $\\rho:V^*\\to V$ be a smooth projective resolution, factoring through the normalization. Then $$\\label{common:subadjunction-inequality}\n K_{V^*}\\cdot(\\rho^*P)^{f-1}\n \\leq (K_Z+\\Theta)|_V\\cdot P^{f-1}.$$ The right side is the intersection on $V$ of the restricted rational Cartier class with $P^{f-1}$. No $\\mathbb{Q}$-Gorenstein hypothesis on the normalization of $V$ is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety over $\\mathbb{C}$, let $\\Theta\\geq0$ be a rational divisor, and let $V\\subset Z$ be an integral positive-dimensional subvariety.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-common-base-component",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety of dimension $d$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor, and let $k>0$ be an integer for which $kP$ is Cartier. Let $0\\ne\\mathcal H\\subset H^0(Z,\\mathcal{O}_Z(kP))$ be a linear subspace with proper base locus and base ideal $\\mathfrak b$. Suppose that $V$ is an integral base-locus component of dimension $f<d$, isolated at its generic point $\\eta_V$, and that $\\eta_V\\in Z_{\\rm sm}$. Write $a=d-f$ and $\\mathfrak m=\\mathfrak m_{\\mathcal{O}_{Z,\\eta_V}}$. If $$\\mathfrak b\\mathcal{O}_{Z,\\eta_V}\\subset\\mathfrak m^h\n \\qquad(h\\in\\mathbb{Z}_{>0}),$$ then $$\\label{common:base-component-degree}\n P^f\\cdot V\\leq (k/h)^aP^d.$$ There are a rational number $c$ with $0<c\\leq ak/h$ and an effective rational divisor $\\Theta\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}cP$ such that $(Z,\\Theta)$ is lc at $\\eta_V$ and has an lc place with center $V$. If $f>0$, on a smooth resolution $V^*$ one consequently has $$\\label{common:base-component-canonical}\n K_{V^*}\\cdot P^{f-1}\n \\leq (K_Z+cP)|_V\\cdot P^{f-1}.$$ The degree estimate includes $f=0$; the canonical estimate is asserted only for positive-dimensional $V$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-common-base-component",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety of dimension $d$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor, and let $k>0$ be an integer for which $kP$ is Cartier. Suppose that $V$ is an integral base-locus component of dimension $f<d$, isolated at its generic point $\\eta_V$, and that $\\eta_V\\in Z_{\\rm sm}$. Write $a=d-f$ and $\\mathfrak m=\\mathfrak m_{\\mathcal{O}_{Z,\\eta_V}}$. If $$\\mathfrak b\\mathcal{O}_{Z,\\eta_V}\\subset\\mathfrak m^h\n \\qquad(h\\in\\mathbb{Z}_{>0}),$$ then $$\\label{common:base-component-degree}\n P^f\\cdot V\\leq (k/h)^aP^d.$$ There are a rational number $c$ with $0<c\\leq ak/h$ and an effective rational divisor $\\Theta\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}cP$ such that $(Z,\\Theta)$ is lc at $\\eta_V$ and has an lc place with center $V$. If $f>0$, on a smooth resolution $V^*$ one consequently has $$\\label{common:base-component-canonical}\n K_{V^*}\\cdot P^{f-1}\n \\leq (K_Z+cP)|_V\\cdot P^{f-1}.$$ The degree estimate includes $f=0$; the canonical estimate is asserted only for positive-dimensional $V$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety of dimension $d$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor, and let $k>0$ be an integer for which $kP$ is Cartier.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-common-moving-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be an integral projective variety of dimension $d$, and let $P$ be a nef, big, semiample rational Cartier divisor. Fix an integer $k$ with $kP$ Cartier and positive real numbers $$\\tau_0<\\tau_1<\\cdots<\\tau_d,\n \\qquad\\tau_{i+1}-\\tau_i=\\delta>0.$$ For a smooth point $x$, let $\\mathcal H_i(x)$ be the entire subspace of sections of $kP$ vanishing at $x$ to order at least $\\lceil k\\tau_i\\rceil$. Suppose that for very general $x$ all these subspaces are nonzero and the base locus of $\\mathcal H_0(x)$ has a positive-dimensional component through $x$. If $k\\delta\\geq4$, then, after an algebraic parameter extension and restriction to nonempty opens, there are an irreducible parameter space $T$, a fixed adjacent pair of levels, and a family of integral subvarieties $V_t$ common to the two base loci such that the incidence $$\\Gamma=\\{(t,z):z\\in V_t\\}\\subset T\\times Z$$ dominates $Z$. Each general $V_t$ has dimension in $[1,d-1]$, is an isolated component of the higher base locus at its generic point, and its entire higher-series base ideal is contained there in $$\\label{common:moving-multiplicity-order}\n \\mathcal I_{V_t}^{h},\\qquad h=\\lceil k\\delta/2\\rceil,\n \\qquad k/h\\leq2/\\delta.$$ These are ordinary local ideal powers in the smooth ambient open.*\n\n*The incidence base ideal lies in $\\mathcal I_\\Gamma^h$ on a dense open of $\\Gamma$. If, in addition, $Z$ is $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial and klt, then for a general member, with $f=\\dim V_t$, one has $$\\label{common:tracking-bounds}\n \\begin{split}\n P^f\\cdot V_t&\\le(2/\\delta)^{d-f}P^d,\\\\\n K_{V_t^*}P^{f-1}&\\le(K_Z+cP)|_{V_t}P^{f-1},\n \\qquad 0<c\\le2(d-f)/\\delta .\n \\end{split}$$ In particular the weaker bound $c\\le2d/\\delta$ also holds. Positivity of $P^f\\cdot V_t$ follows if $V_t$ meets the open where the big semiample contraction is an isomorphism. No such positivity is asserted for every exceptional subvariety.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-common-moving-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be an integral projective variety of dimension $d$, and let $P$ be a nef, big, semiample rational Cartier divisor. Fix an integer $k$ with $kP$ Cartier and positive real numbers $$\\tau_0<\\tau_1<\\cdots<\\tau_d,\n \\qquad\\tau_{i+1}-\\tau_i=\\delta>0.$$ For a smooth point $x$, let $\\mathcal H_i(x)$ be the entire subspace of sections of $kP$ vanishing at $x$ to order at least $\\lceil k\\tau_i\\rceil$. Suppose that for very general $x$ all these subspaces are nonzero and the base locus of $\\mathcal H_0(x)$ has a positive-dimensional component through $x$. If $k\\delta\\geq4$, then, after an algebraic parameter extension and restriction to nonempty opens, there are an irreducible parameter space $T$, a fixed adjacent pair of levels, and a family of integral subvarieties $V_t$ common to the two base loci such that the incidence $$\\Gamma=\\{(t,z):z\\in V_t\\}\\subset T\\times Z$$ dominates $Z$. Each general $V_t$ has dimension in $[1,d-1]$, is an isolated component of the higher base locus at its generic point, and its entire higher-series base ideal is contained there in $$\\label{common:moving-multiplicity-order}\n \\mathcal I_{V_t}^{h},\\qquad h=\\lceil k\\delta/2\\rceil,\n \\qquad k/h\\leq2/\\delta.$$ These are ordinary local ideal powers in the smooth ambient open.*\n\n*The incidence base ideal lies in $\\mathcal I_\\Gamma^h$ on a dense open of $\\Gamma$. If, in addition, $Z$ is $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial and klt, then for a general member, with $f=\\dim V_t$, one has $$\\label{common:tracking-bounds}\n \\begin{split}\n P^f\\cdot V_t&\\le(2/\\delta)^{d-f}P^d,\\\\\n K_{V_t^*}P^{f-1}&\\le(K_Z+cP)|_{V_t}P^{f-1},\n \\qquad 0<c\\le2(d-f)/\\delta .\n \\end{split}$$ In particular the weaker bound $c\\le2d/\\delta$ also holds. Positivity of $P^f\\cdot V_t$ follows if $V_t$ meets the open where the big semiample contraction is an isomorphism. No such positivity is asserted for every exceptional subvariety.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $Z$ be an integral projective variety of dimension $d$, and let $P$ be a nef, big, semiample rational Cartier divisor. Fix an integer $k$ with $kP$ Cartier and positive real numbers $$\\tau_0<\\tau_1<\\cdots<\\tau_d,\n \\qquad\\tau_{i+1}-\\tau_i=\\delta>0.$$ For a smooth point $x$, let $\\mathcal H_i(x)$ be the entire subspace of sections of $kP$ vanishing at $x$ to order at least $\\lceil k\\tau_i\\rceil$. Suppose that for very general $x$ all these subspaces are nonzero and the base locus of $\\mathcal H_0(x)$ has a positive-dimensional component through $x$. If $k\\delta\\geq4$, then, after an algebraic parameter extension and restriction to nonempty opens, there are an irreducible parameter space $T$, a fixed adjacent pair of levels, and a family of integral subvarieties $V_t$ common to the two base loci such that the incidence $$\\Gamma=\\{(t,z):z\\in V_t\\}\\subset T\\times Z$$ dominates $Z$. Each general $V_t$ has dimension in $[1,d-1]$, is an isolated component of the higher base locus at its generic point, and its entire higher-series base ideal is contained there in $$\\label{common:moving-multiplicity-order}\n \\mathcal I_{V_t}^{h},\\qquad h=\\lceil k\\delta/2\\rceil,\n \\qquad k/h\\leq2/\\delta.$$ These are ordinary local ideal powers in the smooth ambient open.*\n\n*The incidence base ideal lies in $\\mathcal I_\\Gamma^h$ on a dense open of $\\Gamma$. If, in addition, $Z$ is $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial and klt, then for a general member, with $f=\\dim V_t$, one has $$\\label{common:tracking-bounds}\n \\begin{split}\n P^f\\cdot V_t&\\le(2/\\delta)^{d-f}P^d,\\\\\n K_{V_t^*}P^{f-1}&\\le(K_Z+cP)|_{V_t}P^{f-1},\n \\qquad 0<c\\le2(d-f)/\\delta .\n \\end{split}$$ In particular the weaker bound $c\\le2d/\\delta$ also holds. Positivity of $P^f\\cdot V_t$ follows if $V_t$ meets the open where the big semiample contraction is an isomorphism. No such positivity is asserted for every exceptional subvariety.",

        "anchor": "stmt-common-fiber-restriction",
        "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#common:moving-kernel\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"common:moving-kernel\">29</a>, let $g:Z\\to B$ be a morphism. There is a dense open of the incidence on which the following holds. Choose $(t,z)$ in that open, let $b=g(z)$, and suppose that $V_t$ is smooth over the smooth open of its actual image at $z$. Let $F$ be the reduced component through $z$ of its scheme fiber. Locally on $Z_b$ at $z$, restriction of the higher kernel’s base ideal has support exactly $F$ and is contained in $\\mathcal I_F^h$. If $F$ is proper in an integral ambient fiber component containing it, the restricted series is nonzero there and $F$ is an isolated base component at its generic point. Application of Lemma <a href=\"#common:base-component\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"common:base-component\">28</a> on that ambient component requires all its hypotheses, including projectivity, $\\mathbb{Q}$-factoriality, klt singularities, and smoothness at the generic point of $F$, separately; they are not conclusions of this restriction lemma.",

        "anchor": "stmt-common-finite-covers",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a normal projective complex variety. Let $\\mathcal V\\to T$ be a smooth projective family with integral fibers, over an integral parameter variety, and let $g:\\mathcal V\\to Y$ be a morphism whose general fiber maps $g_t:V_t\\to Y$ are dominant and generically finite of degree at most a fixed integer $e$. After a finite parameter extension and shrinking, spread the geometric generic components of the zero divisor of the relative Jacobian over $Y_{\\rm sm}$ to divisors $\\mathcal R_1,\\ldots,\\mathcal R_N$ in $\\mathcal V$. Retain the notation $\\mathcal V,T,g$ after this base change. Let $I$ be the set of indices $j$ for which the image closure $\\overline{g_t((\\mathcal R_j)_t)}$ is a divisor in $Y$ for general $t$. Assume $$\\overline{g(\\mathcal R_j)}\\ne Y\\qquad(j\\in I).$$ Then, after shrinking again, one smooth nonempty open $Y^\\circ\\subset Y$ makes every general finite normal Stein cover of $g_t$ finite étale over $Y^\\circ$. There are only finitely many such covers of $Y$, up to isomorphism over $Y$. The same conclusion holds simultaneously for finitely many projections. The open and finite lists may depend on all fixed parameters of the family.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-scalar-jets",
        "exact_quote": "For sufficiently small $t$, let $P$ be the positive part of $2r(K+2tA)$, and put $\\rho=(P^n)^{1/n}$. At a very general point, every nonzero section of every Cartier multiple $kP$ has order at most $4k\\rho$. The same statement holds on a common resolution after adding an effective exceptional divisor that does not change the section spaces.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-jet-bundle-models",
        "exact_quote": "Both the total class $M$ and the slice class have big semiample positive parts $P$ on $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt models $Z$, with $$K_Z+\\Delta\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}c_tP,\\qquad \\Delta\\ge0,$$ where $c_t$ is bounded independently of small $t$. Their volumes satisfy, respectively, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(M)&=\\frac{(2n+1)!}{(n!)^2}\n   \\int_0^q\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(L_b)\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(L_{q-b})\\,db,\\label{jet:total-volume}\\\\\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(L+q\\xi)&=(n+1)\\int_0^q\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(L_b)\\,db.\\label{jet:slice-volume}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular both volumes are bounded above and below by positive constants times $q$. For each rational weight, on common resolutions, $$\\label{jet:weight-domination}\n P\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}N_{b,1}+N_{q-b,2}+E_b,\\qquad E_b\\ge0,$$ with the analogous one-term formula on slices. At a generic point of a base divisor of a slice, and on a general torus fiber, the full system has no fixed subtraction.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-jet-bundle-models",

For each rational weight, on common resolutions, $$\\label{jet:weight-domination}\n P\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}N_{b,1}+N_{q-b,2}+E_b,\\qquad E_b\\ge0,$$ with the analogous one-term formula on slices.         "exact_quote": "For each fixed sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$, there is an integer $q>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small rational $t>0$ and $r$ as in Equation <a href=\"#jet:normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"jet:normalization\">[jet:normalization]</a>, the positive part $P$ of Equation <a href=\"#jet:two-slot\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"jet:two-slot\">[jet:two-slot]</a> has $$\\varepsilon(P;x)>2n+2$$ at a very general smooth point of its torus open. In particular, for some sufficiently divisible integer $k>0$, the complete system $|kP|$ separates jets of order strictly greater than $(2n+2)k$ at such a point.           "anchor": "stmt-prop-large-seshadri",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$, there is an integer $q>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small rational $t>0$ and $r$ as in Equation <a href=\"#jet:normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"jet:normalization\">[jet:normalization]</a>, the positive part $P$ of Equation <a href=\"#jet:two-slot\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"jet:two-slot\">[jet:two-slot]</a> has $$\\varepsilon(P;x)>2n+2$$ at a very general smooth point of its torus open.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-large-seshadri",
          "exact_quote": "In particular, for some sufficiently divisible integer $k>0$, the complete system $|kP|$ separates jets of order strictly greater than $(2n+2)k$ at such a point.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prop-large-seshadri"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-common-finite-data-frobenius",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety of dimension $n\\ge2$. Let $L,H$ be rational Cartier divisors, with $H$ ample, let $S$ be an integral Cartier divisor, and fix an integer $q>0$ and real numbers $r>1$, $\\epsilon>0$. Suppose $$\\label{common:intersection-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n H^n\\le(2\\epsilon)^n,\\qquad\n K_WH^{n-1}\\le2H^n/r,\\qquad\n (2L+qS)H^{n-1}\\le4H^n,\\\\\n (14\\epsilon)^n<\\tfrac14,\\qquad 80\\epsilon<\\tfrac34.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Assume that the following fixed data exist.*\n\n1.  *A smooth integral complete-intersection flag from $W$ to a curve $C$, whose successive divisor classes are $h_i=l_iH$ for fixed positive integers $l_i$, with $\\mu_{\\min}(\\Omega_W^1|_C)\\ge0$.*\n\n2.  *On the projective bundle $$Z=\\mathbf P\\bigl(\\mathcal O_W(S)_1\\oplus\n                             \\mathcal O_W(S)_2\\bigr)\\longrightarrow W\\times W,\n     \\qquad \\xi=c_1(\\mathcal O_Z(1)),\\qquad M=L_1+L_2+q\\xi,$$ use the convention that the direct image of $\\mathcal O_Z(a\\xi)$ is the $a$-th symmetric power of the displayed sum. A smooth point $z_*$ in the complement of the two axes and finitely many sections of $k_0M$, for some integer $k_0>0$ with $k_0L$ Cartier, generate jets through order $(2n+2)k_0$ at $z_*$.*\n\n3.  *A point $x\\in W$, its blowup $\\pi_x:\\widehat W\\to W$ with exceptional divisor $J$, and a curve class $$\\gamma=f_*(A_1\\cdots A_{n-1}),$$ where $f:V\\to\\widehat W$ is one fixed birational morphism, $V$ is smooth integral projective, and the $A_i$ are ample integral Cartier divisors, such that $J\\cdot\\gamma>0$ and $$\\label{common:movable-input}\n     \\pi_x^*(L+K_W/2+\\lambda S)\\cdot\\gamma\n     \\le40\\epsilon J\\cdot\\gamma\\qquad(0\\le\\lambda\\le q).$$ It suffices to check the two endpoints in this affine inequality.*\n\n*These data cannot coexist.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-smooth-good-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex variety with pseudo-effective $K_X$. There is a normal projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety $V$ and a $K_X$-negative birational contraction $X\\dashrightarrow V$ such that $K_V$ is $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier and semiample. On a common smooth resolution $p:W\\to X$, $q:W\\to V$, compatible canonical divisors satisfy $$p^*K_X=q^*K_V+E,\n \\qquad E\\geq0\\quad\\text{and }E\\text{ is }q\\text{-exceptional}.$$ In particular $\\kappa(X,K_X)=\\kappa(V,K_V)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-smooth-good-model",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex variety with pseudo-effective $K_X$. There is a normal projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety $V$ and a $K_X$-negative birational contraction $X\\dashrightarrow V$ such that $K_V$ is $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier and semiample. On a common smooth resolution $p:W\\to X$, $q:W\\to V$, compatible canonical divisors satisfy $$p^*K_X=q^*K_V+E,\n \\qquad E\\geq0\\quad\\text{and }E\\text{ is }q\\text{-exceptional}.$$ In particular $\\kappa(X,K_X)=\\kappa(V,K_V)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex variety with pseudo-effective $K_X$. There is a normal projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety $V$ and a $K_X$-negative birational contraction $X\\dashrightarrow V$ such that $K_V$ is $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier and semiample. On a common smooth resolution $p:W\\to X$, $q:W\\to V$, compatible canonical divisors satisfy $$p^*K_X=q^*K_V+E,\n \\qquad E\\geq0\\quad\\text{and }E\\text{ is }q\\text{-exceptional}.$$ In particular $\\kappa(X,K_X)=\\kappa(V,K_V)$.",
      "explanation": "The smooth zero-boundary case has a klt good model with an effective exceptional comparison that preserves all pluricanonical section spaces.",

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-dlt-extension",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\Delta)$ be a projective dlt pair over $\\mathbb{C}$, where $\\Delta$ is an effective rational divisor, and put $S=\\lfloor\\Delta\\rfloor$. Assume that $K_X+\\Delta$ is nef and that, for an effective rational divisor $D$, $$K_X+\\Delta\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}D\\geq0,\n \\qquad S\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}D.$$ Then, for every integer $m\\geq2$ for which $m(K_X+\\Delta)$ is Cartier, the restriction map $$H^0\\bigl(X,\\mathcal{O}_X(m(K_X+\\Delta))\\bigr)\n \\longrightarrow\n H^0\\bigl(S,\\mathcal{O}_X(m(K_X+\\Delta))|_S\\bigr)$$ is surjective.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-lc-finite-generation",
        "exact_quote": "1.  *Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, let $(X,\\Delta)$ be a normal projective log canonical pair over $k$, and assume that $\\Delta\\geq0$ is rational and $D=K_X+\\Delta$ is $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. Then $$R(X,D)=\\bigoplus_{m\\geq0}\n     H^0\\bigl(X,\\mathcal{O}_X(\\lfloor mD\\rfloor)\\bigr)$$ is a finitely generated $k$-algebra.*\n\n2.  *Let $(X,\\Delta)$ be a log canonical pair over $\\mathbb{C}$, with $\\Delta\\geq0$ rational and $K_X+\\Delta$ $\\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. For every proper morphism $f:X\\to S$ onto an algebraic variety $S$, the relative log canonical algebra $$\\mathcal R(X/S,K_X+\\Delta)\n     =\\bigoplus_{m\\geq0}f_*\\mathcal{O}_X\\bigl(\\lfloor m(K_X+\\Delta)\\rfloor\\bigr)$$ is a finitely generated $\\mathcal{O}_S$-algebra.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-uniruled-mumford",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety.*\n\n1.  *$X$ is uniruled if and only if $\\kappa(X)=-\\infty$.*\n\n2.  *$X$ is rationally connected if and only if $$H^0\\bigl(X,(\\Omega_X^1)^{\\otimes m}\\bigr)=0\n     \\quad\\text{for every integer }m>0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-pluriforms",
        "exact_quote": "1.  *For every smooth connected projective complex variety $X$, $$\\dim R(X)=\n     \\max_{\\substack{p\\in\\mathbb{Z}_{\\geq0},\\,k\\in\\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\\\\\n     H^0(X,\\operatorname{Sym}^k(\\Omega_X^p))\\ne0}} p.$$*\n\n2.  *A compact connected Kähler manifold $X$ is rationally connected if and only if $$H^0\\bigl(X,\\operatorname{Sym}^k(\\Omega_X^p)\\bigr)=0\n     \\quad\\text{for every pair of integers }p,k>0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-pluriforms",
          "exact_quote": "1.  *For every smooth connected projective complex variety $X$, $$\\dim R(X)=\n     \\max_{\\substack{p\\in\\mathbb{Z}_{\\geq0},\\,k\\in\\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\\\\\n     H^0(X,\\operatorname{Sym}^k(\\Omega_X^p))\\ne0}} p.$$*\n\n2.  *A compact connected Kähler manifold $X$ is rationally connected if and only if $$H^0\\bigl(X,\\operatorname{Sym}^k(\\Omega_X^p)\\bigr)=0\n     \\quad\\text{for every pair of integers }p,k>0.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "1.  *For every smooth connected projective complex variety $X$, $$\\dim R(X)=\n     \\max_{\\substack{p\\in\\mathbb{Z}_{\\geq0},\\,k\\in\\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\\\\\n     H^0(X,\\operatorname{Sym}^k(\\Omega_X^p))\\ne0}} p.$$*\n\n2.  *A compact connected Kähler manifold $X$ is rationally connected if and only if $$H^0\\bigl(X,\\operatorname{Sym}^k(\\Omega_X^p)\\bigr)=0\n     \\quad\\text{for every pair of integers }p,k>0.$$",

        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety. If $X$ is positive-dimensional and $\\kappa(X)\\geq0$, then $$\\kappa^+(X)=\\kappa(X).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.4"
      },
```
