<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **nef rational Cartier divisor** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A rational Cartier divisor is *nef* if its degree on every curve is nonnegative.
- **semiample rational divisor** ([source](#sec:introduction)): It is *semiample* if a positive Cartier multiple is generated by global sections.
- **Iitaka dimension of a rational divisor** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We write $\kappa(L)$ for the Iitaka dimension of a rational divisor $L$, with value $-\infty$ if all positive integral systems are empty.
- **numerical dimension of a nef divisor** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a nef divisor $L$, write $\nu(L)=\max\{j:L^jH^{n-j}>0\}$, where $H$ is ample and $n=\dim X$.
- **log discrepancy** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a divisor $E$ on a smooth model $p:W\to X$, our log discrepancy is $$a(E;X,B)=1+\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E\bigl(K_W-p^*(K_X+B)\bigr).$$
- **log canonical and klt discrepancy conditions** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Log canonicity means nonnegative log discrepancies, and klt means strictly positive log discrepancies.
- **good log minimal model** ([source](#sec:induction)): For an effective real boundary $\Delta$, a good log minimal model of $(X,\Delta)$ is a log minimal model on which the adjoint divisor is semiample.
- **real semiampleness** ([source](#sec:induction)): For real divisors, semiampleness means real linear equivalence to the pullback of an ample real divisor by a contraction.
- **very general point** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A very general point avoids a countable union of proper closed subvarieties.
- **root gerbe of the projective line bundle** ([source](#pm-h-18)): For a positive integer $\ell$, the root gerbe $$\mathcal P=\sqrt[\ell]{\mathcal{O}_P(1)}$$ parametrizes $\ell$-th roots of the indicated line bundle without the choice of a section.
- **isolated generic base component** ([source](#sec:common-tools)): “Isolated at the generic point of $V$” means that this ideal is primary for the maximal ideal of $\mathcal O_{Z,\eta_V}$.
- **generation of jets** ([source](#common:frobenius-tools)): For a line bundle on a smooth variety, to generate jets through order $a$ at a point means surjectivity to its stalk modulo the $(a+1)$-st power of the maximal ideal.
- **truncated symmetric piece** ([source](#common:diagonal-graded)): For a rank-$n$ bundle $V$ in characteristic $p$, denote by $A_j(V)$ the degree-$j$ part of its symmetric algebra modulo the $p$-th powers of local generators.
- **quasi-projective analytic cover** ([source](#pm-h-65)): Here a quasi-projective cover means a complex analytic covering space biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety; its deck transformations need not be algebraic.
- **projective analytic morphism** ([source](#sec:analytic-abundance)): Here a projective analytic morphism is proper and possesses a relatively ample line bundle.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-trivial-perturbation-ceiling | \epsilon | < | $\frac{1}{4nm+2}$ | The same Cartier index persists through the driving program. |
| q-null-member-dimension | d | = | $n-1-r=n-v$ | The nontrivial numerical-dimension range. |
| q-base-component-degree | P^f\cdot V | ≤ | $(k/h)^aP^d$ | The degree estimate also permits a zero-dimensional component. |
| q-moving-kernel-multiplicity | h | = | $\lceil k\delta/2\rceil$ | Ordinary local ideal powers on a dense smooth incidence open. |
| q-moving-component-degree | P^f\cdot V_t | ≤ | $(2/\delta)^{d-f}P^d$ | Top-intersection positivity additionally requires meeting the contraction's isomorphism open. |
| q-moving-subadjunction-coefficient | c | ≤ | $2(d-f)/\delta$ | The coefficient is positive and depends on codimension and the level gap. |
| q-scalar-section-order | order of a nonzero section | ≤ | $4k\rho$ | Sufficiently small scaling parameter; the statement includes all Cartier degrees. |
| q-scalar-volume-root-ceiling | \rho | ≤ | $8\epsilon$ | The scaling parameter is sufficiently small after fixing the normalization. |
| q-two-slot-seshadri-lower-bound | \varepsilon(P;x) | > | $2n+2$ | The bundle weight is chosen after the normalization and before the scaling parameter. |
| q-frobenius-polarization-volume | H^n | ≤ | $(2\epsilon)^n$ | This is an assumed intersection bound, not an unconditional estimate. |
| q-frobenius-canonical-intersection | K_WH^{n-1} | ≤ | $2H^n/r$ | The canonical divisor need not be nef or pseudo-effective. |
| q-frobenius-combined-intersection | (2L+qS)H^{n-1} | ≤ | $4H^n$ | The source intersection bound is imposed before reduction. |
| q-frobenius-small-volume-condition | (14\epsilon)^n | < | $\tfrac14$ | Strict inequality supplies room for the residual-characteristic error. |
| q-frobenius-small-order-condition | 80\epsilon | < | $\tfrac34$ | The normalization is fixed before residual characteristic varies. |
| q-truncated-symmetric-slope | \mu_{\max}(A_j(V)) | ≤ | $(p-1)K_W\cdot C+nc$ | The constant is independent of the filtration index and sufficiently large residual characteristic. |
| q-diagonal-section-rank-fraction | h^0(\Delta_F,\mathcal L_p) | < | $R/4$ | The error constant is independent of the filtration index. |
| q-determinant-order-upper | \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{(x',x')}\sigma | ≤ | $R\bigl(80\epsilon+O(1/p)\bigr)$ | Fixed movable witness; error uniform over the determinant columns. |
| q-determinant-order-lower | \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{(x',x')}\sigma | ≥ | $3R/4$ | The diagonal rank deficit forces vanishing of rows in the maximal ideal. |
| q-supported-extension-cartier-range | m | ≥ | $2$ | Only multiples for which the adjoint multiple is Cartier. |

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-common-base-component",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety of dimension $d$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor, and let $k>0$ be an integer for which $kP$ is Cartier. Let $0\\ne\\mathcal H\\subset H^0(Z,\\mathcal{O}_Z(kP))$ be a linear subspace with proper base locus and base ideal $\\mathfrak b$. Suppose that $V$ is an integral base-locus component of dimension $f<d$, isolated at its generic point $\\eta_V$, and that $\\eta_V\\in Z_{\\rm sm}$. Write $a=d-f$ and $\\mathfrak m=\\mathfrak m_{\\mathcal{O}_{Z,\\eta_V}}$. If $$\\mathfrak b\\mathcal{O}_{Z,\\eta_V}\\subset\\mathfrak m^h\n \\qquad(h\\in\\mathbb{Z}_{>0}),$$ then $$\\label{common:base-component-degree}\n P^f\\cdot V\\leq (k/h)^aP^d.$$ There are a rational number $c$ with $0<c\\leq ak/h$ and an effective rational divisor $\\Theta\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}cP$ such that $(Z,\\Theta)$ is lc at $\\eta_V$ and has an lc place with center $V$. If $f>0$, on a smooth resolution $V^*$ one consequently has $$\\label{common:base-component-canonical}\n K_{V^*}\\cdot P^{f-1}\n \\leq (K_Z+cP)|_V\\cdot P^{f-1}.$$ The degree estimate includes $f=0$; the canonical estimate is asserted only for positive-dimensional $V$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-base-component-degree",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-common-moving-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be an integral projective variety of dimension $d$, and let $P$ be a nef, big, semiample rational Cartier divisor. Fix an integer $k$ with $kP$ Cartier and positive real numbers $$\\tau_0<\\tau_1<\\cdots<\\tau_d,\n \\qquad\\tau_{i+1}-\\tau_i=\\delta>0.$$ For a smooth point $x$, let $\\mathcal H_i(x)$ be the entire subspace of sections of $kP$ vanishing at $x$ to order at least $\\lceil k\\tau_i\\rceil$. Suppose that for very general $x$ all these subspaces are nonzero and the base locus of $\\mathcal H_0(x)$ has a positive-dimensional component through $x$. If $k\\delta\\geq4$, then, after an algebraic parameter extension and restriction to nonempty opens, there are an irreducible parameter space $T$, a fixed adjacent pair of levels, and a family of integral subvarieties $V_t$ common to the two base loci such that the incidence $$\\Gamma=\\{(t,z):z\\in V_t\\}\\subset T\\times Z$$ dominates $Z$. Each general $V_t$ has dimension in $[1,d-1]$, is an isolated component of the higher base locus at its generic point, and its entire higher-series base ideal is contained there in $$\\label{common:moving-multiplicity-order}\n \\mathcal I_{V_t}^{h},\\qquad h=\\lceil k\\delta/2\\rceil,\n \\qquad k/h\\leq2/\\delta.$$ These are ordinary local ideal powers in the smooth ambient open.*\n\n*The incidence base ideal lies in $\\mathcal I_\\Gamma^h$ on a dense open of $\\Gamma$. If, in addition, $Z$ is $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial and klt, then for a general member, with $f=\\dim V_t$, one has $$\\label{common:tracking-bounds}\n \\begin{split}\n P^f\\cdot V_t&\\le(2/\\delta)^{d-f}P^d,\\\\\n K_{V_t^*}P^{f-1}&\\le(K_Z+cP)|_{V_t}P^{f-1},\n \\qquad 0<c\\le2(d-f)/\\delta .\n \\end{split}$$ In particular the weaker bound $c\\le2d/\\delta$ also holds. Positivity of $P^f\\cdot V_t$ follows if $V_t$ meets the open where the big semiample contraction is an isomorphism. No such positivity is asserted for every exceptional subvariety.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-moving-kernel-multiplicity",
      "kind": "constant",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "h",
      "regime": {

          "exact_quote": "For sufficiently small $t$, let $P$ be the positive part of $2r(K+2tA)$, and put $\\rho=(P^n)^{1/n}$.           "exact_quote": "For each fixed sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$, there is an integer $q>0$ such that, for all sufficiently small rational $t>0$ and $r$ as in Equation <a href=\"#jet:normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"jet:normalization\">[jet:normalization]</a>, the positive part $P$ of Equation <a href=\"#jet:two-slot\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"jet:two-slot\">[jet:two-slot]</a> has $$\\varepsilon(P;x)>2n+2$$ at a very general smooth point of its torus open. Choose bases adapted to the orders of vanishing at $x'$ in each factor. In each degree their leading terms are linearly independent. Tensor products of these leading terms remain independent in each bidegree of the two disjoint sets of parameters; terms of different bidegrees cannot cancel. Every nonzero tensor consequently has order at most the sum of the two maximum basis orders. Applying Equation <a href=\"#common:slot-order\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"common:slot-order\">[common:slot-order]</a>, we obtain $$\\label{common:determinant-order-upper}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_{(x',x')}\\sigma\\le R\\bigl(80\\epsilon+O(1/p)\\bigr).$$",

Suppose $$\\label{common:intersection-bounds}\n \\begin{gathered}\n H^n\\le(2\\epsilon)^n,\\qquad\n K_WH^{n-1}\\le2H^n/r,\\qquad\n (2L+qS)H^{n-1}\\le4H^n,\\\\\n (14\\epsilon)^n<\\tfrac14,\\qquad 80\\epsilon<\\tfrac34.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Assume that the following fixed data exist.*\n\n1.  *A smooth integral complete-intersection flag from $W$ to a curve $C$, whose successive divisor classes are $h_i=l_iH$ for fixed positive integers $l_i$, with $\\mu_{\\min}(\\Omega_W^1|_C)\\ge0$.*\n\n2.  *On the projective bundle $$Z=\\mathbf P\\bigl(\\mathcal O_W(S)_1\\oplus\n                             \\mathcal O_W(S)_2\\bigr)\\longrightarrow W\\times W,\n     \\qquad \\xi=c_1(\\mathcal O_Z(1)),\\qquad M=L_1+L_2+q\\xi,$$ use the convention that the direct image of $\\mathcal O_Z(a\\xi)$ is the $a$-th symmetric power of the displayed sum. A smooth point $z_*$ in the complement of the two axes and finitely many sections of $k_0M$, for some integer $k_0>0$ with $k_0L$ Cartier, generate jets through order $(2n+2)k_0$ at $z_*$.*\n\n3.  *A point $x\\in W$, its blowup $\\pi_x:\\widehat W\\to W$ with exceptional divisor $J$, and a curve class $$\\gamma=f_*(A_1\\cdots A_{n-1}),$$ where $f:V\\to\\widehat W$ is one fixed birational morphism, $V$ is smooth integral projective, and the $A_i$ are ample integral Cartier divisors, such that $J\\cdot\\gamma>0$ and $$\\label{common:movable-input}\n     \\pi_x^*(L+K_W/2+\\lambda S)\\cdot\\gamma\n     \\le40\\epsilon J\\cdot\\gamma\\qquad(0\\le\\lambda\\le q).$$ It suffices to check the two endpoints in this affine inequality.*\n\n*These data cannot coexist.",
            "source_id": "PM-034.4"
          }
        ],
        "parameter_scope": "Fixed movable witness; error uniform over the determinant columns.",
        "quantifiers": []

      "relation": "≤",
      "value_latex": "R\\bigl(80\\epsilon+O(1/p)\\bigr)"

Trivialize its line bundles near that point. Invertible constant row operations make more than $3R/4$ rows vanish at the point. Each entry of those rows belongs to the maximal ideal, so every term of the determinant has order at least $3R/4$. Therefore $$\\label{common:determinant-order-lower}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_{(x',x')}\\sigma\\ge3R/4.$$ Equations <a href=\"#common:determinant-order-upper\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"common:determinant-order-upper\">[common:determinant-order-upper]</a> and <a href=\"#common:determinant-order-lower\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"common:determinant-order-lower\">[common:determinant-order-lower]</a> contradict $80\\epsilon<3/4$ for sufficiently large $p$.",

Assume that $K_X+\\Delta$ is nef and that, for an effective rational divisor $D$, $$K_X+\\Delta\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}D\\geq0,\n \\qquad S\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}D.$$ Then, for every integer $m\\geq2$ for which $m(K_X+\\Delta)$ is Cartier, the restriction map $$H^0\\bigl(X,\\mathcal{O}_X(m(K_X+\\Delta))\\bigr)\n \\longrightarrow\n H^0\\bigl(S,\\mathcal{O}_X(m(K_X+\\Delta))|_S\\bigr)$$ is surjective.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-subadditivity-input** [claim](#stmt-asm-iitaka) / [proof](#pm-h-6): Use the completed companion logarithmic statement only in the Albanese reduction. -> logarithmic Iitaka lower bound
- **step-nef-comparison** [claim](#stmt-mmp-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mmp-comparison): Apply negativity to the discrepancy comparison and use projection formula to descend generation. -> effective exceptional comparison and nef-source equality
- **step-boundary-gluing** [claim](#stmt-prop-boundary-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-boundary-restriction): Apply componentwise adjunction and lower-dimensional models, then glue along the conductor. -> whole-boundary semiampleness
- **step-uniform-perturbation** [claim](#stmt-mmp-trivial-perturbation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mmp-trivial-perturbation): Combine the rational-curve length bound with the fixed Cartier lattice and line-bundle descent. -> trivial driving steps with unchanged index
- **step-boundary-image** [claim](#stmt-sg-numerical-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sg-numerical-boundary): Use nef intersections and a negative semidefinite boundary intersection form. -> positive component of maximal image dimension with smaller bad intersection image
- **step-root-adjunction** [claim](#stmt-prop-signed-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-signed-construction): Normalize root covers, separate opposite signs, descend equivariant adjunction and embed the Stein image. -> Cartier positive boundary with compatible duality and scheme charts
- **step-lowest-hodge-piece** [claim](#stmt-hg-lowest-piece) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hg-lowest-piece): Use Du Bois comparison, coherent duality and projective strictness while retaining the actual inclusion. -> lowest filtered obstruction sheaf
- **step-negative-twist** [claim](#stmt-hg-negative-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hg-negative-vanishing): Construct a finite-cover retraction and apply ordinary negative-ample Hodge-module vanishing. -> negative-degree hypercohomology vanishing
- **step-formal-lifting** [claim](#stmt-prop-formal-lifting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hg-negative-vanishing-2): Express connecting classes as a derivation; use the graph identity and symbol vanishing to kill coordinate and conormal errors. -> surjective transitions at every infinitesimal order
- **step-null-family** [claim](#stmt-prop-null-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-null-family): Algebraize finite formal thickenings and select a dominating Hilbert family by uncountability. -> dominating compact boundary-avoiding null members
- **step-vertical-descent** [claim](#stmt-sg-vertical-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sg-vertical-descent): Subtract the minimal normalized coefficient over each base prime and eliminate the residual on surface slices. -> actual rational-divisor pullback
- **step-signed-abundance** [claim](#stmt-thm-signed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sg-vertical-descent-2): Bound nef-reduction dimension using null members, flatten its graph, descend the signed divisor and prove the base divisor big. -> equality of Iitaka and numerical dimensions
- **step-albanese-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-ex-irregularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-irregularity): Apply zero-boundary subadditivity to the resolved Albanese map and then use torsion of numerically trivial line bundles. -> irregularity zero and rational-linear numerical comparison
- **step-web-quotient** [claim](#stmt-ex-web) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-web): Track constructible chain endpoints, identify maximal-dimensional leaf closures and parameterize their rational quotient. -> algebraic leaf quotient with connected general fiber
- **step-general-type-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-prop-general-type) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-general-type): Spread zero-Iitaka fibers, annihilate their tangent web with a fixed current and use lower-dimensional good models on the quotient. -> general type for moving proper subvarieties
- **step-supporting-positivity** [claim](#stmt-ex-supporting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-supporting): Use general-type fibers of a moving subsystem and convexity of the pseudo-effective cone. -> strict supporting-functional positivity
- **step-fixed-current-acc** [claim](#stmt-ex-valuation-acc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-valuation-acc): Use Skoda integrability and fixed Siu loci, decreasing discrepancies after principalization. -> ascending chain condition for divisorial Lelong numbers
- **step-no-current-pullback** [claim](#stmt-prop-no-pullback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-no-pullback): Descend the current to a minimal quotient, construct small-degree rational curves and turn an ACC limiting gap into exact equality. -> exclusion of positive-dimensional annihilated fibration directions
- **step-current-model-comparison** [claim](#stmt-ex-current-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-current-comparison): Compare multiplier-generated divisors on a common resolution and use negativity for exceptional equality. -> Lelong domination of canonical differences
- **step-bounded-curve-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-prop-bounded-curves) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bounded-curves): Bound exceptional contacts with the fixed terminal discrepancy lattice and obtain an exact zero residual-current gap. -> contradiction to the current-pullback exclusion
- **step-special-termination** [claim](#stmt-prop-special-termination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-special-termination): Concatenate finite relative programs on floor components and apply lower-dimensional absolute model existence. -> special termination and signed-floor termination
- **step-signed-canonical-alternative** [claim](#stmt-prop-signed-alternative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-signed-alternative): Terminate the reduced-support logarithmic program and apply signed abundance; nonbigness would force canonical nonvanishing. -> big logarithmic adjoint
- **step-fixed-cover-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-ex-fixed-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-fixed-cover): Use the birational-group identity component to obtain an abelian cover, then push forward its divisorial canonical current. -> contradiction to canonical nonvanishing failure
- **step-generic-subadjunction** [claim](#stmt-common-numerical-subadjunction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-common-numerical-subadjunction): Choose a minimal dominating dlt stratum, verify the klt-trivial rank condition and discard nonnegative discriminant, moduli and ramification intersections. -> canonical-intersection upper bound
- **step-base-component-estimate** [claim](#stmt-common-base-component) / [proof](#proof-stmt-common-base-component): Use parameter-ideal multiplicity and realize the generic ideal threshold by general divisor members. -> degree and canonical bounds
- **step-moving-full-kernels** [claim](#stmt-common-moving-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-common-moving-kernel): Track adjacent components of full jet kernels and differentiate parameter coefficients to establish ordinary ideal powers. -> dominant moving incidence with multiplicity and tracking estimates
- **step-fiber-ideal-restriction** [claim](#stmt-common-fiber-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-common-fiber-restriction): Choose a general incidence point and use smoothness over the actual image to restrict ordinary ideals. -> isolated high-multiplicity restricted series
- **step-finite-branch-complement** [claim](#stmt-common-finite-covers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-common-finite-covers): Spread ramification components, remove their proper image closures and classify bounded-degree étale covers of the fixed complement. -> finite normal cover lists
- **step-scalar-jet-bound** [claim](#stmt-prop-scalar-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scalar-jets): Turn excessive order into a moving base component and then general-type curves with bounded normalized degree. -> uniform scalar order ceiling
- **step-two-slot-models** [claim](#stmt-jet-bundle-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-bundle-models): Realize total and slice classes as big klt adjoints and compare full fixed divisors with weight subsystems. -> semiample positive parts, volume integrals and effective weight comparisons
- **step-large-two-slot-jets** [claim](#stmt-prop-large-seshadri) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-large-seshadri): Analyze slice fibers, ruled images, multisections and correspondences; exclude moving branch divisors and then fix both covers. -> large Seshadri constant and a finite jet-generating system
- **step-spread-jet-products** [claim](#stmt-common-positive-characteristic-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-common-positive-characteristic-jets): Multiply fixed jet sections and add a nonvanishing filler chosen from a fixed finite list. -> surjection onto the Frobenius local quotient
- **step-frobenius-generic-rank** [claim](#stmt-common-generic-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-common-generic-rank): Extract the constant coefficient in the torus Frobenius basis to obtain spanning divisible-weight values. -> generic full evaluation rank
- **step-truncated-slope** [claim](#stmt-common-truncated-slope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-common-truncated-slope): Combine Langer's minimum-slope estimate, tensor quotient bounds and the perfect complementary multiplication pairing. -> uniform maximum slope of truncated symmetric pieces
- **step-diagonal-section-bound** [claim](#stmt-common-diagonal-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-common-diagonal-sections): Restrict along the fixed flag and sum graded ranks against a common scalar section polynomial. -> strict diagonal rank deficit
- **step-finite-data-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-common-finite-data-frobenius) / [proof](#pm-h-54): Choose a nonzero maximal minor and compare its diagonal rank-forced order with the order bound in each external-product factor. -> incompatibility of the fixed data
- **step-smooth-nonvanishing** [claim](#stmt-thm-smooth-nonvanishing) / [proof](#fr:geometric-witnesses): Fix the small ample flag, a strongly movable blowup witness and finitely many two-slot jet sections before applying the independent incompatibility theorem. -> smooth canonical nonvanishing
- **step-good-model-induction** [claim](#stmt-prop-inductive-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-inductive-reduction): Reduce real nef boundaries to rational simplices, treat positive Iitaka dimension and analyze the zero-Iitaka perturbation cases. -> current-dimensional real-boundary good models
- **step-complex-main-abundance** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-smooth-nonvanishing): Iterate the dimension induction and descend evaluation from the good model through equality of nef pullbacks. -> semiampleness on the original complex variety
- **step-field-descent** [claim](#stmt-prop-field-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-field-descent): Descend finite defining data, transfer nefness using Hilbert families and descend the Cartier evaluation map by faithful flatness. -> main abundance over the stated fields
- **step-real-good-model-record** [claim](#stmt-thm-good-models-complex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-good-models-complex): Record the full real-boundary conclusion of the simultaneous induction. -> all-dimensional complex real-boundary good models
- **step-smooth-good-model** [claim](#stmt-cor-smooth-good-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-smooth-good-model): Apply real-boundary good-model existence to the zero-boundary klt pair and compare section spaces using exceptional effectivity. -> semiample klt canonical model and matching Iitaka dimension
- **step-semiample-zero** [claim](#stmt-lem-semiample-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-semiample-zero): Pull back a linear form nonzero at the point image of a generated multiple. -> actual trivializing Cartier multiple
- **step-adjoint-zero** [claim](#stmt-cor-qlinear-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-qlinear-zero): Apply main abundance and then the semiample zero-Iitaka lemma. -> rational-linear triviality
- **step-slc-gluing** [claim](#stmt-cor-slc-abundance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-slc-abundance): Generate a common multiple on the normalization and descend it by the conductor gluing theorem. -> semi-log-canonical abundance
- **step-supported-dlt-extension** [claim](#stmt-cor-dlt-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-dlt-extension): Combine main semiampleness with the cited supported extension implication. -> restriction surjectivity
- **step-rounded-ring-generation** [claim](#stmt-cor-lc-finite-generation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-lc-finite-generation): Compare every rounded degree with the nef model, decompose into residue-class section modules and apply the good-model relative reduction. -> absolute and proper complex relative finite generation
- **step-uniruled-tensor-classification** [claim](#stmt-cor-uniruled-mumford) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-uniruled-mumford): Combine pseudo-effective canonical duality with nonvanishing and the cited tensor criterion reduction. -> uniruledness and rational-connectedness equivalences
- **step-pluriform-mrc** [claim](#stmt-cor-pluriforms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-pluriforms): Use cotangent filtrations on rationally connected fibers, pull back base pluricanonical forms and apply the Kähler projectivity reduction. -> MRC dimension and compact Kähler pluriform criterion
- **step-cotangent-invariants** [claim](#stmt-cor-cotangent-mrc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-cotangent-mrc): Apply birational fibration properties and trivial canonical good models of general Iitaka fibers. -> cotangent invariant equalities
- **step-fundamental-group-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-cor-finite-pi-one) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-finite-pi-one): Apply the cited finiteness criterion after establishing zero cotangent invariant. -> finite ordinary topological fundamental group
- **step-quasi-projective-cover** [claim](#stmt-cor-quasi-projective-covers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-quasi-projective-covers): Supply smooth abundance to the cited cover classification theorem. -> holomorphic bundle descriptions after finite étale Galois cover
- **step-relative-analytic-generation** [claim](#stmt-cor-analytic-abundance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-analytic-abundance): Apply algebraic abundance on general stratum fibers, use relative nef-and-log-abundant generation and combine local multiples on a compact cover. -> relative generation on a neighborhood with a subset-dependent Cartier multiple

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-special-termination",
          "exact_quote": "Assume Assumption <a href=\"#mmp:lower-models\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mmp:lower-models\">3</a>. Let $(V,\\Delta)$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial dlt $n$-fold with rational boundary and pseudo-effective adjoint. Choose an effective ample rational divisor $A$ such that $(V,\\Delta+A)$ is dlt and $K_V+\\Delta+A$ is nef. The LMMP for $K_V+\\Delta$ with scaling of $A$ has special termination: if the program is infinite, its flipping loci are eventually disjoint from the round-down of the boundary.*\n\n*In particular, if at every stage the adjoint is rationally linearly equivalent to a signed divisor supported on that round-down, the program terminates at a log minimal model.",

        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-common-numerical-subadjunction",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-common-numerical-subadjunction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety over $\\mathbb{C}$, let $\\Theta\\geq0$ be a rational divisor, and let $V\\subset Z$ be an integral positive-dimensional subvariety. Suppose that $(Z,\\Theta)$ is lc at the generic point of $V$ and that a divisor of log discrepancy zero has center $V$. Let $f=\\dim V$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor on $V$, and let $\\rho:V^*\\to V$ be a smooth projective resolution, factoring through the normalization. Then $$\\label{common:subadjunction-inequality}\n K_{V^*}\\cdot(\\rho^*P)^{f-1}\n \\leq (K_Z+\\Theta)|_V\\cdot P^{f-1}.$$ The right side is the intersection on $V$ of the restricted rational Cartier class with $P^{f-1}$. No $\\mathbb{Q}$-Gorenstein hypothesis on the normalization of $V$ is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-generic-subadjunction",
      "inputs": [
        "generic lc center",
        "nef class on the center"
      ],
      "method": "Choose a minimal dominating dlt stratum, verify the klt-trivial rank condition and discard nonnegative discriminant, moduli and ramification intersections.",
      "output": "canonical-intersection upper bound",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-common-numerical-subadjunction"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-common-base-component",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-generic-subadjunction"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-common-base-component",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety of dimension $d$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor, and let $k>0$ be an integer for which $kP$ is Cartier. Let $0\\ne\\mathcal H\\subset H^0(Z,\\mathcal{O}_Z(kP))$ be a linear subspace with proper base locus and base ideal $\\mathfrak b$. Suppose that $V$ is an integral base-locus component of dimension $f<d$, isolated at its generic point $\\eta_V$, and that $\\eta_V\\in Z_{\\rm sm}$. Write $a=d-f$ and $\\mathfrak m=\\mathfrak m_{\\mathcal{O}_{Z,\\eta_V}}$. If $$\\mathfrak b\\mathcal{O}_{Z,\\eta_V}\\subset\\mathfrak m^h\n \\qquad(h\\in\\mathbb{Z}_{>0}),$$ then $$\\label{common:base-component-degree}\n P^f\\cdot V\\leq (k/h)^aP^d.$$ There are a rational number $c$ with $0<c\\leq ak/h$ and an effective rational divisor $\\Theta\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}cP$ such that $(Z,\\Theta)$ is lc at $\\eta_V$ and has an lc place with center $V$. If $f>0$, on a smooth resolution $V^*$ one consequently has $$\\label{common:base-component-canonical}\n K_{V^*}\\cdot P^{f-1}\n \\leq (K_Z+cP)|_V\\cdot P^{f-1}.$$ The degree estimate includes $f=0$; the canonical estimate is asserted only for positive-dimensional $V$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-common-moving-kernel",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-base-component-estimate"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-common-moving-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be an integral projective variety of dimension $d$, and let $P$ be a nef, big, semiample rational Cartier divisor. Fix an integer $k$ with $kP$ Cartier and positive real numbers $$\\tau_0<\\tau_1<\\cdots<\\tau_d,\n \\qquad\\tau_{i+1}-\\tau_i=\\delta>0.$$ For a smooth point $x$, let $\\mathcal H_i(x)$ be the entire subspace of sections of $kP$ vanishing at $x$ to order at least $\\lceil k\\tau_i\\rceil$. Suppose that for very general $x$ all these subspaces are nonzero and the base locus of $\\mathcal H_0(x)$ has a positive-dimensional component through $x$. If $k\\delta\\geq4$, then, after an algebraic parameter extension and restriction to nonempty opens, there are an irreducible parameter space $T$, a fixed adjacent pair of levels, and a family of integral subvarieties $V_t$ common to the two base loci such that the incidence $$\\Gamma=\\{(t,z):z\\in V_t\\}\\subset T\\times Z$$ dominates $Z$. Each general $V_t$ has dimension in $[1,d-1]$, is an isolated component of the higher base locus at its generic point, and its entire higher-series base ideal is contained there in $$\\label{common:moving-multiplicity-order}\n \\mathcal I_{V_t}^{h},\\qquad h=\\lceil k\\delta/2\\rceil,\n \\qquad k/h\\leq2/\\delta.$$ These are ordinary local ideal powers in the smooth ambient open.*\n\n*The incidence base ideal lies in $\\mathcal I_\\Gamma^h$ on a dense open of $\\Gamma$. If, in addition, $Z$ is $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial and klt, then for a general member, with $f=\\dim V_t$, one has $$\\label{common:tracking-bounds}\n \\begin{split}\n P^f\\cdot V_t&\\le(2/\\delta)^{d-f}P^d,\\\\\n K_{V_t^*}P^{f-1}&\\le(K_Z+cP)|_{V_t}P^{f-1},\n \\qquad 0<c\\le2(d-f)/\\delta .\n \\end{split}$$ In particular the weaker bound $c\\le2d/\\delta$ also holds. Positivity of $P^f\\cdot V_t$ follows if $V_t$ meets the open where the big semiample contraction is an isomorphism. No such positivity is asserted for every exceptional subvariety.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "In the setting of Lemma <a href=\"#common:moving-kernel\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"common:moving-kernel\">29</a>, let $g:Z\\to B$ be a morphism. There is a dense open of the incidence on which the following holds. Choose $(t,z)$ in that open, let $b=g(z)$, and suppose that $V_t$ is smooth over the smooth open of its actual image at $z$. Let $F$ be the reduced component through $z$ of its scheme fiber. Locally on $Z_b$ at $z$, restriction of the higher kernel’s base ideal has support exactly $F$ and is contained in $\\mathcal I_F^h$. If $F$ is proper in an integral ambient fiber component containing it, the restricted series is nonzero there and $F$ is an isolated base component at its generic point. Application of Lemma <a href=\"#common:base-component\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"common:base-component\">28</a> on that ambient component requires all its hypotheses, including projectivity, $\\mathbb{Q}$-factoriality, klt singularities, and smoothness at the generic point of $F$, separately; they are not conclusions of this restriction lemma.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a normal projective complex variety. Let $\\mathcal V\\to T$ be a smooth projective family with integral fibers, over an integral parameter variety, and let $g:\\mathcal V\\to Y$ be a morphism whose general fiber maps $g_t:V_t\\to Y$ are dominant and generically finite of degree at most a fixed integer $e$. After a finite parameter extension and shrinking, spread the geometric generic components of the zero divisor of the relative Jacobian over $Y_{\\rm sm}$ to divisors $\\mathcal R_1,\\ldots,\\mathcal R_N$ in $\\mathcal V$. Retain the notation $\\mathcal V,T,g$ after this base change. Let $I$ be the set of indices $j$ for which the image closure $\\overline{g_t((\\mathcal R_j)_t)}$ is a divisor in $Y$ for general $t$. Assume $$\\overline{g(\\mathcal R_j)}\\ne Y\\qquad(j\\in I).$$ Then, after shrinking again, one smooth nonempty open $Y^\\circ\\subset Y$ makes every general finite normal Stein cover of $g_t$ finite étale over $Y^\\circ$. There are only finitely many such covers of $Y$, up to isomorphism over $Y$. The same conclusion holds simultaneously for finitely many projections. The open and finite lists may depend on all fixed parameters of the family.",

        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-jet-bundle-models",
          "exact_quote": "Both the total class $M$ and the slice class have big semiample positive parts $P$ on $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt models $Z$, with $$K_Z+\\Delta\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}c_tP,\\qquad \\Delta\\ge0,$$ where $c_t$ is bounded independently of small $t$. Their volumes satisfy, respectively, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(M)&=\\frac{(2n+1)!}{(n!)^2}\n   \\int_0^q\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(L_b)\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(L_{q-b})\\,db,\\label{jet:total-volume}\\\\\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(L+q\\xi)&=(n+1)\\int_0^q\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(L_b)\\,db.\\label{jet:slice-volume}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular both volumes are bounded above and below by positive constants times $q$. For each rational weight, on common resolutions, $$\\label{jet:weight-domination}\n P\\sim_{\\mathbb{Q}}N_{b,1}+N_{q-b,2}+E_b,\\qquad E_b\\ge0,$$ with the analogous one-term formula on slices. At a generic point of a base divisor of a slice, and on a general torus fiber, the full system has no fixed subtraction.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.4"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-nef-rational-lc-generation**: A nef rational log canonical adjoint on the original normal projective variety has a globally generated positive Cartier multiple.
- **invariant-signed-boundary-abundance**: A signed boundary representative gives abundance when the adjoint is nef, a positive boundary subtraction remains pseudo-effective, and the restriction to the whole reduced boundary is semiample.
- **invariant-semiample-zero-triviality**: A semiample rational Cartier divisor of Iitaka dimension zero is rationally linearly trivial.
- **invariant-real-boundary-good-models**: A pseudo-effective real log canonical adjoint admits a good log minimal model over the complex numbers.
- **invariant-analytic-generation-near-compact-subsets**: Relative generation holds on a neighborhood of each compact base subset after choosing a suitable Cartier multiple.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-pseudo-effective-source-generation**: Generate sections without base points on the original variety solely from pseudo-effectivity of its adjoint. -> Good-model existence places semiampleness on a birational model; the source-generation theorem requires nefness.
- **anti-use-boundary-components-without-gluing**: Apply the signed criterion after establishing generation separately on normalized boundary components. -> Componentwise sections need compatibility across the conductor to give semiampleness on the whole reduced boundary.
- **anti-use-uniform-generation-index**: Treat existential semiampleness as a uniform bound for the Cartier multiple or an algorithmic running time. -> The source permits the trivializing multiple to depend on the pair and supplies no uniform index.
- **anti-use-global-analytic-multiple**: Use a single generated Cartier multiple across an entire noncompact analytic base. -> The relative result chooses a neighborhood and multiple for the specified compact subset.
- **anti-use-arbitrary-dlt-restriction**: Infer restriction surjectivity to the reduced floor for every nef dlt adjoint without a supported effective representative. -> The extension consequence retains containment of the reduced floor in the support of the effective representative.
- **anti-use-finite-data-as-finite-certificate**: Treat a finite collection of numerical checks in positive characteristic as a certificate of the full Frobenius incompatibility theorem. -> Its proof uses geometric witnesses, spreading, slope estimates, and an asymptotic contradiction for sufficiently large residual characteristics.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result asserts that some positive Cartier multiple is generated by global sections, without providing an effective procedure or uniform index. The signed-boundary proof lifts through every infinitesimal neighborhood and algebraizes formal deformations. The finite-data Frobenius theorem fixes geometric witnesses but reaches its contradiction only for sufficiently large residual characteristics; it is not a finite constructive decision procedure. A finite executable test would therefore check an auxiliary specialization rather than certify these geometric existence and asymptotic results.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Semiampleness guarantees the existence of a globally generated Cartier multiple, but supplies no effective bound or procedure for finding that multiple and its sections. This does not define an executable software contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The projective subvarieties constructed by formal deformation belong to a birational existence argument. The paper supplies no computable evolution law, geometric sampling procedure, or simulation update rule. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite generation of rounded log canonical rings can guide symbolic algebra research, but the statement does not provide generators, a generator degree bound, or an extraction procedure. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The special-termination result concerns geometric programs under lower-dimensional good-model hypotheses. It does not give a finite input encoding, executable selection rule for successive contractions, or running-time bound. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The truncated symmetric algebra and diagonal filtration are mathematical objects used to compare ranks and sections. Their definition does not specify a storage representation or computational operations for general geometric inputs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Frobenius rank calculation uses reduction of fixed algebraic data to large residual characteristic. It provides no hardware construction, parallel execution schedule, or accelerator arithmetic contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finiteness of normal covers follows from bounded degree and a fixed branch complement. The argument provides no computational hardness assumption, key construction, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The slope estimate controls graded cotangent bundles on a fixed projective curve. It does not construct a learning objective, estimator, training procedure, or predictive guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The base-component bounds constrain algebraic intersections under singularity and local multiplicity hypotheses. They do not define an optimisation problem with encoded feasible inputs and a method for computing an optimum. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Whole-boundary semiampleness is obtained through normalization and conductor gluing of geometric sections. This compatibility argument supplies no message protocol, replicated state model, or distributed consistency guarantee. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper directly contributes to algebraic geometry, complex geometry, and the topology of projective varieties. Its good-model and fundamental-group conclusions are theoretical existence and classification results, without a finite constructive engineering contract. |

          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be a projective $\\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety of dimension $d$, let $P$ be a nef rational Cartier divisor, and let $k>0$ be an integer for which $kP$ is Cartier. <a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-main-field-and-pair-scope** (hypothesis): Main abundance requires a normal projective log canonical pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, with effective rational boundary and rational Cartier adjoint. Blocked use: ['Apply main abundance over positive-characteristic or non-algebraically closed fields without a separate theorem.', 'Apply normal abundance to arbitrary nonnormal pairs or singularities worse than log canonical.']
- **limit-nefness-on-original-model** (hypothesis): Generation on the original variety requires nefness there. Pseudo-effective adjoints obtain semiampleness on a good model under the stated good-model theorem. Blocked use: ['Infer basepoint-free generation on the starting variety solely from pseudo-effectivity.']
- **limit-inductive-premises** (hypothesis): The intermediate smooth nonvanishing and good-model implications retain their lower-dimensional hypotheses until the simultaneous induction supplies them. Blocked use: ['Use an intermediate inductive proposition without its lower-dimensional good-model premise.', 'Make same-dimensional good-model existence an input to the smooth nonvanishing step.']
- **limit-subadditivity-boundaries-and-fibers** (formal_scope): The subadditivity input concerns connected-fiber morphisms between smooth connected projective complex varieties, reduced simple normal crossing boundaries, the specified support inclusion, and a very general smooth fiber. Blocked use: ['Replace the stated boundaries with fractional or orbifold boundaries.', 'Transfer the displayed complex fiber statement to arithmetic or nonprojective settings without a separate result.']
- **limit-signed-whole-boundary** (hypothesis): The signed criterion retains nefness, pseudo-effectivity after a positive boundary subtraction, a boundary-supported rational-linear representative, and semiampleness on the whole reduced boundary. Blocked use: ['Apply signed abundance using only componentwise generation without conductor compatibility.', 'Drop the pseudo-effective boundary-subtraction condition or replace rational-linear equivalence with numerical equivalence.']
- **limit-perturbation-fixed-cartier-index** (hypothesis): The trivial-perturbation argument uses the specified rational perturbation range and one fixed Cartier index that persists on every model. Blocked use: ['Choose a perturbation without checking its stated ceiling.', 'Allow the Cartier index to change while reusing the original perturbation estimate.']
- **limit-root-construction-map-extension** (formal_scope): The constructed morphism to the smooth target is defined on the reduced divisor. The construction extends its etale charts to nilpotent thickenings and does not assert an ambient extension of that morphism. Blocked use: ['Assume a preexisting morphism from all infinitesimal neighborhoods to the smooth target.']
- **limit-formal-lifting-category** (formal_scope): Formal transitions are surjective as sheaf maps of abelian groups. Global-section surjectivity is asserted on affine charts, with compatibility under further etale chart changes. Blocked use: ['Treat the transition statement as asserted linearity over an ambient target structure sheaf.', 'Infer global-section surjectivity on arbitrary nonaffine charts.']
- **limit-hodge-vanishing-support** (hypothesis): Negative-twist vanishing requires a compatible mixed Hodge-module system in perverse degree zero whose support is finite over the root gerbe, and retains the stated twist and cohomological-degree conditions. Blocked use: ['Apply the vanishing to arbitrary support or unrelated coherent complexes.', 'Remove the positivity of the twist parameter or the negative-degree restriction.']
- **limit-null-family-noneffective-selection** (nonconstructive): The null-family argument algebraizes compatible formal deformations and selects a dominating family using uncountability. It supplies no effective family-search bound. Blocked use: ['Use the dominating-family existence proof as a finite procedure for enumerating or sampling its members.']
- **limit-vertical-descent-fiber-bound** (hypothesis): Vertical divisor descent requires relative nefness, a smooth projective base, geometrically connected integral generic fiber, and the stated bound on every fiber dimension. Blocked use: ['Descend arbitrary vertical divisors across fibrations with larger special fibers.', 'Replace relative nefness with verticality alone.']
- **limit-counterexample-exclusion-scope** (counterexample_scope): The irregularity, general-type, canonical-current, and bounded-curve exclusions operate within the hypothetical smooth nonvanishing counterexample and its specified birational and scaling models. Blocked use: ['Assert irregularity zero for all smooth projective varieties with pseudo-effective canonical class.', 'Exclude bounded covering curves on arbitrary terminal varieties.']
- **limit-fixed-current-valuation-acc** (hypothesis): The valuation ascending chain condition fixes both the positive current and the discrepancy ceiling. The exact-zero arguments also require bounded contacts and a fixed numerical degree after passage to a subsequence. Blocked use: ['Claim uniform ascending chain control while varying the current.', 'Convert a vanishing limiting gap into exact equality without the fixed-current and bounded-contact conditions.']
- **limit-base-component-local-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The base-component estimates require an actual nonzero linear subsystem, a proper base locus, isolation at the generic point, smoothness there, and ordinary high-order ideal containment. The canonical estimate concerns positive-dimensional centers. Blocked use: ['Apply the degree estimate to an arbitrary subvariety without an isolated primary base ideal.', 'Apply the canonical estimate to a zero-dimensional center.']
- **limit-full-kernels-and-exceptional-positivity** (hypothesis): Moving multiplicity uses entire jet kernels and the stated level-gap condition. Positivity of the component's top intersection additionally requires meeting the isomorphism open of the big semiample contraction. Blocked use: ['Substitute arbitrary selected jet subspaces for the full moving kernels.', 'Assign positive top intersection to every exceptional subvariety.']
- **limit-fiber-restriction-locality** (formal_scope): Fiber restriction preserves local support and ordinary-power containment at a suitable general incidence point. Projectivity, rational factoriality, klt singularities, and generic smoothness of the ambient fiber component require separate verification. Blocked use: ['Apply subadjunction to a restricted fiber solely because the restriction lemma applies.', 'Infer nonzero restriction and isolation on arbitrary special fibers.']
- **limit-cover-finiteness-branch-complement** (hypothesis): A degree bound gives finite cover lists only with the stated finite-type family and nonsweeping divisorial branch images. The complement and lists can depend on the fixed family parameters. Blocked use: ['Infer finiteness of branched covers from bounded degree alone.', 'Assume a common branch complement or cover list across all scaling parameters.']
- **limit-jets-very-general-and-choice-order** (hypothesis): The scalar and bundle jet estimates concern very general points and sufficiently small scaling parameters. The normalization is fixed before the bundle weight, and the scaling parameter is chosen afterward. Blocked use: ['Transfer the jet estimates to every point, including exceptional loci.', 'Choose the scaling threshold independently of the fixed bundle weight.']
- **limit-frobenius-fixed-geometric-witnesses** (hypothesis): The Frobenius incompatibility theorem requires actual fixed flag, jet-section, and birational complete-intersection witnesses. Its reduction uses these fixed data rather than specialization of a characteristic-zero effective cone. Blocked use: ['Replace the geometric witnesses with numerical values alone.', 'Treat positive-characteristic reductions as a positive-characteristic abundance theorem.']
- **limit-asymptotic-frobenius-cutoff** (approximation): The diagonal section and determinant-order comparison applies for sufficiently large residual characteristic, with errors controlled by fixed data. A bounded numerical experiment does not certify the full asymptotic theorem. Blocked use: ['Declare full Frobenius incompatibility certified from agreement at selected residual characteristics.', 'Replace asymptotic errors with exact values without independent bounds.']
- **limit-real-boundary-versus-rational-generation** (formal_scope): Real-boundary good-model existence is recorded over the complex numbers with real semiampleness interpreted by an ample real pullback. Main Cartier generation and rounded-ring finite generation retain rational boundaries. Blocked use: ['Infer an integral generated Cartier multiple for arbitrary real adjoints.', 'Apply the rounded-ring finite-generation statements to arbitrary real boundaries.']
- **limit-no-uniform-generation-or-generator-degree** (nonconstructive): The trivializing Cartier multiple may depend on the pair, and no uniform index or generator-degree bound is asserted. Blocked use: ['Extract a uniform search cutoff for generated multiples.', 'Predict section-ring generator degrees or computational complexity from finite generation alone.']
- **limit-supported-dlt-extension** (hypothesis): Dlt restriction surjectivity retains the supported effective rational representative and the stated range of Cartier multiples. Blocked use: ['Infer arbitrary boundary restriction surjectivity for every nef dlt adjoint.', "Drop containment of the floor in the representative's support."]
- **limit-fundamental-group-euler-condition** (hypothesis): The fundamental-group finiteness consequence requires a positive-dimensional smooth connected projective complex variety with zero Kodaira dimension and nonzero holomorphic Euler characteristic. Blocked use: ['Infer finite fundamental group from zero Kodaira dimension alone.', 'Treat the finiteness theorem as an algorithm computing the group or its order.']
- **limit-cover-classification-holomorphic-scope** (formal_scope): The cover classification concerns analytic covering spaces of normal projective complex varieties. Local triviality is holomorphic, and the simply connected fiber condition belongs to the universal-cover assertion. Blocked use: ['Infer an algebraic deck action or a global product after finite cover.', 'Transfer the simply connected fiber condition to the general infinite-cover assertion.']
- **limit-analytic-compact-neighborhood** (formal_scope): Relative analytic generation is local near each compact base subset, with a multiple that may depend on that subset. Nefness remains required over the entire base, and projectivity cannot be replaced by mere properness or a Kähler hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Assert a single generated multiple over an entire noncompact base.', 'Check nefness only over the selected compact subset.', 'Extend the result to real-boundary or semi-log-canonical analytic pairs.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the rational-boundary log abundance conjecture in every dimension over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero: every nef ℚ-Cartier log canonical divisor on a projective log canonical pair with effective rational boundary is semiample. Over ℂ, the proof also establishes canonical nonvanishing for smooth projective varieties in every dimension.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $(X,B)$ be a projective log canonical pair with rational boundary. Its log canonical divisor $K_X+B$ records the canonical class together with the boundary. A rational Cartier divisor is *nef* if its degree on every curve is nonnegative. It is *semiample* if a positive Cartier multiple is generated by global sections. Such a multiple defines a morphism, so semiampleness turns numerical positivity into a fibration described by sections. The log abundance conjecture asks whether a nef log canonical divisor is semiample. We give a positive answer in characteristic zero.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Log abundance). *Let $(X,B)$ be a normal projective log canonical pair over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Assume that $B$ is an effective rational divisor and $K_X+B$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. If $K_X+B$ is nef, then some positive Cartier multiple of it is generated by global sections.*

</div>

The proof proceeds by dimension induction over $\mathbb{C}$. Its inductive assertion is stronger than the displayed theorem: every projective lc pair with effective real boundary and pseudo-effective real Cartier adjoint has a good log minimal model, on which the adjoint is semiample. Smooth canonical nonvanishing asks whether a pseudo-effective canonical class on a smooth variety has a nonzero pluricanonical section. We establish this statement in each dimension before that good-model assertion is used in the same dimension. At the end, descent of the evaluation map of an actual Cartier line bundle gives the theorem over every algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.

We write $\kappa(L)$ for the Iitaka dimension of a rational divisor $L$, with value $-\infty$ if all positive integral systems are empty. The consequences fall into three groups. Good models, rational-linear triviality when $\kappa=0$, and gluing and extension across boundaries are treated in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">11</a>. Finite generation of the algebras of rounded pluricanonical sections is proved in Section <a href="#sec:canonical-rings" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:canonical-rings">12</a>, both absolutely in characteristic zero and for proper morphisms over algebraic bases over $\mathbb{C}$. Section <a href="#sec:classification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:classification">13</a> relates canonical nonvanishing to rational curves, cotangent tensors, fundamental groups, and quasi-projective covers. These sections give the full statements, additional hypotheses, and derivations from the work of Fujino–Gongyo, Boucksom–Demailly–Păun–Peternell, Lazić–Peternell, Brunebarbe–Campana, Campana, Taji, and Claudon–Höring–Kollár.

There is also a relative analytic application of Fujino’s theorem: Section <a href="#sec:analytic-abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analytic-abundance">14</a> treats projective surjective morphisms of normal complex analytic varieties with rational lc adjoint nef over the whole base. It obtains a relatively generated Cartier multiple near each compact base subset; the multiple may depend on that subset. This is distinct from the compact Kähler symmetric-pluriform criterion in Section <a href="#sec:classification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:classification">13</a>; the cotangent-invariant and fundamental-group consequences there remain projective.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Historical development

The threefold proofs show why nonvanishing and boundary geometry are central to abundance. Miyaoka proved the numerical-dimension-one case by studying an effective pluricanonical divisor and its infinitesimal neighborhoods after suitable coverings (Miyaoka 1988) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Miyaoka1988](#ref-Miyaoka1988)]. Kawamata proved the remaining numerical-dimension-two case for minimal threefolds (Kawamata 1992, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Kawamata1992](#ref-Kawamata1992)]. His Section 4 gives an alternative proof of Miyaoka’s case through finite covers, Hodge theory and compatible infinitesimal deformations. Keel, Matsuki and McKernan established the logarithmic theorem (Keel, Matsuki, and McKernan 1994, 2004; Matsuki 2003) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[KeelMatsukiMcKernan1994](#ref-KeelMatsukiMcKernan1994), [Matsuki2003Correction](#ref-Matsuki2003Correction), [KeelMatsukiMcKernan2004Correction](#ref-KeelMatsukiMcKernan2004Correction)]. Fujino extended abundance to semi-log-canonical threefolds (Fujino 2000) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Fujino2000](#ref-Fujino2000)], where sections on the normalization must agree along the conductor before they define sections on the original space. This compatibility is also needed in higher-dimensional induction: the coefficient-one boundary can be reducible even when the ambient variety is normal.

In higher dimensions, Birkar, Cascini, Hacon and McKernan established minimal models for klt pairs in the big-boundary or log-general-type range (Birkar et al. 2010) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[BCHM2010](#ref-BCHM2010)]. Their finite-generation theorem for projective rational klt pairs does not require bigness (Birkar et al. 2010, Corollary 1.1.2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[BCHM2010](#ref-BCHM2010)]. The rational lc finite-generation consequence below goes beyond that klt setting. Two distinctions remain essential on the boundary of the positive cone. First, nonvanishing produces sections but does not by itself assert that a nef adjoint is semiample. Hashizume reduces real-boundary log canonical nonvanishing and ordinary minimal-model existence through dimension $n$ to smooth canonical nonvanishing in dimension $n$ (Hashizume 2018, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Hashizume2018](#ref-Hashizume2018)]. Our induction must then make these models good. For positive Kodaira dimension, the required step is supplied by lower-dimensional good models (Fujino and Gongyo 2017, Lemma 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[FujinoGongyoRings2014](#ref-FujinoGongyoRings2014)]. Second, Kodaira dimension zero and numerical dimension zero are different hypotheses. Gongyo proves that a numerically trivial rational log canonical adjoint is $\mathbb{Q}$-linearly trivial (Gongyo 2013, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Gongyo2013](#ref-Gongyo2013)]. In the zero-Iitaka argument below, numerical triviality forces an already available effective representative to vanish.

Recent work of Liu and Xu treats numerical dimension at most one. Their Theorem 5.1 gives good minimal models for projective log canonical pairs of dimension at most five with nonnegative invariant Kodaira dimension and numerical dimension at most one; their Theorem 5.4 gives a reduction in that numerical range to smooth nonvanishing (Liu and Xu 2025) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[LiuXu2025](#ref-LiuXu2025)]. The induction here has no numerical-dimension restriction. The ordinary-pair specialization of the companion generalized-minimal-model paper (OpenAI 2026a, Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[OpenAIMinimalModels2026](#ref-OpenAIMinimalModels2026)] concerns existence of a nef model. It is not an input to this good-model induction. After abundance has been proved, we use that ordinary-pair result in the absolute finite-generation argument of Section <a href="#sec:canonical-rings" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:canonical-rings">12</a>.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The two tasks in the induction

Assuming good models in dimensions below $n$, we first prove smooth canonical nonvanishing in dimension $n$, and then make the resulting log minimal models good. Both tasks use a criterion for a *signed* representative on a reduced boundary. The lower-dimensional hypothesis supplies its whole-boundary semiampleness assumption. We describe this common ingredient first. For a nef divisor $L$, write $\nu(L)=\max\{j:L^jH^{n-j}>0\}$, where $H$ is ample and $n=\dim X$. The criterion is $$\begin{gathered}
 L=K_X+D\text{ nef},\qquad L-cD\text{ pseudo-effective }(c>0),\\
 L\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}\sum_i a_iD_i,\qquad L|_D\text{ semiample}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad\kappa(L)=\nu(L).
 \end{gathered}$$ Here $(X,D)$ is projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial dlt, and $D=\sum_iD_i$ is the decomposition of the reduced boundary into its prime components. The rational coefficients $a_i$ may be positive, negative, or zero. Semiampleness is required on the entire reduced scheme $D$. Normalization and conductor gluing therefore belong to the induction, as in the slc interface of Fujino–Gongyo (Fujino and Gongyo 2014a, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014)]. For the good-model task, positive Kodaira dimension is handled by lower-dimensional good models; the signed criterion addresses the remaining zero-Iitaka-dimension case in Proposition <a href="#prop:inductive-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inductive-reduction">7</a>.

The signed criterion is proved geometrically. A generated multiple of $L|_D$ defines a morphism from $D$ to projective space. Over a general point of the image of a positive-coefficient component, a root construction separates that component from the negative and coefficient-zero boundary. A filtered Hodge-module calculation lifts local parameters on this image, together with a parameter transverse to the lifted boundary, through all infinitesimal neighborhoods. Fixing the lifted base parameters and varying the transverse one deforms a boundary fiber into a projective subvariety disjoint from $D$. Varying the fiber gives a dominating family on which $L$ is trivial. Section <a href="#sg:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sg:descent">5</a> explains the final geometric step: these subvarieties bound the dimension of the nef reduction, and vertical descent identifies $L$ with the pullback of a big divisor on its base.

This formal deformation method has a direct precedent in Miyaoka’s construction; Miyaoka credits Reid with the suggestion to analyze a pluricanonical divisor (Miyaoka 1988, 220) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Miyaoka1988](#ref-Miyaoka1988)]. Boundary extension provides a second related approach. Demailly, Hacon and Păun use a singular-metric refinement of Ohsawa–Takegoshi extension to extend pluricanonical sections in the purely log terminal setting (Demailly, Hacon, and Păun 2013, Theorem 1.7 and Corollary 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[DemaillyHaconPaun2013](#ref-DemaillyHaconPaun2013)]. Their nef corollary requires an effective representative whose support contains the coefficient-one divisor and lies in the boundary. Chan and Choi subsequently removed the containment of that representative in the boundary (Chan and Choi 2021, Theorem 1.6.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[ChanChoi2021](#ref-ChanChoi2021)]. Here the representative may have both signs, and compatibility is required across the whole reducible boundary. We therefore construct compatible formal lifts on the whole reduced boundary. Semiampleness on that boundary is supplied by the normalization theorem of Fujino and Gongyo (Fujino and Gongyo 2014a, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014)].

The construction combines familiar tools with an additional compatibility argument. Normalized cyclic covers and their eigenspaces follow the formalism of Esnault and Viehweg (Esnault and Viehweg 1992, vol. 20, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[EsnaultViehweg1992](#ref-EsnaultViehweg1992)]. Deligne’s logarithmic Hodge theory supplies the underlying framework (Deligne 1971) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Deligne1971](#ref-Deligne1971)]. On the singular boundary, we use Saito’s projective strictness and graded de Rham vanishing, together with his comparison with the Du Bois complex (Saito 1990, Theorem 2.14 and Proposition 2.33) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Saito1990](#ref-Saito1990)] <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Saito2000](#ref-Saito2000)] (Saito 2000, Theorem 0.2 and Corollary 0.3). The proof must still identify the obstruction to lifting and show that these ordinary-variety results apply to the covering construction.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Smooth nonvanishing.

To prove smooth nonvanishing, suppose instead that a counterexample exists. The zero-boundary case of logarithmic Iitaka subadditivity, stated below as Theorem <a href="#asm:iitaka" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="asm:iitaka">2</a>, first excludes positive irregularity through the Albanese map. This is its only use in the proof. The geometric preparations rule out two ways it could be covered by lower-dimensional subvarieties: families that carry a positive current pulled back from a smaller space, and covering families of curves with bounded genus and bounded degree after normalizing the nef divisors used in the construction to a fixed small positive volume. The first exclusion uses positive currents and algebraic webs. The second uses an ascending chain condition for the singularity multiplicities of one fixed current, measured by Lelong numbers at valuations of bounded discrepancy. The signed criterion has a second role here: it forces the full reduced support of any signed canonical representative to have big logarithmic adjoint, as proved in Proposition <a href="#prop:signed-alternative" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:signed-alternative">25</a>. This will control the multisections in the two-slot construction below.

These exclusions produce two incompatible estimates for vanishing at a moving point. At a very general point, the first estimate bounds the vanishing order of every nonzero section of a Cartier multiple $kP$ of a suitable nef divisor $P$ by $k$ times a small normalized constant. To obtain sections with stronger jet separation, we use the projective bundle associated to the sum of the two pullbacks of a line bundle to the product of a smooth model with itself. Its two summands allow the argument to move in either factor.

Failure of the required jet separation would produce a moving base component. Slice estimates and the curve exclusions leave only components that project generically finitely onto both base factors, hence give correspondences between them. Their branch divisors cannot sweep either factor; a fixed branch complement and bounded degree therefore leave only finitely many covers. After fixing the covers, the correspondences give a family of birational maps whose graphs dominate their product. Hanamura’s birational-group theorem then makes a fixed cover birational to an abelian variety (Hanamura 1988, Theorems 2.1–2.2) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Hanamura1988](#ref-Hanamura1988)]. The ramification and positive-current argument in Section <a href="#sec:exclusions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exclusions">6</a> would give a pluricanonical section on the counterexample, again a contradiction.

Once the projective bundle, its divisors, and the required finite collection of jet sections are fixed, reduction modulo a large prime gives an evaluation map on the product of two copies of the Frobenius-twisted model. The map has full generic rank. Its restriction to the ordinary diagonal is controlled by sections on the Frobenius thickening of the diagonal, where the two relative Frobenius maps agree. A filtration of those sections gives a much smaller rank bound there. The determinant is a section of an external product of two line bundles. The first estimate, expressed by a fixed movable curve on a blowup, bounds vanishing in each factor and hence the order of the determinant at the selected diagonal point. The rank deficit gives a larger lower bound on that order, a contradiction.

Theorem <a href="#common:finite-data-frobenius" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:finite-data-frobenius">35</a> states this incompatibility for a fixed smooth variety, fixed jet sections, and a fixed movable-curve bound. Its proof opens Section <a href="#fr:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fr:section">9</a> and uses neither abundance nor subadditivity. The geometric application follows in the same section, using the fixed inputs supplied by the preceding curve exclusions and jet constructions. The numerical subadjunction and moving-kernel results needed for those constructions are proved in Section <a href="#sec:common-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:common-tools">7</a>.

The numerical part combines two established methods. The argument that follows a moving base component uses differentiation in the parameter space, as in Ein–Küchle–Lazarsfeld (Ein, Küchle, and Lazarsfeld 1995, Proposition 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[EinKuchleLazarsfeld1995](#ref-EinKuchleLazarsfeld1995)]; we give the tracking and adjunction estimates needed here. After reduction to positive characteristic, we compare ordinary jets with Frobenius jets using the local containment recorded by Mustaţă–Schwede (Mustaţă and Schwede 2014, Equation (2.8)) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[MustataSchwede2014](#ref-MustataSchwede2014)]. The resulting evaluation map is studied through Sun’s canonical Frobenius filtration (Sun 2008, Theorem 3.7) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Sun2008](#ref-Sun2008)], whose curve case is treated by Joshi–Ramanan–Xia–Yu (Joshi et al. 2006, sec. 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[JoshiRamananXiaYu2006](#ref-JoshiRamananXiaYu2006)]. We use its description by powers of the diagonal ideal due to Kitadai–Sumihiro (Kitadai and Sumihiro 2008, Definition 3.1 and Remark 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[KitadaiSumihiro2008](#ref-KitadaiSumihiro2008)]. Langer’s instability estimate (Langer 2004, Corollary 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Langer2004](#ref-Langer2004)] then controls the slopes of the graded bundles.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The subadditivity input

The all-dimensional argument uses the following completed companion result. Its application occurs only in Lemma <a href="#ex:irregularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ex:irregularity">16</a>.

<a id="stmt-asm-iitaka"></a>
<div id="asm:iitaka" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Logarithmic Iitaka subadditivity, (OpenAI 2026b, Corollary 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[OpenAIIitaka2026](#ref-OpenAIIitaka2026)]). *Let $f:X\to Y$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers between smooth connected projective complex varieties. Let $D_X,D_Y$ be reduced effective simple normal crossing divisors, allowing zero boundaries, such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}(f^*D_Y)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}(D_X).$$ For a very general smooth fiber $F$, put $D_F=D_X|_F$. Then $$\kappa(X,K_X+D_X)\ge
 \kappa(F,K_F+D_F)+\kappa(Y,K_Y+D_Y).$$ Here $D_F$ is reduced with simple normal crossings and $K_F+D_F\sim(K_X+D_X)|_F$. The convention $(-\infty)+b=-\infty$ applies also when $b=-\infty$, and the zero divisor on a point has Iitaka dimension zero.*

</div>

The support inclusion allows additional components in $D_X$, and the morphism need not be smooth away from the boundary. The theorem has no nefness, abundance, bigness, or good-model hypothesis. Its zero-boundary case is used in the Albanese reduction of Section <a href="#sec:exclusions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exclusions">6</a>; this is the sole subadditivity input in our proof. The separate characteristic-zero specialization (OpenAI 2026b, Corollary 6.3) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[OpenAIIitaka2026](#ref-OpenAIIitaka2026)] uses a geometric generic fiber. We keep that field-and-fiber statement distinct from the complex, very-general-fiber statement above. No fractional-boundary, orbifold, nonprojective, Hodge-conjectural, or arithmetic extension is assumed here.

The particular zero-boundary application in Lemma <a href="#ex:irregularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ex:irregularity">16</a> also follows from the external maximal-Albanese-dimension theorem of Hacon, Popa and Schnell (Hacon, Popa, and Schnell 2017, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[HaconPopaSchnell2017](#ref-HaconPopaSchnell2017)]: the smooth base there maps generically finitely to a subvariety of an abelian variety. Cao and Păun prove the underlying abelian-base case (Cao and Păun 2016, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[CaoPaunAbelian2016](#ref-CaoPaunAbelian2016)]. These results cover that application, not the full logarithmic statement above.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### Reading order.

Section <a href="#sec:induction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:induction">2</a> sets out the dimension induction. Sections <a href="#sg:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sg:section">3</a>–<a href="#sg:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sg:descent">5</a> prove the signed-boundary criterion: first the root and adjunction construction, then infinitesimal lifting, and finally compact fibers and descent. Section <a href="#sec:exclusions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:exclusions">6</a> derives the geometric exclusions for a hypothetical smooth counterexample. After the local tools of Section <a href="#sec:common-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:common-tools">7</a>, Section <a href="#sec:jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:jets">8</a> constructs its scalar and two-slot jets. Section <a href="#fr:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fr:section">9</a> proves the independent Frobenius comparison and then constructs its fixed witnesses to establish smooth nonvanishing. Sections <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">10</a> and <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">11</a> finish the induction, descend actual global generation, and record good models, linear triviality, semi-log-canonical abundance, and supported dlt extension. Section <a href="#sec:canonical-rings" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:canonical-rings">12</a> then proves finite generation of rational log canonical rings in the absolute and complex proper relative settings. Section <a href="#sec:classification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:classification">13</a> combines canonical nonvanishing and abundance with classical results on rational curves, cotangent tensors, fundamental groups, and quasi-projective covers. Section <a href="#sec:analytic-abundance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analytic-abundance">14</a> records relative analytic abundance near compact subsets of the base for projective morphisms. A reader using only the finite-data comparison can start with Theorem <a href="#common:finite-data-frobenius" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:finite-data-frobenius">35</a> and its proof in Section <a href="#fr:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fr:section">9</a>; neither assumes the counterexample geometry.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Conventions

A variety is integral. Canonical divisors are chosen compatibly on birational models. For a divisor $E$ on a smooth model $p:W\to X$, our log discrepancy is $$a(E;X,B)=1+\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E\bigl(K_W-p^*(K_X+B)\bigr).$$ Log canonicity means nonnegative log discrepancies, and klt means strictly positive log discrepancies. We use the usual dlt and slc conventions. For non-nef pseudo-effective divisors, statements about numerical dimension use Nakayama’s $\kappa_\sigma$, as specified in the relevant reduction. These notions are not interchanged without a hypothesis that justifies doing so.

For nonempty systems, the Iitaka dimension is the maximum dimension of their images. Numerical and rational-linear equivalence are denoted by $\equiv$ and $\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}$, respectively. Unless a different base field is specified, the constructions are over $\mathbb{C}$. A very general point avoids a countable union of proper closed subvarieties. The transfer to arbitrary algebraically closed characteristic-zero fields is made only after the complex argument has been completed.

<a id="sec:induction"></a>
### The inductive framework
