The underlying ordinary/Frobenius ideal comparison is also recorded in (Mustaţă and Schwede 2014, proof of Proposition 2.12, Equation (2.8)) <a id="pm-cite-78"></a>[[MustataSchwede2014](#ref-MustataSchwede2014)]. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-51"></a>
##### Full rank off the diagonal

<a id="common:two-slot-evaluation"></a>
Let $F:W\to W'$ be relative Frobenius to the base-field twist. It is finite flat because $W$ is smooth. Write $S'$ for the twist of $S$, so $F^*S'=pS$, and define $$\mathcal E=F_*\mathcal{O}_W(B_p),\quad s=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}\mathcal E=p^n,\quad R=s^2,
 \qquad U_a=H^0(W,\mathcal{O}_W(B_p+paS)).$$ Projection formula gives an evaluation map on $W'\times W'$: $$\label{common:two-slot-evaluation}
 \bigoplus_{\substack{a+b=q\\a,b\ge0}}
 U_a\otimes U_b\otimes
 \mathcal{O}(-aS'_1-bS'_2)
 \longrightarrow \mathcal E\boxtimes\mathcal E.$$

<a id="stmt-common-generic-rank"></a>
<div id="common:generic-rank" class="lemma">

**Lemma 37**. *The map in Equation <a href="#common:two-slot-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:two-slot-evaluation">[common:two-slot-evaluation]</a> has generic rank $R$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-common-generic-rank"><summary>Proof of stmt-common-generic-rank</summary>

*Proof.* Trivialize the two summands defining $Z$ near the selected base points. Let $z$ be the torus coordinate, whose value at $z_*$ is $z_0\ne0$. By the projective-bundle formula, the sections in Lemma <a href="#common:positive-characteristic-jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:positive-characteristic-jets">36</a> are weight polynomials in $z$ with weight-$j$ coefficients in $$H^0(W,B_p+jS)\otimes
 H^0(W,B_p+(pq-j)S),\qquad 0\le j\le pq.$$ Let $A_*$ be the tensor product of the local algebras of the two base Frobenius fibers. The local quotient in Lemma <a href="#common:positive-characteristic-jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:positive-characteristic-jets">36</a> is $$A_*[z]/(z^p-z_0^p),$$ free over $A_*$ with basis $1,z,\ldots,z^{p-1}$. Project to the coefficient of $1$ in this basis. Exactly the weights $j=pa$ survive, with multipliers $z_0^{pa}$. Their coefficients are the values of the corresponding summands of Equation <a href="#common:two-slot-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:two-slot-evaluation">[common:two-slot-evaluation]</a>, in the chosen frames and the induced twisted frames. As the full jet map is surjective, these coefficients span $A_*$, of dimension $p^{2n}=R$. Thus the evaluation has full rank at this pair of base points, and hence generically. The calculation uses a basis over the possibly nonreduced algebra $A_*$, so reducedness of the Frobenius fibers is unnecessary. ◻

</details>

The jet calculation at $z_*$ proves generic full rank; the diagonal estimate below will be tested at $(x',x')$, obtained from the separately fixed point $x$.

<a id="pm-h-52"></a>
##### The diagonal filtration

<a id="common:diagonal-rank-upper"></a>
The Frobenius fiber product has the cartesian square <a id="common:frobenius-square"></a>$$\label{common:frobenius-square}
\begin{tikzcd}
 \Delta_F=W\times_{W'}W \arrow[r,"\mathrm{pr}_2"] \arrow[d,"\mathrm{pr}_1"'] & W \arrow[d,"F"]\\
 W \arrow[r,"F"'] & W'.
\end{tikzcd}$$ Its reduced subscheme is the diagonal $W$. Put $$\mathcal L_p=
 \mathcal{O}_{\Delta_F}(B_{p,1}+B_{p,2}+pqS_1).$$ Write $h=F\circ\mathrm{pr}_1=F\circ\mathrm{pr}_2:
\Delta_F\to W'$. On this fiber product, $$\mathcal{O}(pS_1)\simeq h^*\mathcal{O}_{W'}(S')\simeq\mathcal{O}(pS_2).$$ Consequently every pair of weights $a+b=q$ gives the same line bundle: $$\mathcal{O}_{\Delta_F}(B_{p,1}+B_{p,2}+paS_1+pbS_2)
 \simeq\mathcal L_p.$$ On the diagonal of $W'\times W'$, every source twist of Equation <a href="#common:two-slot-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:two-slot-evaluation">[common:two-slot-evaluation]</a> becomes $-qS'$. Finite base change and projection formula identify the twisted target as $$(\mathcal E\otimes\mathcal E)(qS')\simeq h_*\mathcal L_p.$$ The product sections defining the columns restrict to global sections of this one bundle $\mathcal L_p$. Their values at a diagonal point therefore lie in the image of $H^0(\Delta_F,\mathcal L_p)$ in the corresponding fiber of $h_*\mathcal L_p$. This proves that the rank at every diagonal point is at most $$\label{common:diagonal-rank-upper}
 h^0(\Delta_F,\mathcal L_p).$$

<a id="common:diagonal-graded"></a>
<a id="common:Frob-pullback-graded"></a>
For a rank-$n$ bundle $V$ in characteristic $p$, denote by $A_j(V)$ the degree-$j$ part of its symmetric algebra modulo the $p$-th powers of local generators. This construction is independent of frame: the $p$-th power of a linear combination is the sum of the $p$-th powers in characteristic $p$. Each $A_j(V)$ is locally free and a quotient of $V^{\otimes j}$. Set $$u=n(p-1),\qquad e_j=\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}A_j(V),\qquad
 \sum_{j=0}^u e_j=p^n.$$ Filtering $\mathcal L_p$ by powers of the ideal of the reduced diagonal in $\Delta_F$ gives the graded bundles $$\label{common:diagonal-graded}
 \mathcal G_j=\mathcal{O}_W(2B_p+pqS)\otimes A_j(\Omega_W^1),
 \qquad 0\le j\le u.$$ Indeed, in smooth local coordinates the algebra is generated by parameter differences with their $p$-th powers zero, and its degree-one conormal is $\Omega_W^1$. This coordinate calculation can be made étale locally or in completions and identifies the global associated graded. This is the diagonal-ideal form of the canonical Frobenius filtration (Kitadai and Sumihiro 2008, Corollary 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-79"></a>[[KitadaiSumihiro2008](#ref-KitadaiSumihiro2008)]. The same calculation, using only the bundle from the second factor, filters $F^*\mathcal E$ with pieces (Sun 2008, Theorem 3.7) <a id="pm-cite-80"></a>[[Sun2008](#ref-Sun2008)] $$\label{common:Frob-pullback-graded}
 \mathcal{O}_W(B_p)\otimes A_j(\Omega_W^1).$$

<a id="common:socle-pairing"></a>
Complementary multiplication is a perfect pairing, giving $$\label{common:socle-pairing}
 A_{u-j}(V)\simeq A_j(V)^\vee\otimes(\det V)^{p-1}.$$ On monomials, each exponent vector pairs with its complement to $(p-1,\ldots,p-1)$. The top line transforms by the character $(\det V)^{p-1}$: this is immediate on diagonal matrices and hence identifies the character of $\mathrm{GL}_n$ on that line.

<a id="pm-h-53"></a>
##### One slope bound and one flag polynomial

We bound the sections of every graded bundle in Equation <a href="#common:diagonal-graded" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:diagonal-graded">[common:diagonal-graded]</a> by its rank times the same scalar polynomial in $p$. This uniformity lets us sum the ranks of the graded pieces without an additional factor for their number. We begin with a slope bound on the fixed curve; here slope means ordinary degree divided by rank.

<a id="stmt-common-truncated-slope"></a>
<div id="common:truncated-slope" class="lemma">

**Lemma 38**. *There is a constant $c\ge0$, independent of the sufficiently large residual characteristic $p$ and of $j$, such that for $V=\Omega_W^1|_C$ one has $$\mu_{\max}(A_j(V))\le(p-1)K_W\cdot C+nc.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-common-truncated-slope"><summary>Proof of stmt-common-truncated-slope</summary>

*Proof.* Write $F_C$ for absolute Frobenius of $C$. Langer’s instability estimate (Langer 2004, Corollary 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-81"></a>[[Langer2004](#ref-Langer2004)] gives <a id="common:Langer-min"></a>$$\label{common:Langer-min}
 L_{\min}(V):=
 \lim_{e\to\infty}p^{-e}\mu_{\min}(F_C^{e*}V)
 \ge-\frac{c}{p-1}.$$ To check the uniform constant, the discrepancy from $\mu_{\min}(V)\ge0$ is at most $(\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}V-1)\deg A_C/(p-1)$, where $A_C$ is a nef line bundle such that $T_C\otimes A_C$ is globally generated. Its degree can be bounded in terms of the fixed genus, using a sufficiently positive line bundle. Here the rank is $n$, irrespective of the fact that the base is a curve.

<a id="common:tensor-min"></a>
We also need, for every integer $i\ge0$, $$\label{common:tensor-min}
 \mu_{\min}(V^{\otimes i})\ge iL_{\min}(V).$$ Fix $p$ and $i$. Choose a line bundle $A_e$ of degree $-\mu_{\min}(F_C^{e*}V)+O(1)$, rounded up with a fixed genus-dependent additive constant, so that $F_C^{e*}V\otimes A_e$ is globally generated. This follows from Serre duality: after subtracting any point, its minimum slope can be made greater than $2g(C)-2$, which annihilates $H^1$ and makes evaluation at that point surjective. For every vector-bundle quotient $Q$ of $V^{\otimes i}$, the bundle $F_C^{e*}Q\otimes A_e^{\otimes i}$ is then globally generated. Consequently $$p^e\mu(Q)+i\deg A_e\ge0.$$ Divide by $p^e$ and let $e\to\infty$ to prove Equation <a href="#common:tensor-min" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:tensor-min">[common:tensor-min]</a>. This limit is taken for each fixed $p,i$; there is no interchange of Frobenius-iteration and characteristic limits.

Since $A_{u-j}(V)$ is a quotient of $V^{\otimes(u-j)}$, the perfect pairing Equation <a href="#common:socle-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:socle-pairing">[common:socle-pairing]</a> yields $$\begin{aligned}
 \mu_{\max}(A_j(V))
 &= (p-1)\deg\det V-\mu_{\min}(A_{u-j}(V))\\
 &\le(p-1)K_W\cdot C+\frac{(u-j)c}{p-1}\\
 &\le(p-1)K_W\cdot C+nc.
\end{aligned}$$ ◻

</details>

The divisors $B_p-pL=T_0-(p-k_0N_p)L$ range over a fixed finite list. Combining Equation <a href="#common:intersection-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:intersection-bounds">[common:intersection-bounds]</a>, Equation <a href="#common:flag-degrees" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:flag-degrees">[common:flag-degrees]</a>, Lemma <a href="#common:truncated-slope" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:truncated-slope">38</a> therefore gives, uniformly for $0\le j\le u$, <a id="common:common-slope-bound"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \mu_{\max}(\mathcal G_j|_C)
 &\le p(4+2/r)\Lambda H^n+O(1)\notag\\
 &\le M_p:=7p\Lambda H^n+c_0,\label{common:common-slope-bound}
\end{aligned}$$ where $c_0\ge0$ is fixed. The coefficient $7$ provides harmless room in this common bound.

<a id="stmt-common-diagonal-sections"></a>
<div id="common:diagonal-sections" class="lemma">

**Lemma 39**. *For all sufficiently large $p$, $$h^0(\Delta_F,\mathcal L_p)
 \le R\bigl(7^nH^n+O(1/p)\bigr)<R/4.$$ The error constant is independent of the filtration index $j$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-common-diagonal-sections"><summary>Proof of stmt-common-diagonal-sections</summary>

<a id="common:flag-lattice-sum"></a>
*Proof.* Write the fixed flag as $$W=W_n\supset W_{n-1}\supset\cdots\supset W_1=C,
 \qquad W_{n-k}\in|h_k|_{W_{n-k+1}}
 \quad(1\le k\le n-1).$$ At step $k$, fix the preceding nonnegative integers $b_1,\ldots,b_{k-1}$ and put $$\mathcal F_k=
 \mathcal G_j|_{W_{n-k+1}}
 \Bigl(-\sum_{i<k}b_i h_i\Bigr).$$ For each $b_k\ge0$, the divisor restriction sequence is $$0\longrightarrow\mathcal F_k(-(b_k+1)h_k)
 \longrightarrow\mathcal F_k(-b_kh_k)
 \longrightarrow
 \mathcal F_k(-b_kh_k)|_{W_{n-k}}
 \longrightarrow0.$$ Iterating it for $b_k=0,1,\ldots$ and then proceeding to the next member of the flag bounds the section space by $$\label{common:flag-lattice-sum}
 h^0(W,\mathcal G_j)\le
 \sum_{b_1,\ldots,b_{n-1}\ge0}
 h^0\left(C,\mathcal G_j|_C\Bigl(-\sum_i b_i h_i|_C\Bigr)\right).$$ At each stage the remainder is zero after sufficiently negative ample twisting. Its stopping index need not be uniform in $j,p$ or in the preceding twists: enlarging the nonnegative finite sums to the displayed lattice sum preserves the inequality.

<a id="common:common-flag-polynomial"></a>
A summand in Equation <a href="#common:flag-lattice-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:flag-lattice-sum">[common:flag-lattice-sum]</a> vanishes when $\sum_i b_i(h_i\cdot C)>M_p$, because then its maximum slope is negative. Each nonzero summand has at most $e_j(M_p+1)$ sections. Indeed, evaluation at $\lfloor M_p\rfloor+1$ distinct points is injective; the kernel has negative maximum slope after subtracting those points. By Equation <a href="#common:flag-degrees" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:flag-degrees">[common:flag-degrees]</a> it follows that $$\begin{aligned}
 h^0(W,\mathcal G_j)
 &\le e_j(M_p+1)
       \prod_{i=1}^{n-1}
       \left(1+\frac{M_p}{l_i\Lambda H^n}\right)\notag\\
 &=e_jp^n\bigl(7^nH^n+O(1/p)\bigr).
 \label{common:common-flag-polynomial}
\end{aligned}$$ The leading coefficient follows from $\Lambda=\prod_i l_i$. More importantly, the whole scalar polynomial on the first line is the same for every $j$.

Sum over the filtration in Equation <a href="#common:diagonal-graded" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:diagonal-graded">[common:diagonal-graded]</a>. The ranks satisfy $\sum_j e_j=p^n$, so Equation <a href="#common:common-flag-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:common-flag-polynomial">[common:common-flag-polynomial]</a> gives the asserted bound with $R=p^{2n}$. There is no extra factor for the number of graded pieces. Finally $$7^nH^n\le(14\epsilon)^n<1/4$$ by Equation <a href="#common:intersection-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:intersection-bounds">[common:intersection-bounds]</a>, proving the strict inequality for all large $p$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-54"></a>
##### The determinant has incompatible orders

Choose $R$ generically independent columns of Equation <a href="#common:two-slot-evaluation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:two-slot-evaluation">[common:two-slot-evaluation]</a> from bases of its source vector spaces. Their determinant is a nonzero section $$0\ne\sigma\in H^0(W'\times W',\mathcal Q_1\boxtimes\mathcal Q_2)$$ of an actual external-product line bundle. To specify its factors, let $m_i$ be the sum of the weights of the $R$ chosen columns in slot $i$. The target determinant is $(\det\mathcal E)^s\boxtimes(\det\mathcal E)^s$, and the chosen source determinant is $\mathcal{O}(-m_1S')\boxtimes\mathcal{O}(-m_2S')$. Thus $$\mathcal Q_i=(\det\mathcal E)^{\otimes s}
                 \otimes\mathcal{O}_{W'}(m_iS'),\qquad
 \lambda_i=m_i/R.$$ Every column weight lies between $0$ and $q$, so $0\le\lambda_i\le q$ independently of the columns chosen.

<a id="common:slot-determinant-class"></a>
Under the numerical identification with the base-field twist, $$\label{common:slot-determinant-class}
 \frac{c_1(\mathcal Q_i)}R
 =\frac{c_1(\mathcal E)}s+\lambda_iS
 =\frac{B_p}p+\frac{p-1}{2p}K_W+\lambda_iS,
 \qquad 0\le\lambda_i\le q.$$ The Frobenius first-Chern-class identity is also (Sun 2008, Lemma 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-82"></a>[[Sun2008](#ref-Sun2008)]; it is an identity of rational divisor classes. For the second equality, use Equation <a href="#common:Frob-pullback-graded" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:Frob-pullback-graded">[common:Frob-pullback-graded]</a>. Complementary degrees in Equation <a href="#common:socle-pairing" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:socle-pairing">[common:socle-pairing]</a> have total first Chern class $e_j(p-1)K_W$. Summing gives $$\sum_jc_1(A_j(\Omega_W^1))=\frac{s(p-1)}2K_W.$$ Thus $c_1(F^*\mathcal E)=sB_p+s(p-1)K_W/2$, and Frobenius pullback of twisted divisor classes multiplies by $p$, as required.

For $0\le\lambda\le q$, the fixed movable inequality gives $$(L+K_W/2+\lambda S)\cdot\gamma\le40\epsilon J\cdot\gamma,$$ where pullback by $\pi_x$ is understood. This concerns only intersections of fixed divisors and the fixed complete-intersection witness, so it remains true on the reduction. The class in Equation <a href="#common:slot-determinant-class" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:slot-determinant-class">[common:slot-determinant-class]</a> differs from its left-hand divisor at $\lambda=\lambda_i$ by $$\frac1p\bigl(B_p-pL-K_W/2\bigr).$$ Its numerator belongs to the fixed finite list $T_0-aL-K_W/2$, $0\le a<k_0$. The resulting error after intersection with $\gamma$ is therefore $O(p^{-1})$, uniformly over the selected determinant columns.

<a id="common:slot-order"></a>
Let $x'$ be the twisted point and let $0\ne\tau_i\in
H^0(W',\mathcal Q_i)$. The strict transform of its effective divisor on the blowup at $x'$ pairs nonnegatively with the twist of $\gamma$. Since $J\cdot\gamma>0$, it follows that $$\label{common:slot-order}
 \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{x'}(\tau_i)}R\le40\epsilon+O(1/p).$$ The constant is uniform in the columns and in $\tau_i$. This argument tests sections on the reduction itself; it does not require them to lift to characteristic zero.

<a id="common:determinant-order-upper"></a>
Künneth identifies the space containing $\sigma$ with $H^0(W',\mathcal Q_1)\otimes H^0(W',\mathcal Q_2)$. Choose bases adapted to the orders of vanishing at $x'$ in each factor. In each degree their leading terms are linearly independent. Tensor products of these leading terms remain independent in each bidegree of the two disjoint sets of parameters; terms of different bidegrees cannot cancel. Every nonzero tensor consequently has order at most the sum of the two maximum basis orders. Applying Equation <a href="#common:slot-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:slot-order">[common:slot-order]</a>, we obtain $$\label{common:determinant-order-upper}
 \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{(x',x')}\sigma\le R\bigl(80\epsilon+O(1/p)\bigr).$$

<a id="common:determinant-order-lower"></a>
On the other hand, Equation <a href="#common:diagonal-rank-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:diagonal-rank-upper">[common:diagonal-rank-upper]</a>, Lemma <a href="#common:diagonal-sections" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:diagonal-sections">39</a> bound the matrix rank at $(x',x')$ by a number strictly smaller than $R/4$. Trivialize its line bundles near that point. Invertible constant row operations make more than $3R/4$ rows vanish at the point. Each entry of those rows belongs to the maximal ideal, so every term of the determinant has order at least $3R/4$. Therefore $$\label{common:determinant-order-lower}
 \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_{(x',x')}\sigma\ge3R/4.$$ Equations <a href="#common:determinant-order-upper" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:determinant-order-upper">[common:determinant-order-upper]</a> and <a href="#common:determinant-order-lower" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="common:determinant-order-lower">[common:determinant-order-lower]</a> contradict $80\epsilon<3/4$ for sufficiently large $p$.

This contradiction proves Theorem <a href="#common:finite-data-frobenius" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:finite-data-frobenius">35</a>. The full-rank calculation used $z_*$, while the diagonal estimate holds at every point and was tested at the separately chosen $x$. The errors were controlled by one fixed finite list of divisor classes and one fixed flag polynomial. Thus their constants do not depend on $p$, the filtration index, or the chosen columns.

<a id="fr:geometric-witnesses"></a>
#### The geometric witnesses and smooth nonvanishing

We return to the notation and the hypothetical counterexample of Section <a href="#sec:jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:jets">8</a>. Theorem <a href="#common:finite-data-frobenius" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:finite-data-frobenius">35</a> asks for fixed divisors, a small polarization and flag, finite two-slot jets, and one actual strongly movable curve. We construct all these inputs in characteristic zero. The positive parts on the scaling models, rather than the original non-nef classes, provide the small polarization. No minimal-model statement or pseudo-effective cone is specialized to positive characteristic.

<a id="stmt-thm-smooth-nonvanishing"></a>
<div id="thm:smooth-nonvanishing" class="theorem">

**Theorem 40** (The smooth nonvanishing step). *Assume the lower-dimensional real-boundary good-model hypothesis of Assumption <a href="#mmp:lower-models" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mmp:lower-models">3</a>. If $X$ is a smooth projective complex variety of dimension $n$ and $K_X$ is pseudo-effective, then $\kappa(X,K_X)\geq0$.*

</div>

<a id="fr:epsilon"></a>
In dimension zero the assertion is immediate. On a smooth curve, pseudo-effectivity of $K_X$ gives $g(X)\geq1$, hence $h^0(X,K_X)=g(X)>0$. We therefore suppose $n\geq2$ and argue by contradiction. We use the late terminal model $Y$, the divisors $K=K_Y$ and $A=A_Y$, and the scaling models of the preceding section. Fix $m>0$ with $mK$ Cartier, and write $$\mu_t=\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(K_X+tA_X)^{1/n},\qquad
 L=r(K+tA).$$ As before, $r$ is a positive integer chosen so that $r\mu_t\longrightarrow\epsilon$ as $t\downarrow0$. Choose the rational number $\epsilon>0$ sufficiently small for Propositions <a href="#prop:scalar-jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:scalar-jets">32</a> and <a href="#prop:large-seshadri" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:large-seshadri">34</a>, and also so that $$\label{fr:epsilon}
 (14\epsilon)^n<\frac14,\qquad
 80\epsilon<\frac34.$$ The strict inequalities leave room for the errors that tend to zero with the characteristic. We next fix a sufficiently large integer $q$ as in Proposition <a href="#prop:large-seshadri" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:large-seshadri">34</a>, and then choose a positive rational $t$ small enough for the estimates below and for $r>qm+1$. Once those estimates hold, $t$ and $r$ remain fixed through all the subsequent characteristic-zero constructions and reductions.

<a id="pm-h-56"></a>
##### A small polarization and a cotangent flag

A small ample polarization and a complete-intersection flag will control the number of sections on the Frobenius thickening of the diagonal. We construct them from the positive part on a scaling model.

<a id="fr:intersection-bounds"></a>
Choose a smooth common resolution $W$ of $Y$ and the scaling model for $K+tA$. In this application $K,A,L$ also denote their pullbacks to $W$, and $$K_W=K+E,\qquad E\geq0.$$ Let $H_0$ be the pullback of the nef semiample positive part $N_0$ of $L$ on its scaling model. The comparison of canonical pullbacks and moving parts gives <a id="fr:limit-intersections"></a>$$\label{fr:limit-intersections}
 H_0^n\longrightarrow\epsilon^n,\qquad
 LH_0^{n-1}=H_0^n,\qquad
 K_WH_0^{n-1}=KH_0^{n-1}\leq\frac{H_0^n}{r}.$$ To justify these intersections, recall that the maps $Y\dashrightarrow Y_t$ are small by the choice of the late model. Every divisor exceptional over $Y$ is therefore also exceptional over $Y_t$. The differences discarded when passing to the moving parts are exceptional over the scaling model, and the transform of $A$ is effective up to rational linear equivalence. Intersecting with $H_0^{n-1}$ gives the displayed equalities and inequality. In particular, since $qm/r<1$, $$(2L+qmK)H_0^{n-1}
 \le(2+qm/r)H_0^n<3H_0^n.$$ Choose $t$ small enough that also $H_0^n<(2\epsilon)^n$, and now fix $t,r$ and the resolution $W$. For a fixed ample divisor $H_{\mathrm{amp}}$, put $H=H_0+\eta H_{\mathrm{amp}}$, where $\eta>0$ is rational. The strict margins just obtained, together with $K_WH_0^{n-1}\le H_0^n/r$, allow us to choose $\eta$ small enough that continuity gives $$\label{fr:intersection-bounds}
 H^n\leq(2\epsilon)^n,\qquad
 K_WH^{n-1}\leq\frac{2H^n}{r},\qquad
 (2L+qmK)H^{n-1}\leq4H^n.$$

<a id="fr:generic-nef"></a>
We next choose the flag for the section estimates. The canonical divisor of $W$ is pseudo-effective. Generic semipositivity, applied with empty boundary (Campana and Păun 2015, Theorem 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-83"></a>[[CampanaPaun2015](#ref-CampanaPaun2015)], and restriction to a sufficiently general complete-intersection curve give the following fixed data. Apply restriction to the Harder–Narasimhan filtration and its factors (Mehta and Ramanathan 1982, Theorem 6.1 and Remark 6.2) <a id="pm-cite-84"></a>[[MehtaRamanathan1982](#ref-MehtaRamanathan1982)]. Choose successively large divisible integers $l_i$ such that $h_i=l_iH$ are integral very ample classes, and choose a smooth integral complete-intersection flag $$W=W_n\supset W_{n-1}\supset\cdots\supset W_1=C,
 \qquad W_{i-1}\in |h_{n-i+1}|_{W_i},$$ for which $$\label{fr:generic-nef}
 \mu_{\min}(\Omega_W^1|_C)\geq0.$$ Here and below slopes on $C$ mean degree divided by rank. One may obtain the flag by applying restriction to the finitely many Harder–Narasimhan quotients of $\Omega_W^1$.

<a id="pm-h-57"></a>
##### A movable witness for all determinant weights

For the vanishing-order estimate, choose a very general point $x\in W$, away from the exceptional loci, and let $\pi_x:\widehat W\to W$ be its blowup, with exceptional divisor $J$. Set $$D^+=2r(K+2tA)+2E.$$ This big divisor has the scalar sections controlled by Proposition <a href="#prop:scalar-jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:scalar-jets">32</a>: pushing to $Y$ identifies the section spaces, since the added exceptional part does not change them. If $\rho=\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(2r(K+2tA))^{1/n}$, then $$\rho\leq4r\mu_t\leq8\epsilon$$ for our sufficiently small $t$. Thus every section of a sufficiently divisible multiple of $D^+$ has normalized order at $x$ at most $4\rho\leq32\epsilon$.

We convert this order bound into a curve inequality. It follows that $\pi_x^*D^+-40\epsilon J$ is not pseudo-effective. Otherwise its convex combination with the big class $\pi_x^*D^+$ would make $\pi_x^*D^+-cJ$ big for some rational $32\epsilon<c<40\epsilon$, giving a section of excessive order. Movable-curve duality (Boucksom et al. 2013, Theorem 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-85"></a>[[BDPP2013](#ref-BDPP2013)] therefore supplies an actual covering curve class $\gamma$ on $\widehat W$ such that <a id="fr:curve-test"></a>$$\label{fr:curve-test}
 (\pi_x^*D^+)\cdot\gamma
       <40\epsilon\,J\cdot\gamma,\qquad J\cdot\gamma>0.$$ The strictness lets us fix an algebraic family that will survive reduction. More precisely, the negative pairing can first be detected by a strongly movable curve: take the pushforward of a general complete intersection of very ample divisors on one fixed smooth birational model of $\widehat W$. Fix this model, its divisors, and the resulting covering family. Thus the choice consists of finite algebraic data, rather than a limiting real movable class. Positivity of $J\cdot\gamma$ follows from the strict inequality and pseudo-effectivity of $\pi_x^*D^+$.

To turn this curve into the witness required by Theorem <a href="#common:finite-data-frobenius" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:finite-data-frobenius">35</a>, set $S=mK$ on $W$; it is an integral Cartier divisor. We verify all its weight inequalities before proceeding to the jet construction. Put $Q_\lambda=L+K_W/2+\lambda S$ for $0\le\lambda\le q$. The equality $K_W=K+E$ gives <a id="fr:slot-domination"></a>$$\label{fr:slot-domination}
 D^+-Q_\lambda
 =(r-\tfrac12-\lambda m)K+3rtA+\tfrac32E.$$ This class is pseudo-effective in characteristic zero: $r>qm+1$, $K$ is pseudo-effective, and $A,E$ are effective up to the stated equivalences. Pairing with the fixed strongly movable class therefore gives $$\pi_x^*Q_\lambda\cdot\gamma
 \le \pi_x^*D^+\cdot\gamma
 <40\epsilon J\cdot\gamma,
 \qquad J\cdot\gamma>0.$$ These are the required movable inequalities. Only the two endpoint inequalities need to be retained as finite data, since the pairing is affine in $\lambda$. The common theorem spreads these fixed numerical inequalities and the actual birational complete-intersection witness; it does not spread the pseudo-effective assertion used to obtain them.

<a id="pm-h-58"></a>
##### A finite two-slot jet system

We must similarly realize the two-slot jet estimate by finitely many sections before reducing. Their products will then supply the jet orders needed for arbitrarily large characteristics.

On $W\times W$, let $$Z=\mathbb{P}(mK_1\oplus mK_2),\qquad
 \xi=\mathcal{O}_Z(1),\qquad
 M=L_1+L_2+q\xi,$$ with the symmetric-power convention for the projective bundle. Proposition <a href="#prop:large-seshadri" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:large-seshadri">34</a> gives a semiample big model whose Seshadri constant exceeds $2n+2$ at a very general torus point. On the open set where its birational contraction is an isomorphism, the jet interpretation of the Seshadri constant consequently gives an integer $k_0>0$ such that $|k_0M|$ generates jets of order at least $(2n+2)k_0$ at a fixed smooth torus point $z_*\in Z$. Indeed, on the ample model choose a rational number $c$ strictly between $2n+2$ and its Seshadri constant. On the blowup of the selected smooth point, the pullback of the ample class minus $c$ times the exceptional divisor is ample. Serre vanishing and the exceptional-divisor sequence then give jets of order $kc-1$ for all sufficiently divisible large $k$. Pulling sections back on the isomorphic open gives the asserted bound. Enlarge $k_0$ to clear every denominator, in particular that of $L$, and fix finitely many sections realizing this jet surjection.

<a id="pm-h-59"></a>
##### Applying the comparison

All the preceding choices are now fixed. With $S=mK$, the dimension, intersection and flag hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#common:finite-data-frobenius" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="common:finite-data-frobenius">35</a> are Equations <a href="#fr:epsilon" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:epsilon">[fr:epsilon]</a>, <a href="#fr:intersection-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:intersection-bounds">[fr:intersection-bounds]</a>, and <a href="#fr:generic-nef" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fr:generic-nef">[fr:generic-nef]</a>. The curve construction gives its movable witness and both endpoint inequalities, and the finite jet system gives the required surjection at $z_*$. This point need not lie over the separately chosen testing point $x$. The proof of the common theorem also fixes its auxiliary divisor $T_0$ and finitely many filler sections before choosing any residual characteristic.

The common theorem now rules out these fixed data. Its contradiction compares two orders of the same nonzero determinant. After reduction, the Frobenius direct image $\mathcal E$ used there has rank $p^n$, so $\mathcal E\boxtimes\mathcal E$ has rank $R=p^{2n}$. The fixed jets give full evaluation rank $R$, while the small polarization bounds the rank on the diagonal by $R/4$. Thus a nonzero maximal minor vanishes to order at least $3R/4$ at $(x',x')$, where $x'$ is the Frobenius image of the testing point $x$. Its line bundle is an actual external product. Applying the fixed curve inequality to each factor bounds the same order above by $R(80\epsilon+O(p^{-1}))$, a contradiction for large $p$. The theorem proves these determinant estimates for the fixed witnesses we have now supplied; it requires no additional geometric input here.

All constants are fixed before the residual prime varies. The contradiction excludes the assumed counterexample and proves Theorem <a href="#thm:smooth-nonvanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:smooth-nonvanishing">40</a>.

<a id="sec:completion"></a>
### Completion and change of ground field

Smooth nonvanishing is now available in the order required by the induction. We first finish the complex proof, including descent from the good model to the original normal variety. We then descend the globally generated Cartier multiple to an arbitrary algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.

<details id="proof-stmt-thm-smooth-nonvanishing"><summary>Proof of stmt-thm-smooth-nonvanishing</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> over $\mathbb{C}$.* Use dimension induction in the real-boundary category of Proposition <a href="#prop:inductive-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inductive-reduction">7</a>. In dimension zero the variety is a point. Suppose the good-model assertion holds in all smaller dimensions. The signed-representative argument (Theorem <a href="#thm:signed" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:signed">8</a>), the exclusions, and the jet estimates are then available with exactly that lower-dimensional hypothesis. Theorem <a href="#thm:smooth-nonvanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:smooth-nonvanishing">40</a> establishes smooth nonvanishing in the present dimension. Proposition <a href="#prop:inductive-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inductive-reduction">7</a> therefore proves the real-boundary good-model assertion in the present dimension. This is precisely the hypothesis needed to continue to the next dimension.

Apply this to the rational lc pair $(X,B)$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Let $f:(X_d,B_d)\to(X,B)$ be a crepant dlt model and let $(V,B_V)$ be its good log minimal model. Take a common resolution $a:W\to X_d$, $q:W\to V$, and put $p=f\circ a$. Then $$p^*(K_X+B)=a^*(K_{X_d}+B_d)=q^*(K_V+B_V).$$ The first equality is crepancy. The second is the nef comparison in Lemma <a href="#mmp:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mmp:comparison">4</a>, since $K_{X_d}+B_d=f^*(K_X+B)$ is nef. The right side is semiample because $(V,B_V)$ is a good model, so the pullback of $K_X+B$ is semiample. Normality gives $p_*\mathcal{O}_W=\mathcal{O}_X$. Choose a sufficiently divisible Cartier multiple $m(K_X+B)$ whose pullback is globally generated. The projection formula identifies its sections with those of its pullback. Surjectivity of evaluation upstairs implies surjectivity downstairs: otherwise a base point downstairs would make every pulled-back section vanish on its nonempty fiber. Hence $K_X+B$ is semiample. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-field-descent"></a>
<div id="prop:field-descent" class="proposition">

**Proposition 41** (Change of algebraically closed field). *The conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> over $\mathbb{C}$ implies its conclusion over every algebraically closed field of characteristic zero.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-field-descent"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-field-descent</summary>

*Proof.* Let $(X,B)$ be defined over such a field $k$. Choose a finitely generated subfield $k_1\subset k$ over which the projective variety, the rational boundary, a Cartier multiple of its adjoint, and a log resolution are all defined. After enlarging $k_1$ if necessary, these data base change to the given data. Let $k_0$ be its algebraic closure inside $k$, and denote the resulting pair by $(X_0,B_0)$. Choose an embedding $k_0\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}$, and write $(X_{\mathbb{C}},B_{\mathbb{C}})$ for the complex base change.

The chosen log resolution tests log canonicity after either algebraically closed field extension: its boundary remains simple normal crossing and all discrepancy coefficients remain unchanged. The descended pair is consequently lc over both $k_0$ and $\mathbb{C}$.

Nefness is also invariant under such extensions. Indeed, a curve over an extension is represented by a point of a relative Hilbert scheme after its finite defining data have been spread over a finite-type parameter scheme. The degree of a fixed line bundle is constant in the resulting flat family. Specializing to a closed point over the algebraically closed smaller field preserves a negative degree, if one existed; some irreducible component of the specialized curve would then have negative degree. This contradicts nefness over that field. Conversely, curves over the smaller field remain available after extension. Apply the converse to $k_0\subset k$: nefness of $K_X+B$ gives nefness of $K_{X_0}+B_0$. Apply the forward direction to $k_0\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}$: its complex base change is nef as well.

The complex case supplies an integer $m>0$, enlarged to a multiple of the Cartier index fixed over $k_0$, for which the Cartier line bundle $M=\mathcal{O}_{X_0}(m(K_{X_0}+B_0))$ becomes globally generated over $\mathbb{C}$. Proper flat base change for sections identifies $$H^0(X_0,M)\otimes_{k_0}\mathbb{C}
   = H^0(X_{\mathbb{C}},M_{\mathbb{C}}).$$ Tensoring the evaluation map for $M$ with $\mathbb{C}$ therefore gives the surjective complex evaluation map. Its coherent cokernel is zero by faithful flatness. Extending from $k_0$ to $k$ preserves this surjectivity, so the same integer $m$ gives a globally generated line bundle on the original $X$. ◻

</details>

Together with Proposition <a href="#prop:field-descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:field-descent">41</a>, the complex proof establishes Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> in its stated generality.

<a id="sec:consequences"></a>
### Good models, linear triviality, and boundary consequences

The real-boundary induction and the final line-bundle descent have different natural scopes. We first record good-model existence over $\mathbb{C}$, including the precise smooth canonical comparison used by fiber-space applications. We then deduce actual rational-linear triviality in Iitaka dimension zero over every field covered by Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Finally, over $\mathbb{C}$, normalization gluing and a section-extension theorem give two consequences for reducible boundaries and nonnormal pairs.

<a id="stmt-thm-good-models-complex"></a>
<div id="thm:good-models-complex" class="theorem">

**Theorem 42** (Real-boundary good models over $\mathbb{C}$). *Every projective log canonical pair $(X,B)$ over $\mathbb{C}$, with effective real boundary and pseudo-effective real Cartier adjoint $K_X+B$, has a good log minimal model. Real semiampleness has the meaning fixed in Section <a href="#sec:induction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:induction">2</a>.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-thm-good-models-complex"><summary>Proof of stmt-thm-good-models-complex</summary>

*Proof.* The simultaneous induction in Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">10</a> starts with dimension zero and proves the full real-boundary conclusion of Proposition <a href="#prop:inductive-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inductive-reduction">7</a> at each dimension. In particular, its conclusion applies to the given pair before any rational-boundary or nefness specialization. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-smooth-good-model"></a>
<div id="cor:smooth-good-model" class="corollary">

**Corollary 43** (Smooth canonical good models). *Let $X$ be a smooth projective complex variety with pseudo-effective $K_X$. There is a normal projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial klt variety $V$ and a $K_X$-negative birational contraction $X\dashrightarrow V$ such that $K_V$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier and semiample. On a common smooth resolution $p:W\to X$, $q:W\to V$, compatible canonical divisors satisfy $$p^*K_X=q^*K_V+E,
 \qquad E\geq0\quad\text{and }E\text{ is }q\text{-exceptional}.$$ In particular $\kappa(X,K_X)=\kappa(V,K_V)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-smooth-good-model"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-smooth-good-model</summary>

*Proof.* Apply Theorem <a href="#thm:good-models-complex" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:good-models-complex">42</a> to the klt pair $(X,0)$. Choose its $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial log minimal model, as in the construction of Proposition <a href="#prop:inductive-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inductive-reduction">7</a>. The klt clause of Lemma <a href="#mmp:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mmp:comparison">4</a> shows that it extracts no divisor, remains klt, and has the displayed effective exceptional comparison. Its boundary is zero, being the strict transform of the zero boundary with no extracted divisors. Discrepancies strictly improve at contracted divisors, which is the $K_X$-negative condition; semiampleness is the good-model conclusion. For sufficiently divisible $m>0$, the effective $q$-exceptional divisor $mE$ satisfies $q_*\mathcal{O}_W(mE)=\mathcal{O}_V$, since $V$ is normal. Projection formula and the displayed comparison identify the pluricanonical section spaces. Their ratios agree in the common function field, proving the assertion about Iitaka dimension. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-semiample-zero"></a>
<div id="lem:semiample-zero" class="lemma">

**Lemma 44**. *Let $Z$ be a nonempty integral projective variety over an algebraically closed field, and let $A$ be a semiample $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier divisor. If $\kappa(Z,A)=0$, then $A\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-semiample-zero"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-semiample-zero</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a positive integer $m$ such that the line bundle $L=\mathcal{O}_Z(mA)$ is globally generated. Its complete system gives $$\phi:Z\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}(H^0(Z,L)^*),\qquad
 L\simeq\phi^*\mathcal{O}(1).$$ The image has dimension at most $\kappa(Z,A)=0$, so it is a point. A linear form nonzero at that point pulls back to a nowhere-vanishing section of $L$. Thus $L\simeq\mathcal{O}_Z$, an actual line-bundle isomorphism, and $A\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-qlinear-zero"></a>
<div id="cor:qlinear-zero" class="corollary">

**Corollary 45**. *Let $(X,B)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. If $\kappa(X,K_X+B)=0$, then $K_X+B\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}0$. In particular, every nef rational adjoint of Iitaka dimension zero on a projective klt fivefold over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero is $\mathbb{Q}$-linearly trivial.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-qlinear-zero"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-qlinear-zero</summary>

*Proof.* Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> gives semiampleness on the original normal variety. Lemma <a href="#lem:semiample-zero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:semiample-zero">44</a> gives a positive Cartier multiple with trivial associated line bundle. Klt pairs are lc, so the fivefold assertion is a special case. ◻

</details>

Thus the nef adjoint also has numerical dimension zero. This is a consequence of the trivializing Cartier multiple, not an additional hypothesis. The multiple may depend on the pair; no uniform index is asserted.

<a id="pm-h-62"></a>
#### Semi-log-canonical abundance and dlt extension

We use the standard semi-log-canonical convention, in which the underlying projective scheme may be reducible or nonnormal. It is reduced, satisfies $S_2$, is pure-dimensional, and has ordinary double crossings in codimension one. No component of the effective boundary is contained in the codimension-one singular locus. If $\nu:X^\nu\to X$ is the normalization, the boundary $\Theta$ defined by $$K_{X^\nu}+\Theta=\nu^*(K_X+\Delta)$$ includes the conductor boundary, and $(X^\nu,\Theta)$ is componentwise log canonical. These are the conventions of (Fujino and Gongyo 2014a, Definition 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-86"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014)].

<a id="stmt-cor-slc-abundance"></a>
<div id="cor:slc-abundance" class="corollary">

**Corollary 46** (Semi-log-canonical abundance over $\mathbb{C}$). *Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a projective semi-log-canonical pair over $\mathbb{C}$, where $\Delta$ is an effective rational divisor and $K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. If $K_X+\Delta$ is nef, then it is semiample.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-slc-abundance"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-slc-abundance</summary>

*Proof.* Write $X^\nu=\coprod_i X_i^\nu$ for the normalization and put $\Theta_i=\Theta|_{X_i^\nu}$. Each $(X_i^\nu,\Theta_i)$ is a normal projective log canonical pair with effective rational boundary. The adjoint $K_{X_i^\nu}+\Theta_i$ is the pullback of $K_X+\Delta$, so it is nef. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> makes each of these adjoints semiample. There are only finitely many components, hence one common positive Cartier multiple is globally generated on their disjoint union. Thus $\nu^*(K_X+\Delta)$ is semiample. The normalization gluing theorem (Fujino and Gongyo 2014a, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-87"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014)] now gives semiampleness on $X$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-dlt-extension"></a>
<div id="cor:dlt-extension" class="corollary">

**Corollary 47** (Supported extension for dlt pairs over $\mathbb{C}$). *Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a projective dlt pair over $\mathbb{C}$, where $\Delta$ is an effective rational divisor, and put $S=\lfloor\Delta\rfloor$. Assume that $K_X+\Delta$ is nef and that, for an effective rational divisor $D$, $$K_X+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}D\geq0,
 \qquad S\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}D.$$ Then, for every integer $m\geq2$ for which $m(K_X+\Delta)$ is Cartier, the restriction map $$H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal{O}_X(m(K_X+\Delta))\bigr)
 \longrightarrow
 H^0\bigl(S,\mathcal{O}_X(m(K_X+\Delta))|_S\bigr)$$ is surjective.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-dlt-extension"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-dlt-extension</summary>

*Proof.* Set $B=\Delta-S$. The pair $(X,S+B)$ is dlt, $B$ is an effective rational divisor, and $\lfloor S+B\rfloor=S$. Since a dlt pair is log canonical, Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> makes its nef adjoint semiample. The pair and the displayed effective representative therefore satisfy the hypotheses of (Fujino and Gongyo 2014a, Proposition 5.12) <a id="pm-cite-88"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014)], which gives the stated restriction surjectivity for every Cartier multiple with $m\geq2$. This is the supported extension formulation in (Fujino and Gongyo 2014a, Conjecture 5.8) <a id="pm-cite-89"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014)]. ◻

</details>

The first corollary settles the rational-boundary form of the semi-log-canonical abundance conjecture (Fujino and Gongyo 2014a, Conjecture 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-90"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014)] in every dimension over $\mathbb{C}$. The second settles the supported dlt extension conjecture (Fujino and Gongyo 2014a, Conjectures 1.10 and 5.8) <a id="pm-cite-91"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014)] with the precise Cartier-multiple range supplied by Proposition 5.12. Both are applications of the cited implications after normal abundance has been established here. The support condition $S\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}D$ is retained; no arbitrary restriction-surjectivity statement is asserted.

<a id="sec:canonical-rings"></a>
### Finite generation of rational log canonical rings

The preceding results also settle finite generation for rational lc pairs. The finite-generation conjecture asks whether finitely many rounded pluricanonical sections generate the log canonical ring (Fujino and Gongyo 2017, Conjecture A) <a id="pm-cite-92"></a>[[FujinoGongyoRings2014](#ref-FujinoGongyoRings2014)]. The assertion below concerns the full rounded ring, not merely a sufficiently divisible subring, and requires no nefness assumption. For the projective absolute statement, we combine the ordinary minimal-model theorem with log abundance and keep track of every rounded degree. For the proper relative statement over $\mathbb{C}$, we apply Fujino–Gongyo’s published reduction from good minimal models. In that relative statement the morphism is only required to be proper; the source need not be projective over $\mathbb{C}$.

For an integral Weil divisor $G$ on a normal variety $X$, $\mathcal{O}_X(G)$ denotes its divisorial sheaf. For a rational divisor $D$ on $X$ and nonnegative integers $m,n$, the inclusions $\lfloor mD\rfloor+\lfloor nD\rfloor\leq\lfloor(m+n)D\rfloor$ give the usual multiplication on the section algebras below.

<div class="samepage">

<a id="stmt-cor-lc-finite-generation"></a>
<div id="cor:lc-finite-generation" class="corollary">

**Corollary 48** (Finite generation for rational lc pairs).

1.  *Let $k$ be an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, let $(X,\Delta)$ be a normal projective log canonical pair over $k$, and assume that $\Delta\geq0$ is rational and $D=K_X+\Delta$ is $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. Then $$R(X,D)=\bigoplus_{m\geq0}
     H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal{O}_X(\lfloor mD\rfloor)\bigr)$$ is a finitely generated $k$-algebra.*

2.  *Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a log canonical pair over $\mathbb{C}$, with $\Delta\geq0$ rational and $K_X+\Delta$ $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. For every proper morphism $f:X\to S$ onto an algebraic variety $S$, the relative log canonical algebra $$\mathcal R(X/S,K_X+\Delta)
     =\bigoplus_{m\geq0}f_*\mathcal{O}_X\bigl(\lfloor m(K_X+\Delta)\rfloor\bigr)$$ is a finitely generated $\mathcal{O}_S$-algebra.*

</div>

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-lc-finite-generation"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-lc-finite-generation</summary>

*Proof.* In dimension zero, $X$ is a point, and in <span class="upright">(ii)</span> so is $S$. The boundary and canonical divisor are zero, and the two algebras are $k[t]$ and $\mathcal{O}_S[t]$, respectively, with $\deg t=1$. We therefore assume $\dim X\geq1$.

We first prove <span class="upright">(i)</span>. If $D$ is not pseudo-effective, a nonzero section $s$ in degree $m>0$ would give $$\operatorname{div}(s)+mD
 =\bigl(\operatorname{div}(s)+\lfloor mD\rfloor\bigr)
   +\bigl(mD-\lfloor mD\rfloor\bigr)\geq0.$$ This would make $D$ pseudo-effective. Thus all positive graded pieces vanish in this case. Since $H^0(X,\mathcal{O}_X)=k$, we have $R(X,D)=k$.

Suppose now that $D$ is pseudo-effective. The ordinary-pair conclusion of the companion minimal-model theorem (OpenAI 2026a, Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-93"></a>[[OpenAIMinimalModels2026](#ref-OpenAIMinimalModels2026)] gives a normal projective lc pair $(Y,\Delta_Y)$ and a birational map $\phi:X\dashrightarrow Y$ extracting no divisors, with $\Delta_Y=\phi_*\Delta$ and $D_Y=K_Y+\Delta_Y$ $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier and nef. Log discrepancies do not decrease. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> makes the rational adjoint $D_Y$ semiample.

<a id="eq:ring-comparison"></a>
On a common smooth resolution $p:W\to X$, $q:W\to Y$, choose compatible canonical divisors. Then $$\label{eq:ring-comparison}
 p^*D=q^*D_Y+E,\qquad E\geq0,\qquad E\text{ is }q\text{-exceptional}.$$ Indeed, the coefficient of $E$ at a prime divisor $F$ is $a(F;Y,\Delta_Y)-a(F;X,\Delta)$, which is nonnegative by the discrepancy comparison. Suppose $F$ is not $q$-exceptional. Since $\phi$ extracts no divisors, $F$ is the strict transform of a divisor on $X$. The pushed-forward boundary gives equality of these discrepancies. This proves the asserted support.

For any normal variety $Z$, rational Cartier divisor $G$, and $m\geq0$, the nonzero global sections of $\mathcal{O}_Z(\lfloor mG\rfloor)$ are exactly the rational functions $s$ satisfying $$\operatorname{div}_Z(s)+mG\geq0.$$ This is equivalent to the usual inequality with $\lfloor mG\rfloor$ because the coefficients of $\operatorname{div}_Z(s)$ are integers. If the inequality holds on $X$, pull back its effective $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier left side to $W$ and push forward by $q$. Equation <a href="#eq:ring-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ring-comparison">[eq:ring-comparison]</a> and $q_*E=0$ give the inequality on $Y$. Conversely, pull back the inequality on $Y$, add $mE$, and push forward by $p$. Thus, inside the common function field, $$H^0\bigl(X,\mathcal{O}_X(\lfloor mD\rfloor)\bigr)
 =H^0\bigl(Y,\mathcal{O}_Y(\lfloor mD_Y\rfloor)\bigr)
 \qquad(m\geq0).$$ These identifications preserve multiplication, so they identify the full graded rings.

It remains to use semiampleness on $Y$. Choose $r>0$ such that $rD_Y$ is Cartier and globally generated. Its complete linear system defines $\psi:Y\to\mathbb{P}^N_k$ with $\mathcal{O}_Y(rD_Y)\simeq\psi^*\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^N}(1)$. Let $A=k[T_0,\ldots,T_N]$ act on $R(Y,D_Y)$ by pulling back the coordinate sections, each in degree $r$. For $0\leq i<r$, put $\mathcal F_i=\psi_*\mathcal{O}_Y(\lfloor iD_Y\rfloor)$, which is coherent on $\mathbb{P}^N_k$ because $\psi$ is proper. Since $$\lfloor(\ell r+i)D_Y\rfloor
 =\ell(rD_Y)+\lfloor iD_Y\rfloor\qquad(\ell\geq0),$$ projection formula identifies the part in degrees congruent to $i$ modulo $r$, as an $A$-module, with $$\bigoplus_{\ell\geq0}H^0\bigl(\mathbb{P}^N_k,\mathcal F_i(\ell)\bigr).$$ This is a finite $A$-module by the standard finite-generation theorem for section modules (The Stacks Project Authors 2026a, Lemma 30.14.1(5)) <a id="pm-cite-94"></a>[[StacksSectionModules](#ref-StacksSectionModules)]. There are only $r$ residue classes, so $R(Y,D_Y)$ is finite as an $A$-module and hence finitely generated as a $k$-algebra. This proves <span class="upright">(i)</span>.

For <span class="upright">(ii)</span>, set $n=\dim X$. In the notation of Fujino–Gongyo, Conjecture $C_{\leq n-1}$ asks for good minimal models of projective $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial dlt pairs with effective real boundary and pseudo-effective adjoint in dimensions at most $n-1$. Theorem <a href="#thm:good-models-complex" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:good-models-complex">42</a> supplies this statement. Its good-model convention is the one fixed in Section <a href="#sec:induction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:induction">2</a>, with a $\mathbb{Q}$-factorial dlt model as in Proposition <a href="#prop:inductive-reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inductive-reduction">7</a>. Theorem 1.1 of Fujino–Gongyo (Fujino and Gongyo 2017) <a id="pm-cite-95"></a>[[FujinoGongyoRings2014](#ref-FujinoGongyoRings2014)] therefore gives their Conjecture $B_n$: finite generation of the log canonical ring for projective rational plt pairs of dimension $n$ whose adjoint is big and whose boundary has irreducible round-down. Their relative reduction, Corollary 1.4, applies to the given rational lc pair and proper morphism onto $S$, and gives exactly <span class="upright">(ii)</span> without requiring $X$ to be projective over $\mathbb{C}$. Their Theorem 1.1 also gives <span class="upright">(i)</span> directly when $k=\mathbb{C}$. ◻

</details>

The good-model input allows real boundaries, but both finite-generation statements retain rational boundaries. In the absolute proof, rationality is what leaves finitely many residue-class section modules. The relative assertion is finite generation over the algebraic base $S$ in the complex setting; it is not a claim about rounded rings for real boundaries or about analytic bases. No uniform degree bound for generators is asserted.

<a id="sec:classification"></a>
### Rational curves, cotangent positivity, and covers

We first combine smooth canonical nonvanishing with classical results on rational curves and cotangent tensors. We then use smooth abundance to remove the abundance premise from the quasi-projective-cover results of Claudon, Höring and Kollár. Throughout this section, $\kappa(X)=\kappa(X,K_X)$. We retain the convention $\kappa(\mathrm{pt})=0$, and regard a point as rationally connected but not uniruled.

<a id="stmt-cor-uniruled-mumford"></a>
<div id="cor:uniruled-mumford" class="corollary">

**Corollary 49** (Uniruledness and Mumford’s criterion). *Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety.*

1.  *$X$ is uniruled if and only if $\kappa(X)=-\infty$.*

2.  *$X$ is rationally connected if and only if $$H^0\bigl(X,(\Omega_X^1)^{\otimes m}\bigr)=0
     \quad\text{for every integer }m>0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-uniruled-mumford"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-uniruled-mumford</summary>

*Proof.* For positive-dimensional $X$, the theorem of Boucksom–Demailly–Păun–Peternell (Boucksom et al. 2013, Corollary 0.3 and Theorem 2.6) <a id="pm-cite-96"></a>[[BDPP2013](#ref-BDPP2013)] says that $X$ is non-uniruled exactly when $K_X$ is pseudo-effective. Corollary <a href="#cor:smooth-good-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:smooth-good-model">43</a> then gives $\kappa(X)\geq0$: the semiample canonical divisor on the good model has a nonzero section in a positive multiple, and the pluricanonical section spaces agree. Conversely, a nonzero pluricanonical section makes $K_X$ pseudo-effective. This proves <span class="upright">(i)</span>.

For <span class="upright">(ii)</span>, rational connectedness forces all positive covariant tensor differentials to vanish, as recalled by Lazić and Peternell. For the converse, their Proposition 2.4 reduces Mumford’s criterion in dimension $n$ to tensor nonvanishing for smooth projective varieties of positive dimension at most $n$ whose canonical class is pseudo-effective (Lazić and Peternell 2017, Proposition 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-97"></a>[[LazicPeternell2017](#ref-LazicPeternell2017)]. If $Y$ is such a variety of dimension $d$, the preceding good-model argument supplies a nonzero section of $mK_Y$ for some integer $m>0$. Antisymmetrization embeds $\Omega_Y^d$ into $(\Omega_Y^1)^{\otimes d}$ over $\mathbb{C}$; tensoring this injection $m$ times gives the required nonzero tensor differential. This is the observation in (Lazić and Peternell 2017, Remark 2.5) <a id="pm-cite-98"></a>[[LazicPeternell2017](#ref-LazicPeternell2017)], so their reduction applies in every dimension. Both assertions for a point follow from our conventions. ◻

</details>

For a smooth connected projective complex variety $X$, let $R(X)$ be a smooth projective model of the base of its maximal rationally connected (MRC) fibration. This base is determined up to birational equivalence and is non-uniruled unless it is a point. We use $\Omega_X^0=\mathcal{O}_X$.

<a id="stmt-cor-pluriforms"></a>
<div id="cor:pluriforms" class="corollary">

**Corollary 50** (Pluriforms and the rational quotient).

1.  *For every smooth connected projective complex variety $X$, $$\dim R(X)=
     \max_{\substack{p\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq0},\,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\\
     H^0(X,\operatorname{Sym}^k(\Omega_X^p))\ne0}} p.$$*

2.  *A compact connected Kähler manifold $X$ is rationally connected if and only if $$H^0\bigl(X,\operatorname{Sym}^k(\Omega_X^p)\bigr)=0
     \quad\text{for every pair of integers }p,k>0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-pluriforms"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-pluriforms</summary>

*Proof.* Write $r=\dim R(X)$ in <span class="upright">(i)</span>. Resolve the MRC map to a morphism $f:X'\to R(X)$, with $X'$ smooth and projective. For a smooth general fiber $F$, the cotangent sequence is $$0\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_F^{\oplus r}
 \longrightarrow\Omega_{X'}^1|_F
 \longrightarrow\Omega_F^1\longrightarrow0.$$ If $p>r$, every graded piece of the induced exterior-power filtration on $\Omega_{X'}^p|_F$ contains a positive exterior power of $\Omega_F^1$: at most $r$ factors can come from $\mathcal{O}_F^{\oplus r}$. Consequently, for $k>0$, the graded pieces of the induced filtration on $\operatorname{Sym}^k(\Omega_{X'}^p)|_F$ are direct summands of sums of positive tensor powers of $\Omega_F^1$. Here we use antisymmetrization and symmetrization over $\mathbb{C}$. Rational connectedness of $F$ makes all their sections vanish, by Corollary <a href="#cor:uniruled-mumford" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:uniruled-mumford">49</a>. A global section therefore vanishes on every sufficiently general fiber, hence on $X'$. Birational invariance of global covariant tensor differentials on smooth projective varieties gives the same vanishing on $X$. This is the upper-bound argument of (Brunebarbe and Campana 2015, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-99"></a>[[BrunebarbeCampana2015](#ref-BrunebarbeCampana2015)].

If $r>0$, the smooth non-uniruled base has $\kappa(R(X))\geq0$ by Corollary <a href="#cor:uniruled-mumford" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:uniruled-mumford">49</a>. A nonzero section of $kK_{R(X)}$ for some $k>0$ pulls back to a nonzero section of $\operatorname{Sym}^k(\Omega_X^r)$. The pullback is taken on $X'$ and descends to $X$ by the same birational invariance. If $r=0$, the term $p=0$ gives a nonzero section and the same equality follows. This is the equality $r=r^-$ predicted in the remark ending (Brunebarbe and Campana 2015, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-100"></a>[[BrunebarbeCampana2015](#ref-BrunebarbeCampana2015)].

For <span class="upright">(ii)</span>, the forward vanishing is the rational-curve argument recalled by Brunebarbe and Campana in the same section. Conversely, the stated vanishing gives $H^{2,0}(X)=H^0(X,\Omega_X^2)=0$. Kodaira’s projectivity criterion then makes $X$ projective: the real degree-two cohomology is of type $(1,1)$, so a rational class sufficiently close to a Kähler class is still Kähler, and clearing denominators gives an integral Kähler class. This is the projectivity reduction in (Brunebarbe and Campana 2015, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-101"></a>[[BrunebarbeCampana2015](#ref-BrunebarbeCampana2015)]. Part <span class="upright">(i)</span> now applies. Its maximum is zero because every term with $p>0$ vanishes, so the MRC base is a point and $X$ is rationally connected. ◻

</details>

We next use ordinary cotangent invariants, with no boundary. If $\mathcal L$ is a coherent rank-one subsheaf of a vector bundle on a smooth projective variety, its double dual is a line bundle; put $\kappa(\mathcal L)=\kappa(\mathcal L^{**})$. Define $$\omega(X)=
 \max_{\substack{m>0\\
 \mathcal L\subset(\Omega_X^1)^{\otimes m}\\
 \operatorname{rank}\mathcal L=1}}
 \kappa(\mathcal L),$$ where $m$ is an integer and the value is $-\infty$ if no such sheaf has nonnegative Iitaka dimension. The inclusion of $\mathcal L$ extends to its double dual: away from codimension two these sheaves agree, and a map into a locally free sheaf extends across that subset. Taking tensor powers then realizes the systems $H^0(X,(\mathcal L^{**})^{\otimes a})$, for integers $a>0$, inside higher cotangent tensor powers. Thus allowing all $m$ makes this equivalent to Campana’s definition using the dimensions of the rational maps defined by $H^0(X,\mathcal L)$. For positive-dimensional $X$, also put $$\kappa^+(X)=
 \max_{\substack{p>0\\0\ne\mathcal F\subset\Omega_X^p}}
 \kappa(\det\mathcal F).$$ Here $p$ is an integer, $\mathcal F$ is coherent, and $\det\mathcal F=(\bigwedge^{\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}\mathcal F}\mathcal F)^{**}$. These are the invariants used in Campana’s Appendix A to Taji (Taji 2014) <a id="pm-cite-102"></a>[[Taji2014](#ref-Taji2014)].

<a id="stmt-cor-cotangent-mrc"></a>
<div id="cor:cotangent-mrc" class="corollary">

**Corollary 51** (Cotangent invariants and the MRC base). *Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety. Then $$\omega(X)=\omega(R(X))=
 \begin{cases}
 -\infty,&\text{if }R(X)\text{ is a point},\\
 \kappa(R(X)),&\text{if }\dim R(X)>0.
 \end{cases}$$ In particular, if $X$ is positive-dimensional and non-uniruled, then $\omega(X)=\kappa(X)$. If $X$ is positive-dimensional and $\kappa(X)\geq0$, then $$\kappa^+(X)=\kappa(X).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-cotangent-mrc"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-cotangent-mrc</summary>

*Proof.* Campana’s birationally invariant fibration properties (Taji 2014, Appendix A, property 4) <a id="pm-cite-103"></a>[[Taji2014](#ref-Taji2014)] give $\omega(X)=\omega(R(X))$: the general MRC fiber is rationally connected and has $\omega=-\infty$. Indeed any rank-one subsheaf with nonnegative Iitaka dimension would give a section of a positive tensor power, contrary to Corollary <a href="#cor:uniruled-mumford" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:uniruled-mumford">49</a>; a point has $\omega=-\infty$ by the empty maximum. Birational invariance permits resolving the MRC map. If $R(X)$ is a point, the displayed formula therefore follows without changing our convention $\kappa(\mathrm{pt})=0$.

Suppose that $R=R(X)$ has positive dimension. It is non-uniruled, so $\kappa(R)\geq0$ by Corollary <a href="#cor:uniruled-mumford" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:uniruled-mumford">49</a>. To apply Campana’s abundance deduction (Taji 2014, Conjecture A.2 and Remark A.3) <a id="pm-cite-104"></a>[[Taji2014](#ref-Taji2014)], take a resolved Iitaka fibration $R'\to Z$, with $R'$ and $Z$ smooth and projective, $R'$ birational to $R$, and $\dim Z=\kappa(R)$. Its smooth general fiber $F$ has $\kappa(F)=0$. If $F$ is positive-dimensional, Corollary <a href="#cor:smooth-good-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:smooth-good-model">43</a> and Lemma <a href="#lem:semiample-zero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:semiample-zero">44</a> give a normal birational good model $V_F$ with $K_{V_F}\sim_{\mathbb{Q}}0$. In particular its canonical divisor is numerically trivial on its smooth locus. Campana’s criterion (Taji 2014, Appendix A, Examples A.1(2)) <a id="pm-cite-105"></a>[[Taji2014](#ref-Taji2014)] says that the existence of such a normal birational model implies $\omega(F)=0$; it does not require $K_F$ itself to be trivial. Property 4 of the same appendix and birational invariance now give $\omega(R)=\omega(R')\leq\dim Z=\kappa(R)$. If $F$ is a point, this upper bound is simply $\omega(R)\leq\dim R=\kappa(R)$. The reverse inequality $\omega(R)\geq\kappa(R)$ is one of the defining cotangent inequalities recorded in the same appendix. This proves the piecewise formula. A non-uniruled $X$ has $R(X)$ birational to $X$, giving the first stated specialization.

Campana also records $\omega(X)\geq\kappa^+(X)\geq\kappa(X)$ (Taji 2014, Appendix A, property 2) <a id="pm-cite-106"></a>[[Taji2014](#ref-Taji2014)]. When $\kappa(X)\geq0$, Corollary <a href="#cor:uniruled-mumford" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:uniruled-mumford">49</a> makes $X$ non-uniruled, and the two outer terms are equal by the formula just proved. Thus $\kappa^+(X)=\kappa(X)$. This is the abundance consequence recalled by Taji (Taji 2014, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-107"></a>[[Taji2014](#ref-Taji2014)], who attributes it to Campana (Campana 1995, Proposition 3.10) <a id="pm-cite-108"></a>[[Campana1995](#ref-Campana1995)]. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-finite-pi-one"></a>
<div id="cor:finite-pi-one" class="corollary">

**Corollary 52** (Finite fundamental group in Kodaira dimension zero). *Let $X$ be a positive-dimensional smooth connected projective complex variety. If $\kappa(X)=0$ and $\chi(X,\mathcal{O}_X)\ne0$, then its ordinary topological fundamental group $\pi_1(X)$ is finite.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-finite-pi-one"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-finite-pi-one</summary>

*Proof.* Corollary <a href="#cor:cotangent-mrc" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:cotangent-mrc">51</a> gives $\kappa^+(X)=0$. Campana’s criterion (Campana 1995, Corollary 5.3) <a id="pm-cite-109"></a>[[Campana1995](#ref-Campana1995)], in the form recalled by Taji (Taji 2014, Theorem 1.6) <a id="pm-cite-110"></a>[[Taji2014](#ref-Taji2014)], gives the conclusion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-65"></a>
#### Quasi-projective covers

Here a quasi-projective cover means a complex analytic covering space biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety; its deck transformations need not be algebraic. The following application describes such covers in terms of bundles over abelian varieties.

<a id="stmt-cor-quasi-projective-covers"></a>
<div id="cor:quasi-projective-covers" class="corollary">

**Corollary 53** (Quasi-projective covering spaces). *Let $X$ be a connected normal projective complex variety.*

1.  *Its universal cover is biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety if and only if there is a finite étale Galois cover $X'\to X$ and a locally trivial holomorphic fiber bundle $X'\to A$, where $A$ is an abelian variety and the fiber is simply connected and projective.*

2.  *If $\widetilde X\to X$ is an infinite étale Galois cover and $\widetilde X$ is biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety, there are a finite étale Galois cover $X'\to X$, a locally trivial holomorphic fiber bundle $\alpha:X'\to A$ over an abelian variety, and an étale cover $\widetilde A\to A$ with no positive-dimensional compact analytic subvarieties, such that $$\widetilde X\simeq X'\times_A\widetilde A$$ as covering spaces over $X$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-quasi-projective-covers"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-quasi-projective-covers</summary>

*Proof.* Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> with zero boundary on a smooth projective complex variety gives precisely Conjecture 1.2 of Claudon–Höring–Kollár (Claudon, Höring, and Kollár 2013) <a id="pm-cite-111"></a>[[ClaudonHoeringKollar2013](#ref-ClaudonHoeringKollar2013)]. Their Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.5 therefore give <span class="upright">(i)</span> and <span class="upright">(ii)</span>, respectively. ◻

</details>

The simply connected fiber condition belongs to <span class="upright">(i)</span>, not to the general-cover statement <span class="upright">(ii)</span>. Local triviality is holomorphic; no global product after a finite cover or compact Kähler extension is asserted.

<a id="sec:analytic-abundance"></a>
### Relative abundance for projective analytic morphisms

The complex rational case of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> supplies the algebraic fiber input in Fujino’s relative analytic reduction (Fujino 2025, Theorem 1.10 and its proof) <a id="pm-cite-112"></a>[[FujinoRelativeAnalytic2025](#ref-FujinoRelativeAnalytic2025)]. We record only its normal rational case. Throughout this section, complex analytic spaces are Hausdorff and second-countable, and a complex variety means a reduced and irreducible complex analytic space, as in (Fujino 2025, 5) <a id="pm-cite-113"></a>[[FujinoRelativeAnalytic2025](#ref-FujinoRelativeAnalytic2025)].

Here a projective analytic morphism is proper and possesses a relatively ample line bundle. Rational Cartierness is local on the source; a single global Cartier index is not part of that definition. A divisor is $\pi$-nef over $Y$ if it has nonnegative intersection with every projective integral curve contracted by $\pi$, over every point of $Y$. These are the conventions of (Fujino 2022, Definitions 2.28, 2.32 and 2.48) <a id="pm-cite-114"></a>[[FujinoAnalyticMMP2022](#ref-FujinoAnalyticMMP2022)].

<a id="stmt-cor-analytic-abundance"></a>
<div id="cor:analytic-abundance" class="corollary">

**Corollary 54** (Relative analytic abundance). *Let $\pi:X\to Y$ be a projective surjective morphism of normal complex analytic varieties. Let $(X,\Delta)$ be a log canonical pair with $\Delta$ an effective rational divisor and $D=K_X+\Delta$ $\mathbb{Q}$-Cartier. Assume that $D$ is $\pi$-nef over all of $Y$. For every compact subset $W\subset Y$, there exist an analytic open neighborhood $U\supset W$ and a positive integer $m$ such that $mD|_{X_U}$ is Cartier, where $X_U=\pi^{-1}(U)$, and the line bundle $$L=\mathcal{O}_{X_U}(mD|_{X_U})$$ is generated relative to $\pi_U:X_U\to U$. Equivalently, the evaluation map $$\pi_U^*(\pi_U)_*L\longrightarrow L$$ is surjective.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-analytic-abundance"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-analytic-abundance</summary>

*Proof.* Since $\pi$ is proper, $\pi^{-1}(W)$ is compact. Local rational Cartier indices therefore have a common multiple $m_0$ on an open neighborhood $O$ of $\pi^{-1}(W)$. The image of the closed set $X\setminus O$ is closed and disjoint from $W$, so there is an open neighborhood $V\supset W$ with $\pi^{-1}(V)\subset O$. Thus $m_0D$ is Cartier over $V$.

Fix $P\in W$. Following (Fujino 2025, the proof of Theorem 1.10, p. 30) <a id="pm-cite-115"></a>[[FujinoRelativeAnalytic2025](#ref-FujinoRelativeAnalytic2025)], work over a sufficiently small neighborhood of $P$ contained in $V$ and take a crepant dlt blow-up $p:(X',\Delta')\to(X,\Delta)$. Put $\pi'=\pi\circ p$ and $D'=K_{X'}+\Delta'=p^*D$. For every log canonical stratum $S$ of $(X',\Delta')$, including $X'$ itself, adjunction gives an effective rational boundary $\Delta_S$ with $$K_S+\Delta_S=D'|_S.$$ The strata are normal, and this adjoint is nef over $\pi'(S)$. On each normal projective component $F$ of an analytically sufficiently general fiber of $S\to\pi'(S)$, restriction gives a log canonical pair $(F,\Delta_F)$ with effective rational boundary and nef adjoint $K_F+\Delta_F=(K_S+\Delta_S)|_F$. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> makes this adjoint semiample, so its Iitaka and numerical dimensions agree. Thus $D'|_S$ is abundant over $\pi'(S)$ for every stratum: this is precisely $\pi'$-log abundance in (Fujino 2025, Definitions 2.10 and 2.13) <a id="pm-cite-116"></a>[[FujinoRelativeAnalytic2025](#ref-FujinoRelativeAnalytic2025)].

The nef-and-log-abundant theorem (Fujino 2025, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-117"></a>[[FujinoRelativeAnalytic2025](#ref-FujinoRelativeAnalytic2025)] now gives a relatively generated multiple of $D'$ over a neighborhood $U_P\subset V$ of $P$. Enlarge its integer to a multiple $m_P$ of $m_0$; tensor powers preserve relative generation. Normality gives $p_*\mathcal{O}_{X'}=\mathcal{O}_X$, and projection formula identifies the direct images over $U_P$ of $\mathcal{O}_X(m_PD)$ and $\mathcal{O}_{X'}(m_PD')$. Their evaluation maps are related by pullback. Generation upstairs therefore implies generation downstairs: a base point downstairs would make every pulled-back section vanish on its nonempty fiber.

Choose finitely many of these neighborhoods $U_{P_1},\ldots,U_{P_s}$ covering $W$, and let $m$ be a common multiple of $m_0,m_{P_1},\ldots,m_{P_s}$. Put $U=\bigcup_i U_{P_i}$. The divisor $mD$ is Cartier over $U$, and its line bundle is relatively generated on each $U_{P_i}$. Surjectivity of evaluation is local on the base, so it holds over $U$, as asserted. ◻

</details>

The base $Y$ need not be algebraic, compact, or Stein, and $W$ need not be a Stein compact subset. The integer $m$ may depend on the pair, the morphism, and $W$; no single Cartier index or relatively generated multiple over all of a noncompact base is asserted. The nefness premise is over all of $Y$, not only over $W$. Projectivity cannot be replaced here by mere properness or by a Kähler hypothesis: when $Y$ is a point it requires $X$ to be projective. The statement does not include real boundaries or semi-log-canonical analytic pairs.

      "explanation": "The source invokes the companion's logarithmic subadditivity theorem; its zero-boundary specialization is used in the Albanese reduction.",

      "source_pm_id": "PM-034.4",
      "source_quote": "Let $f:X\\to Y$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers between smooth connected projective complex varieties.",
      "target_anchor": "stmt-spec-logarithmic",

      "target_quote": "Let $f:X\\to Y$ be a surjective morphism with connected fibers between smooth connected projective complex varieties.",

    },
